## Skill 1: SQL

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [SQL_BEGINNER_001]
Which SQL statement is used to extract data from a database?

A. OPEN
B. EXTRACT
C. GET
D. SELECT

Correct Answer: D

Q2 [SQL_BEGINNER_002]
Which keyword is used to sort the result-set in SQL?

A. SORT BY
B. ORDER BY
C. ALIGN BY
D. GROUP BY

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SQL_BEGINNER_003]
Which SQL clause is used to filter records based on a specific condition?

A. WHERE
B. IF
C. HAVING
D. FILTER

Correct Answer: A

Q4 [SQL_BEGINNER_004]
Which aggregate function returns the total number of rows in a table?

A. SUM()
B. TOTAL()
C. COUNT()
D. MAX()

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [SQL_BEGINNER_005]
What does SQL stand for?

A. Structured Query Language
B. Simple Query Language
C. Statistical Query Language
D. Standard Query List

Correct Answer: A

Q6 [SQL_BEGINNER_006]
Which SQL statement is used to update data in a database?

A. SAVE
B. MODIFY
C. UPDATE
D. CHANGE

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [SQL_BEGINNER_007]
How do you select all columns from a table named "Customers"?

A. SELECT Customers
B. SELECT * FROM Customers
C. SELECT [all] FROM Customers
D. GET * FROM Customers

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SQL_BEGINNER_008]
Which command is used to remove all records from a table, but not the table structure itself?

A. DROP
B. DELETE FROM
C. REMOVE
D. TRUNCATE

Correct Answer: D

Q9 [SQL_BEGINNER_009]
In SQL, how do you select all the records from a table named "Persons" where the value of the column "FirstName" is "Peter"?

A. SELECT * FROM Persons WHERE FirstName='Peter'
B. SELECT [all] FROM Persons WHERE FirstName LIKE 'Peter'
C. SELECT * FROM Persons WHERE FirstName<>'Peter'
D. SELECT Persons WHERE FirstName='Peter'

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [SQL_BEGINNER_010]
Which operator is used to search for a specified pattern in a column?

A. SEARCH
B. MATCH
C. LIKE
D. FIND

Correct Answer: C

Q11 [SQL_BEGINNER_011]
Which SQL keyword is used to return only different values?

A. UNIQUE
B. DISTINCT
C. DIFFERENT
D. ONLY

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SQL_BEGINNER_012]
Which SQL statement is used to insert new data in a database?

A. ADD NEW
B. INSERT INTO
C. ADD RECORD
D. INSERT NEW

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SQL_BEGINNER_013]
The default sorting order for the ORDER BY clause is:

A. Descending
B. Ascending
C. Random
D. Case-sensitive

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SQL_BEGINNER_014]
How can you return the number of records in the "Persons" table?

A. SELECT COUNT(*) FROM Persons
B. SELECT COLUMNS(*) FROM Persons
C. SELECT COUNT() FROM Persons
D. SELECT ROWCOUNT FROM Persons

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [SQL_BEGINNER_015]
What is the correct SQL syntax to delete a table?

A. DELETE TABLE table_name
B. DROP TABLE table_name
C. REMOVE TABLE table_name
D. TRUNCATE TABLE table_name

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SQL_BEGINNER_016]
Which operator is used to select values within a range?

A. WITHIN
B. BETWEEN
C. RANGE
D. IN

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SQL_BEGINNER_017]
Which of the following is NOT a valid SQL aggregate function?

A. MIN()
B. AVG()
C. COMPUTE()
D. SUM()

Correct Answer: C

Q18 [SQL_BEGINNER_018]
True or False: The NULL value is the same as a zero or a blank space.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q19 [SQL_BEGINNER_019]
Which SQL constraint uniquely identifies each record in a table?

A. UNIQUE
B. PRIMARY KEY
C. FOREIGN KEY
D. NOT NULL

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SQL_BEGINNER_020]
What is the purpose of the AS clause in SQL?

A. To define a new table
B. To rename a column or table with an alias
C. To filter the data
D. To join two tables

Correct Answer: B

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_001]
Which join returns all records from the left table, and the matched records from the right table?

A. INNER JOIN
B. FULL JOIN
C. LEFT JOIN
D. RIGHT JOIN

Correct Answer: C

Q2 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is the difference between the WHERE and HAVING clauses?

A. WHERE is used for rows; HAVING is used for groups
B. WHERE is used for groups; HAVING is used for rows
C. There is no difference
D. WHERE is used only in SELECT; HAVING only in UPDATE

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_003]
How do you return the mean value of a numeric column named "Price"?

A. SELECT MEAN(Price) FROM Products
B. SELECT AVG(Price) FROM Products
C. SELECT SUM(Price)/COUNT(Price) FROM Products
D. SELECT AVERAGE(Price) FROM Products

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_004]
Which of the following joins is used to return only the rows that have matching values in both tables?

A. LEFT JOIN
B. CROSS JOIN
C. INNER JOIN
D. OUTER JOIN

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Which command is used to combine the result-set of two or more SELECT statements while removing duplicates?

A. JOIN
B. UNION
C. UNION ALL
D. MERGE

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_006]
A Primary Key column must contain:

A. Unique values and no NULL values
B. Unique values and can contain NULL values
C. Duplicate values but no NULL values
D. Any values

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is a Foreign Key?

A. A key used to encrypt the database
B. A field in one table that refers to the Primary Key in another table
C. A key that allows duplicate values in a Primary Key column
D. A key used only in external databases

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_008]
Which SQL function is used to convert all characters in a string to uppercase?

A. UPPER()
B. CAPS()
C. UCASE()
D. Both A and C depending on the SQL dialect

Correct Answer: D

Q9 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_009]
In a query using GROUP BY, where should the ORDER BY clause be placed?

A. Before GROUP BY
B. After GROUP BY
C. Before the WHERE clause
D. It cannot be used with GROUP BY

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is the result of the following query?
`SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT City) FROM Customers;`

A. The total number of customers
B. The number of unique cities where customers are located
C. A list of all cities
D. An error

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which operator is used to specify multiple possible values for a column in a WHERE clause?

A. BETWEEN
B. ANY
C. IN
D. LIKE

Correct Answer: C

Q12 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What does the "TRUNCATE" command do?

A. Deletes the entire table structure
B. Deletes all rows from a table without logging individual row deletions
C. Deletes specific rows based on a condition
D. Renames the table

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_013]
Which clause is used with aggregate functions to filter groups?

A. WHERE
B. HAVING
C. GROUP BY
D. ORDER BY

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_014]
How do you select all records from "TableA" that do not have a corresponding match in "TableB"?

A. Use an INNER JOIN
B. Use a LEFT JOIN and filter WHERE TableB.ID IS NULL
C. Use a CROSS JOIN
D. Use a UNION

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_015]
Which function is used to remove leading and trailing spaces from a string?

A. CLEAN()
B. REMOVE()
C. TRIM()
D. STRIP()

Correct Answer: C

Q16 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is the purpose of the "COALESCE" function?

A. To combine two strings
B. To return the first non-null expression in a list
C. To count the number of null values
D. To sort data alphabetically

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_017]
Which SQL keyword is used to change the structure of an existing table (e.g., adding a column)?

A. UPDATE TABLE
B. MODIFY TABLE
C. CHANGE TABLE
D. ALTER TABLE

Correct Answer: D

Q18 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is a subquery?

A. A query that returns more than one table
B. A query nested inside another SQL statement
C. A query that uses only one column
D. A query that runs after the main query is finished

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_019]
To get the current date in SQL, which function might you use (depending on DB system)?

A. GETDATE()
B. CURRENT_DATE
C. NOW()
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q20 [SQL_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What happens if you run an UPDATE statement without a WHERE clause?

A. Only the first row is updated
B. The query fails with an error
C. All rows in the table are updated
D. SQL asks for confirmation before proceeding

Correct Answer: C

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [SQL_ADVANCED_001]
What is the primary difference between a Common Table Expression (CTE) and a Subquery?

A. CTEs are faster than subqueries
B. CTEs can be recursive and are often more readable
C. Subqueries can only be used in the WHERE clause
D. There is no technical difference

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SQL_ADVANCED_002]
Which window function is used to provide a unique rank to each row within a partition, but assigns the same rank to rows with equal values and skips subsequent ranks?

A. ROW_NUMBER()
B. RANK()
C. DENSE_RANK()
D. NTILE()

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SQL_ADVANCED_003]
What is the purpose of an Index in a database?

A. To ensure data integrity
B. To speed up data retrieval operations
C. To reduce storage space
D. To prevent duplicate entries

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SQL_ADVANCED_004]
In the context of ACID properties, what does "Atomicity" ensure?

A. Data remains consistent across the database
B. Multiple transactions can run simultaneously
C. A transaction is treated as a single unit; either all operations succeed or none do
D. Committed data is saved even in case of a system crash

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [SQL_ADVANCED_005]
Which normal form deals with removing "Partial Functional Dependencies"?

A. 1NF
B. 2NF
C. 3NF
D. BCNF

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SQL_ADVANCED_006]
What is a "Clustered Index"?

A. An index that determines the physical order of data in a table
B. A collection of multiple non-clustered indexes
C. An index that allows duplicate keys
D. An index that is stored in a separate file from the data

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [SQL_ADVANCED_007]
What is the output of a CROSS JOIN between a table with 5 rows and a table with 10 rows?

A. 15 rows
B. 5 rows
C. 50 rows
D. 0 rows

Correct Answer: C

Q8 [SQL_ADVANCED_008]
What is a "Deadlock" in SQL?

A. When a database is shut down unexpectedly
B. When two transactions are waiting for each other to release locks
C. When a query takes too long to execute
D. When a Primary Key is deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SQL_ADVANCED_009]
What is the difference between `RANK()` and `DENSE_RANK()`?

A. `RANK()` skips ranks after ties; `DENSE_RANK()` does not
B. `DENSE_RANK()` skips ranks after ties; `RANK()` does not
C. `RANK()` can only be used with numbers
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [SQL_ADVANCED_010]
Which SQL clause allows you to perform calculations across a set of table rows that are somehow related to the current row?

A. GROUP BY
B. OVER
C. HAVING
D. WHERE

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SQL_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Database Normalization"?

A. The process of increasing redundancy to speed up reads
B. The process of organizing data to minimize redundancy
C. The process of backup and recovery
D. The process of converting SQL to NoSQL

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SQL_ADVANCED_012]
What does the "EXPLAIN" keyword do in SQL?

A. Provides a text description of what a table contains
B. Shows the execution plan used by the database engine for a query
C. Explains why a query returned an error
D. Comments out a block of code

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SQL_ADVANCED_013]
What is a "Stored Procedure"?

A. A backup of the database
B. A prepared SQL code that you can save so the code can be reused over and over again
C. A table that stores metadata
D. A specific type of constraint

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SQL_ADVANCED_014]
Which of the following is a characteristic of a "View"?

A. It physically stores data on the disk
B. It is a virtual table based on the result-set of an SQL statement
C. It cannot be used with JOINS
D. It automatically indexes all columns

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SQL_ADVANCED_015]
In a database transaction, what does "Isolation" refer to?

A. Keeping the database on a separate server
B. Ensuring that concurrent transactions do not interfere with each other
C. Preventing unauthorized users from accessing the data
D. Restricting a table to a single user at all times

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SQL_ADVANCED_016]
What is the "Nth highest salary" query typically solved with?

A. CROSS JOIN
B. OFFSET and LIMIT (or window functions like DENSE_RANK)
C. Simple WHERE clause
D. UNION ALL

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SQL_ADVANCED_017]
Which type of lock prevents other transactions from modifying or reading a row?

A. Shared Lock
B. Exclusive Lock
C. Intent Lock
D. Update Lock

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [SQL_ADVANCED_018]
What is a "Self Join"?

A. A join between two different databases
B. A table joined with itself
C. A join that requires no ON condition
D. A join that merges all columns into one

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SQL_ADVANCED_019]
What is the purpose of the `LEAD()` function?

A. To find the first value in a column
B. To access data from a subsequent row in the same result set without a self-join
C. To sort data in descending order
D. To identify the primary key of a table

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SQL_ADVANCED_020]
When is it better to use a `NoSQL` database over a `SQL` database?

A. When data is highly structured and requires complex joins
B. When horizontal scalability and handling unstructured data are priorities
C. When ACID compliance is the absolute priority for every transaction
D. When the dataset is very small

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [EXCEL_INTERMEDIATE_015]

B. Winsorizing (replacing them with the nearest non-outlier value)
C. Keeping them (if they represent valid but rare events)
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q20 [DATA_CLEANING_INTERMEDIATE_020]

A. Making the data analysts wealthy
B. Adding new, relevant information from external sources to improve the existing dataset
C. Deleting unnecessary columns
D. Cleaning the data twice

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [EDA_BEGINNER_017]

A. Box Plot
B. Scatter Plot
C. Pie Chart
D. Bar Chart

Correct Answer: A

Q18 [EDA_BEGINNER_018]

A. Analyzing data from two different computers
B. Analyzing the relationship between two variables
C. Analyzing the same variable twice
D. Cleaning two tables at once

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [EDA_BEGINNER_019]

A. Numerical
B. Categorical
C. Continuous
D. Boolean

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [EDA_BEGINNER_020]
What is "Skewness" broadly related to in EDA?

A. The color of the chart
B. The asymmetry of the data distribution
C. The number of rows in the table

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_001]

C. Only the outliers
D. The Mean of the data

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_002]

A. Data points form a horizontal line
B. Data points slope upwards from left to right
C. Data points slope downwards from left to right
D. Data points are scattered randomly with no pattern

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_003]

A. Median
B. Standard Deviation
C. Mode
D. Frequency

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_004]

D. To delete unwanted data

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_005]

A. The left (lower values)
B. The right (higher values)
C. Both sides equally
D. The center

Q6 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is the "Interquartile Range" (IQR)?

A. Q3 - Q1
B. Q4 - Q1
C. Max - Min
D. Mean - Median

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_007]

A. Median
B. Mean
C. Mode
D. Range (relative to the bulk of data)

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_008]

A. A weak positive relationship
B. A strong negative relationship
C. No relationship
D. An error in data entry

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is the purpose of a "Correlation Matrix"?

A. To solve mathematical equations
B. To see how all pairs of numerical variables in a dataset relate to each other
C. To identify missing values
D. To sort the data alphabetically

Q10 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_010]

D. Stacked Area Chart

Q11 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is "Multivariate Analysis"?

A. Analyzing more than two variables simultaneously to find complex patterns
B. Analyzing many datasets from different companies
C. Analyzing the same variable over many years
D. Creating a very large bar chart

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What does "Kurtosis" measure in a distribution?

A. The average value
B. The "tailedness" or peakness of the distribution
C. The distance between the mean and median
D. The number of unique categories

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_013]

A. Values greater than Q3 + 1.5*IQR or less than Q1 - 1.5*IQR

C. Values that are equal to the Median
D. Values that are zero

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is the "Standard Deviation"?

A. The difference between Max and Min
B. A measure of how much values typically deviate from the Mean
C. The most common value in a set
D. The sum of all squared values

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_015]

A. Pie Chart and Line Chart

C. Box Plot and Scatter Plot
D. Two different histograms

Q16 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_016]

Q18 [EDA_INTERMEDIATE_018]

Q1 [EDA_ADVANCED_001]

A. The power of machine learning
B. That datasets with identical summary statistics can have vastly different distributions and patterns
C. How to perform a four-way join in SQL
D. The speed of four different database engines

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [EDA_ADVANCED_002]

A. To delete columns to save space
B. To visualize high-dimensional data in 2D or 3D while preserving as much variance as possible
C. To increase the number of features
D. To make the charts smaller on the screen

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [EDA_ADVANCED_003]
What does "Multicollinearity" refer to during EDA?

A. When a column has multiple colors in a chart
B. When two or more independent variables are highly correlated with each other
C. When a dataset has too many rows
D. When the target variable is missing

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [EDA_ADVANCED_004]
How can EDA help in "Feature Engineering"?

A. By identifying which variables have the most predictive power or need transformation
B. By automatically writing the code for the model

D. By renaming the features to make them shorter

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [EDA_ADVANCED_005]

Q6 [EDA_ADVANCED_006]

D. A rule about the size of a dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [EDA_ADVANCED_007]

A. The Standard Deviation divided by the Mean

D. The range of the first quartile

Q8 [EDA_ADVANCED_008]

A. A plot used to analyze musical instruments

D. A chart with no axes

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [EDA_ADVANCED_009]

A. By finding a variable that has an unnaturally high correlation with the target variable
B. By checking the server logs
C. By seeing if the file is leaking memory
D. By counting the number of nulls

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [EDA_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Rescaling" in EDA (e.g., Log Transformation)?

A. Changing the font size of the axes
B. Applying a mathematical function to data to handle skewness and make patterns more visible
C. Deleting the axis labels
D. Moving the chart to a different page

Q11 [EDA_ADVANCED_011]

C. Scatter Plot matrix
D. Treemap

Q12 [EDA_ADVANCED_012]

D. When there are too many columns

Q13 [EDA_ADVANCED_013]

A. Colors
B. The size of the points

D. Sound effects

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [EDA_ADVANCED_014]

D. To sort data geographically

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [EDA_ADVANCED_015]

Q16 [EDA_ADVANCED_016]

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [EDA_ADVANCED_017]

D. A type of geographic map

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [EDA_ADVANCED_018]
What does "Homoscedasticity" mean in the context of analyzing residuals?

A. The variance of errors is constant across all levels of an independent variable
B. The mean is always zero
C. The data has no outliers
D. The variables are perfectly correlated

Q19 [EDA_ADVANCED_019]

## Skill 10: Statistical Analysis

### Beginner — 20 Questions

B. 5

D. 7

C. Predictive Statistics

Q3 [STATS_BEGINNER_003]

B. Continuous
C. Nominal
D. Ordinal

Correct Answer: B

A. 0
B. 0.5
C. 1
D. 100

D. Range

D. Distance

D. Ratio

Q1 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_001]

A. 50%
B. 68%
C. 95%
D. 99.7%

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What does the "Standard Deviation" measure?

A. The average value
B. The middle value
C. The amount of variation or dispersion from the mean
D. The total number of items

Correct Answer: C

Q3 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_003]
The "Null Hypothesis" (H0) typically states that:

A. There is a significant effect or difference
B. There is no effect or no difference
C. The sample size is too small
D. The data is incorrect

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_004]
A correlation coefficient (r) of -0.85 indicates:

A. A weak positive correlation
B. A strong negative correlation
C. No correlation
D. A perfect positive correlation

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is the "Variance" in relation to the "Standard Deviation"?

A. Variance is the square root of Standard Deviation
B. Variance is the square of the Standard Deviation
C. They are the same thing
D. Variance is double the Standard Deviation

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_006]
In a "Normal Distribution", which of the following is true?

A. Mean > Median > Mode
B. Mean < Median < Mode
C. Mean = Median = Mode
D. There is no Mode

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is the "Z-score"?

A. The total sum of the data
B. The number of standard deviations a data point is from the mean
C. The probability of an event
D. The range of the data

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_008]

A. Kurtosis
B. Skewness
C. Variance
D. Range

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What does the "Central Limit Theorem" state about the distribution of sample means?

A. It will always be skewed
B. It will approach a Normal Distribution as the sample size becomes large
C. It will be the same as the population distribution
D. It will always have a mean of zero

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_010]

A. Chi-Square
B. ANOVA
C. T-test
D. Regression

Correct Answer: C

Q11 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is the "Level of Significance" (Alpha), typically set at 0.05?

A. The probability of accepting the null hypothesis
B. The probability of rejecting the null hypothesis when it is actually true
C. The number of rows in the sample
D. The average error in the data

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_012]

A. 0.9
B. -0.5
C. 0.1
D. -0.8

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_013]

C. Mean, SD, Variance, Min, Max
D. Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, Q5

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_014]

A. Mean < Median
B. Mean > Median
C. Mean = Median
D. Mode > Mean

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is a "Type I Error"?

A. Failing to reject a false null hypothesis
B. Rejecting a true null hypothesis
C. An error in data entry
D. A calculation error in the mean

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_016]

A. The error in a single measurement

Q17 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_017]

Q18 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is "Kurtosis"?

A. The measure of how spread out the data is
B. The measure of the "tailedness" or peakness of a distribution
C. The relationship between two variables
D. The average of the median and mode

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_019]
If events A and B are "Independent", the probability of both occurring is:

A. P(A) + P(B)

D. P(A) / P(B)

Q20 [STATS_INTERMEDIATE_020]

A. The difference between the Max and Min

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [STATS_ADVANCED_001]
What does a "P-value" of 0.03 mean if your alpha level is 0.05?

A. The null hypothesis is true
B. The result is statistically significant; reject the null hypothesis
C. The result is not significant; fail to reject the null hypothesis
D. The data is corrupted

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [STATS_ADVANCED_002]
Which test is used to determine if there is a significant association between two "Categorical" variables?

B. Linear Regression

D. Pearson Correlation

Correct Answer: C

Q3 [STATS_ADVANCED_003]
In Linear Regression, what does "R-squared" represent?

A. The slope of the line
B. The proportion of variance in the dependent variable explained by the independent variable(s)
C. The probability of an error
D. The average value of Y

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [STATS_ADVANCED_004]
What is a "Type II Error"?

A. Rejecting a true null hypothesis
B. Failing to reject a false null hypothesis
C. A software bug
D. A sampling bias

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [STATS_ADVANCED_005]

A. When comparing the means of more than two groups

Q6 [STATS_ADVANCED_006]
What is "Multicollinearity" in a multiple regression model?

A. When the target variable is missing
B. When independent variables are highly correlated with each other
C. When the residuals are not normal
D. When the model has too many rows

Q7 [STATS_ADVANCED_007]

A. 95% of the data points are within this interval
B. We are 95% confident that the true population parameter falls within this interval
C. There is a 5% chance the data is wrong
D. The mean is equal to 95

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [STATS_ADVANCED_008]
What is the "Power" of a statistical test?

A. The ability to calculate results quickly
B. The probability of correctly rejecting a false null hypothesis
C. The number of variables in the test
D. The maximum value of the Z-score

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [STATS_ADVANCED_009]
"Homoscedasticity" refers to:

A. The data being normally distributed
B. Constant variance of errors across all levels of the independent variable
C. All variables having the same mean
D. The presence of no outliers

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [STATS_ADVANCED_010]

C. Logistic Regression
D. Polynomial Regression

Q11 [STATS_ADVANCED_011]

D. A measure of the range

Q12 [STATS_ADVANCED_012]

B. The dependent variable

Q13 [STATS_ADVANCED_013]

Q14 [STATS_ADVANCED_014]

D. The intercept on the X-axis

Q15 [STATS_ADVANCED_015]

D. Linear Regression

Q16 [STATS_ADVANCED_016]

Q17 [STATS_ADVANCED_017]

Q18 [STATS_ADVANCED_018]

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [STATS_ADVANCED_019]
The "Law of Large Numbers" states that:

A. Large numbers are harder to calculate
B. As a sample size grows, its mean gets closer to the average of the whole population
C. You need at least 1,000,000 rows for statistics
D. Big data is always better than small data

Q20 [STATS_ADVANCED_020]

A. When the user interacts with the graph
B. When the effect of one independent variable on the dependent variable changes depending on the level of another independent variable

D. When the residuals are correlated

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 11: Data Visualization

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATAVIZ_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary goal of data visualization?

A. To make reports look artistic
B. To communicate information clearly and efficiently via statistical graphics
C. To hide errors in the raw data
D. To replace the need for data analysis

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATAVIZ_BEGINNER_002]
Which chart type is best for showing a trend over a continuous period of time?

A. Pie Chart

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the danger of a "Truncated Y-Axis" (starting the axis above zero)?

A. It makes the chart hard to read
B. It can visually exaggerate small differences between data points
C. It makes the file size larger
D. It hides the legend

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_002]
A "Stacked Bar Chart" is most useful when you want to:

A. Show the total across categories and the sub-components of each category
B. Show a trend over 100 years
C. Compare two unrelated variables
D. Identify outliers in a distribution

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is a "Heatmap" typically used for?

A. Showing the physical temperature of a room
B. Using color intensity to represent the magnitude of a value across two dimensions
C. Creating a 3D model of a city
D. Showing the path of a website user

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_004]
When visualizing "Sequential" data (e.g., Temperature), which color scheme is most appropriate?

A. A palette of many unrelated colors (Rainbow)
B. A single color with varying intensity (e.g., Light Blue to Dark Blue)
C. Two highly contrasting colors like Red and Green
D. Only Black and White

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Which visualization is best for showing the "distribution" and "outliers" of a dataset simultaneously?

A. Line Chart
B. Box Plot (Whisker Plot)
C. Pie Chart
D. Area Chart

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is the purpose of "Dual-Axis" charts?

A. To show two different variables with different scales in the same visual
B. To make the chart look more complex
C. To backup the data
D. To rotate the chart 90 degrees

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is "Visual Encoding"?

A. Writing code to generate charts
B. The process of representing data values using visual properties like length, color, or position
C. Encrypting a chart so only the manager can see it
D. Converting a chart to a PDF

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is a "Treemap"?

A. A chart showing the growth of trees
B. A visualization that uses nested rectangles to show hierarchical data and proportions
C. A map showing forest locations
D. A type of flow chart

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_009]
"Data-Ink Ratio" (concept by Edward Tufte) suggests that:

A. You should use as much ink as possible to make the chart dark
B. Most of the ink in a chart should represent data information, avoiding "chart junk"
C. Charts should be printed with high-quality ink
D. Data should be written in text, not visualized

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_010]
Which of the following is a "Diverging" color palette?

A. Light Gray to Dark Gray
B. Red to White to Blue (showing values above and below a midpoint)
C. Yellow, Green, and Orange
D. All shades of Green

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is a "Slicer" in the context of interactive visualizations?

A. A tool to delete data rows
B. An on-screen button or filter that allows users to segment the data shown in visuals
C. A type of circular chart
D. A method for cutting a report into two pieces

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "Overplotting" in a scatter plot?

A. Plotting too many different variables
B. When data points overlap so much that it's impossible to see the density or individual points
C. Drawing a line that is too thick
D. Placing the legend over the data

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_013]
How can you fix "Overplotting"?

A. Using transparency (Alpha blending) or jittering (adding small random noise to positions)
B. Making the points larger
C. Changing the chart to a Pie Chart
D. Deleting 90% of the data

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is a "Bullet Graph" commonly used for?

A. Tracking progress against a goal or benchmark
B. Showing a time-series trend
C. Visualizing geographic data
D. Identifying correlations

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_015]
Which principle suggests that users perceive objects that are close to each other as being part of a group?

A. Continuity
B. Proximity
C. Similarity
D. Enclosure

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_016]
When should you avoid using a Pie Chart?

A. When you have more than 5-7 categories
B. When the differences between categories are very small
C. When you want to show exact comparisons
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q17 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is "Drill-down" capability in a dashboard?

A. The ability to delete the dashboard
B. The ability to click a high-level data point to see more granular details
C. A physical tool for servers
D. A way to increase the brightness of the screen

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does an "Area Chart" emphasize that a Line Chart does not?

A. The exact data points
B. The magnitude of change and the volume under the line
C. The names of the categories
D. The outliers

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_019]
Which visualization is best for showing a process or flow from one stage to another?

A. Sankey Diagram
B. Histogram
C. Scatter Plot
D. Box Plot

Correct Answer: A

Q20 [DATAVIZ_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is the "Gestalt Principle of Similarity"?

A. People perceive similar elements (in color, shape, or size) as being part of the same group
B. Similar charts should be on the same page
C. Data analysts should look similar
D. All data points should be the same size

Correct Answer: A

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_001]
What are "Pre-attentive Attributes"?

A. Features that require a long time to process
B. Visual properties (like color, orientation, size) that the brain processes almost instantaneously before conscious thought
C. Settings in the visualization software
D. Data points that are missing

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_002]
In the context of accessibility, why is the "Red-Green" color palette often avoided?

A. It is visually unappealing
B. It is the most common form of color blindness (Deuteranopia)
C. Red and Green are too similar in intensity
D. Those colors are only for financial data

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Lie Factor" in a visualization?

A. The number of incorrect data points
B. The ratio of the size of the effect shown in the graphic to the size of the effect in the data
C. A metric for how much the analyst lied in the report
D. The number of missing labels

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_004]

A. Waffle Chart
B. Treemap
C. Sorted Bar Chart
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q5 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_005]
What is a "Small Multiple" (Trellis Plot)?

A. A chart with very small numbers
B. A series of similar graphs with the same scale and axes, allowing them to be easily compared
C. A bar chart with only two bars
D. A thumbnail of a dashboard

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_006]
What is "Cognitive Load" in dashboard design?

A. The amount of electricity the computer uses

D. The number of users viewing the report

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_007]
How can you reduce "Cognitive Load" in a complex dashboard?

A. Adding more text
B. Removing unnecessary borders, gridlines, and using clear visual hierarchy
C. Using more 3D effects
D. Adding more colors to differentiate everything

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Visual Hierarchy"?

A. The organizational structure of the design team
B. The arrangement of elements in a way that implies importance and guides the eye through the information
C. Sorting data alphabetically
D. Putting the largest chart at the bottom

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_009]
What is a "Sunburst Chart"?

A. A chart that looks like the sun
B. A multi-level pie chart used to visualize hierarchical data
C. A map showing sunlight intensity
D. A type of scatter plot with rays

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_010]
When is a "Logarithmic Scale" used on an axis?

A. When you want to make the numbers look smaller
B. When data covers a very large range of values (orders of magnitude)
C. When the data is already normalized
D. When you want to hide a decrease in sales

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Slopegraph" used for?

A. To measure the slope of a hill
B. To show the change in rank or value between two time periods or categories
C. To create a 3D bar chart
D. To identify outliers in a single category

Q12 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_012]

A. The data line
B. The axes and labels
C. The trend
D. The data points

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_013]
What is the primary benefit of a "Violin Plot" over a standard "Box Plot"?

A. It shows the color of the data
B. It shows the full probability density of the data, revealing multiple peaks (modality)
C. It is easier to explain to non-technical users
D. It uses less screen space

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_014]
What is "Data Latency" in a dashboard?

A. The time it takes for a user to understand the chart
B. The time delay between when data is generated and when it appears in the visualization
C. The speed of the mouse cursor
D. The distance between the server and the user

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_015]
"Enclosure" (a Gestalt principle) is achieved in a dashboard by:

A. Using a common background color or a border to group related visuals
B. Making all charts the same size
C. Putting the dashboard in a folder
D. Encrypting the file

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_016]
What is the "F-Pattern" in user behavior?

A. A way to grade a dashboard (A to F)
B. The tendency of users to scan a screen in an "F" shape (top to bottom, left to right)
C. A type of statistical distribution
D. A failure in the visualization software

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_017]

B. Bar Chart
C. Histogram
D. Pie Chart

Q18 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_018]
What does "Exploded" mean in the context of a Pie Chart?

A. The chart was deleted
B. One or more slices are separated from the rest of the chart for emphasis
C. The data is incorrect
D. The chart is shown in 3D

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Brushing and Linking" in interactive data visualization?

A. Cleaning the data and linking the tables
B. Selecting data in one view and having it automatically highlighted in all other related views
C. Deleting data in one view and having it deleted in others
D. Changing the color of all charts at once

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [DATAVIZ_ADVANCED_020]
What is the "McKinsey Style" of visualization often characterized by?

A. Using bright neon colors
B. Clean, minimalist design with clear "So What?" headers that explain the insight

## Skill 12: Predictive Analytics

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary goal of Predictive Analytics?

A. To summarize what happened in the past
B. To use historical data to make predictions about future events
C. To delete irrelevant data from the database
D. To create artistic visualizations of current trends

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_002]
Predictive analytics is a branch of:

A. Descriptive Analytics
B. Advanced Analytics
C. Hardware Engineering
D. Financial Auditing

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_003]
Which of the following questions does Predictive Analytics answer?

A. What is our current revenue?
B. Why did we lose customers last month?
C. What is the likely sales volume for next quarter?
/D. Which region had the highest growth last year?

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_004]
In predictive modeling, the "Target Variable" is:

A. The variable we want to predict
B. The variable used to make the prediction
C. A variable that remains constant
D. The name of the dataset

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_005]
In predictive modeling, "Features" or "Predictors" are:

A. The final results of the model
B. The input variables used to make a prediction
C. Errors found in the data
D. The people who use the software

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_006]

A. Churn Prediction (identifying customers likely to leave)
B. Printing monthly payroll
C. Creating a company logo
D. Setting up a local Wi-Fi network

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_007]
True or False: Predictive analytics guarantees that a future event will happen exactly as predicted.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q8 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_008]

A. A set of instructions for employees
/B. A portion of data used to "teach" the model the relationship between inputs and outputs
C. The final report presented to the board
D. A dataset containing only incorrect information

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_009]
What is a "Test Set"?

A. A dataset used to evaluate how well the model predicts on new, unseen data
B. A dataset used to train the model
C. A list of questions for the analyst
D. A backup copy of the database

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_010]
Which type of predictive model would you use to predict a numerical value (e.g., house price)?

A. Classification
B. Regression
C. Clustering
D. Association

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_011]

A. Regression
B. Classification
C. Summarization
D. Standardization

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_012]

A. The loan amount
B. The applicant's credit score
C. The Approval status
D. The interest rate

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_013]
"Historical Data" is important for predictive analytics because:

A. It is required by law
B. It provides the patterns and trends the model learns from
C. It takes up less space than new data
D. It doesn't need to be cleaned

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_014]

A. Data Collection

Q15 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_015]

Q16 [PRED_ANALYTICS_BEGINNER_016]

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is "Overfitting"?

A. When a model is too simple to capture the trend
B. When a model learns the training data too well, including its noise, making it perform poorly on new data
C. When the dataset is too small
D. When the model takes too long to run

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is "Underfitting"?

A. When a model is too complex
B. When a model is too simple to capture the underlying pattern in the data
C. When the model uses too many features
D. When the model is 100% accurate

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_003]
Which metric is commonly used to evaluate the error of a regression model?

A. Accuracy
B. F1-Score
C. Mean Squared Error (MSE)
D. Precision

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What is a "Confusion Matrix"?

A. A matrix that makes the analyst confused
B. A table used to describe the performance of a classification model
C. A method for cleaning data
D. A type of regression model

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_005]
In a confusion matrix, "True Positives" are:

A. Cases where the model predicted "No" but the actual was "Yes"
B. Cases where the model predicted "Yes" and the actual was "Yes"
C. Cases where the model predicted "Yes" but the actual was "No"
D. Cases where the model was wrong

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is "Logistic Regression" primarily used for?

A. Predicting continuous stock prices
B. Classification tasks (predicting the probability of a class)
C. Creating 3D charts
D. Calculating the mean of a dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is the "Baseline" in predictive analytics?

A. The bottom line of a chart
B. A simple model (like a random guess or an average) used to compare more complex models against
C. The first row of the dataset
D. The title of the project

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What does "Feature Selection" involve?

A. Adding as many variables as possible to the model
B. Identifying and keeping only the most relevant variables to improve model performance
C. Renaming the columns in the database
D. Changing the data type of every column

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is "Data Leakage"?

A. A security breach where data is stolen
B. When information from outside the training dataset is used to create the model, leading to overly optimistic results
C. When the computer runs out of RAM
D. When data is deleted by mistake

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_010]

A. Accuracy
B. F1-Score
C. Total Count
D. Mean

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is "R-Squared" in the context of regression?

A. The square root of the mean
B. A measure of how much variance in the dependent variable is explained by the model
C. The number of rows in the model
D. The probability of an error

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_012]
A "Decision Tree" works by:

A. Multiplying all values together
B. Splitting the data into branches based on feature values to arrive at a prediction
C. Drawing a picture of a forest
D. Randomly choosing a value

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is "Precision" in classification?

A. The ability of the model to find all positive cases
B. The proportion of predicted positive cases that were actually correct
C. The speed of the model
D. The total number of correct predictions

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is "Recall" (Sensitivity)?

A. The proportion of actual positive cases that were correctly identified by the model
B. Remembering the data from last year
C. The percentage of rows that were deleted
D. The average error rate

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_015]

A. Predicting a person's age
B. Predicting future values based on past values ordered in time
C. Scheduling a meeting
D. Measuring how long a query takes

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_016]

A. Seasonality
B. Alphabetical order
C. Font size
D. Row count

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is "Normalization" in predictive analytics?

A. Making the data look normal
B. Scaling numerical features to a standard range (e.g., 0 to 1)
C. Deleting outliers
D. Converting text to numbers

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_018]

A. Predict the price of a basket
B. Identify products that are frequently bought together

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_019]
A "Type I Error" in predictive analytics is also known as a:

A. False Negative
B. False Positive
C. True Positive
D. Random Error

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [PRED_ANALYTICS_INTERMEDIATE_020]

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_001]
What is "Cross-Validation" (e.g., K-Fold)?

A. Checking if the data is valid
B. A technique for assessing how the results of a statistical analysis will generalize to an independent data set
C. Cross-referencing two different databases
D. A way to encrypt the training set

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_002]
What does "AUC-ROC" measure?

A. The speed of a database
B. The performance of a classification model across all possible thresholds
C. The average revenue of a company
D. The number of clusters in the data

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_003]
What is a "Random Forest"?

A. A collection of randomly selected data points
B. An ensemble of many decision trees that outputs the average or majority prediction
C. A type of data visualization with green dots
D. A database stored on multiple servers

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_004]

A. Changing the hardware of the computer
B. Optimizing the settings of an algorithm that are not learned from the data (e.g., depth of a tree)
C. Deleting features that are too large
D. Sorting the data by importance

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_005]
In the context of predictive analytics, what is "Bias"?

A. A preference for one brand over another
B. The error introduced by approximating a real-life problem with a simplified model
C. The total count of data points
D. A mistake in data entry

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_006]
In the context of predictive analytics, what is "Variance"?

A. The difference between the highest and lowest value
B. The model's sensitivity to small fluctuations in the training set
C. The square root of the mean
D. The color intensity of a chart

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_007]
What is the "Bias-Variance Trade-off"?

A. A deal between two analysts
B. The balance between a model being too simple (high bias) and too complex (high variance)
C. Increasing the number of rows while decreasing columns
D. Trading old data for new data

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Gradient Boosting"?

A. Making the charts look brighter
B. An ensemble technique that builds models sequentially, each one correcting the errors of the previous one

D. A method for deleting outliers

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_009]

A. A list of features in order of their names

D. The size of the data file

Q10 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Principal Component Analysis" (PCA) used for?

A. To predict the future
B. Dimensionality reduction by transforming features into a smaller set of uncorrelated variables
C. To find the mean of the dataset
D. To join two tables

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_011]

A. A way for computers to talk to each other
B. A set of algorithms modeled after the human brain that are designed to recognize patterns
C. A type of social network for analysts
D. A hardware component of the CPU

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_012]

A. When the computer takes too long to boot
B. The difficulty in making recommendations for new users or items with no history
C. When the office is too cold
D. When the data is not cleaned properly

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_013]

A. Using multiple computers for one task

Q14 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_014]

Q15 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_015]

Q16 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_016]

Q17 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_017]

A. When the columns have different heights in a bar chart

D. When the mean is not zero

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_018]

D. Training the model twice

Q19 [PRED_ANALYTICS_ADVANCED_019]

## Skill 13: Predictive Modeling

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of a predictive model?

A. To record historical transactions for tax purposes
B. To use mathematical techniques to predict future outcomes based on historical data
C. To clean the database of all duplicates
D. To create a visual logo for a data company

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_002]
In a predictive model, the variable you are trying to predict is called the:

A. Independent Variable
B. Predictor
C. Target Variable (Dependent Variable)
D. Constant

Correct Answer: C

Q3 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_003]
Predicting whether a customer will "Renew" or "Cancel" a subscription is an example of:

A. Regression
B. Classification
C. Clustering
D. Data Cleaning

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_004]
Predicting the exact dollar amount of sales for the next month is an example of:

A. Classification
B. Regression
C. Sorting
D. Pivot Table

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_005]
Which of the following is an input into a predictive model?

A. Features
B. Predictions
C. Accuracy Scores
D. Model Reports

Correct Answer: A

Q6 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_006]
What does the "Training" phase of modeling involve?

A. Presenting the results to a manager
B. Showing the model historical data so it can learn patterns
C. Deleting the model and starting over
D. Asking users to fill out a survey

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_007]
Why do we set aside a "Test Set" in predictive modeling?

A. To have a backup in case the computer crashes
B. To evaluate the model's performance on data it hasn't seen before
C. To increase the size of the training data
D. To make the modeling process take longer

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_008]
If a model predicts "True" for a situation that is actually "True", it is called a:

A. False Positive
B. True Positive
C. False Negative
D. True Negative

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_009]
What is a "Categorical" variable?

A. A variable that represents a count or measurement
B. A variable that represents labels or groups (e.g., Red, Blue, Green)
C. A variable that is always zero
D. A variable that changes every second

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: Predictive models are always 100% accurate.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q11 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_011]

A. Microsoft Notepad
B. Microsoft Excel
C. Adobe Premiere
D. Windows Media Player

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_012]
What is "Historical Data"?

A. Data about ancient civilizations
B. Past data used to train a model to recognize patterns
C. Data that is no longer useful
D. Data stored on paper only

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_013]
In the equation of a line (y = mx + b), what represents the target variable?

A. m
B. x
C. y
D. b

Correct Answer: C

Q14 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_014]
What is an "Algorithm" in the context of modeling?

A. A type of computer monitor
B. A set of mathematical rules or logic used to build the model
C. The person who cleans the data
D. A mistake in the dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_015]
Which step comes first in the predictive modeling process?

A. Model Evaluation
B. Data Collection and Preparation
C. Model Deployment
D. Making Predictions

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_016]
What is an "Observation" in a dataset?

A. A single row representing one instance or record
B. A column title
C. A chart or graph
D. A comment from the analyst

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_017]
Predicting a "Low", "Medium", or "High" risk level is a:

A. Binary Classification
B. Multi-class Classification
C. Linear Regression
D. Data Sorting task

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_018]

A. To give the model a trophy
B. To apply the model to new data to generate predictions
C. To count the number of rows in the model
D. To delete the model

Q19 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_019]
What is a "Binary" outcome?

A. An outcome with ten possibilities
B. An outcome with only two possibilities (e.g., 0 or 1, Yes or No)
C. An outcome that is a decimal number
D. An outcome that is missing

Q20 [PREMOD_BEGINNER_020]

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is "Overfitting"?

A. When a model is too simple to capture the data's patterns
B. When a model performs well on training data but poorly on new (test) data
C. When the model takes up too much disk space
D. When you have too few columns in your dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is "Underfitting"?

A. When a model is too complex
B. When a model is too simple to capture the underlying trend of the data
C. When the model is 100% accurate
D. When you have too many rows of data

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_003]
Which metric is commonly used to measure the average error of a regression model?

A. Accuracy
B. Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE)
C. F1-Score
D. Precision

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_004]
In a Confusion Matrix, what is a "False Positive"?

A. The model predicted "No" but it was actually "Yes"
B. The model predicted "Yes" but it was actually "No"
C. The model predicted "Yes" and it was "Yes"
D. The model was unable to make a prediction

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is "Linear Regression" used for?

A. To predict a category
B. To model the relationship between a dependent variable and one or more independent variables
C. To group data into clusters
D. To find the most frequent word in a text

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is "R-Squared"?

A. The square root of the mean
B. A statistical measure of how close the data are to the fitted regression line
C. The number of rows in the training set
D. The total number of errors in the model

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is "Feature Selection"?

A. Choosing the color of the chart
B. The process of selecting the most relevant features to include in the model
C. Asking the manager which columns they like
D. Deleting every second column in the dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What does "Logistic Regression" predict?

A. The exact price of a stock
B. The probability of an observation belonging to a specific category
C. The distance between two points
D. The mean of a numerical column

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is "Correlation" in the context of predictive modeling?

A. One variable causing another
B. A measure of the linear relationship between two variables
C. The total count of missing values
D. A type of database join

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_010]
Which of the following is a common classification algorithm?

A. K-Means Clustering
B. Decision Tree
C. Principal Component Analysis
D. VLOOKUP

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is "Precision" in classification?

A. The ratio of correctly predicted positive observations to the total predicted positives
B. The ratio of correctly predicted positive observations to all observations in actual class
C. The speed of the model calculation
D. The number of rows in the test set

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "Recall"?

A. The ability of a model to find all the positive cases in the dataset
B. The ability of a model to be fast
C. The process of deleting old models
D. The ratio of True Negatives to False Positives

Correct Answer: A

Q13 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_013]
The "F1-Score" is the harmonic mean of:

A. Mean and Median
B. Precision and Recall
C. Accuracy and Error Rate
D. Bias and Variance

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is "Multicollinearity"?

A. When the target variable is missing
B. When two or more independent variables are highly correlated with each other
C. When a table has too many columns
D. When the model is used in multiple countries

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_015]
In a Decision Tree, what is a "Leaf Node"?

A. The starting point of the tree
B. A node that represents a final outcome or prediction
C. A branch that connects two nodes
D. A row that was deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is "One-Hot Encoding"?

A. A way to heat up the server
B. A process of converting categorical variables into a format that can be provided to ML algorithms (Binary columns)
C. Using only one feature to build a model
D. Deleting columns with low temperature

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is a "Residual" in regression?

A. The data left over after a project
B. The difference between the observed value and the predicted value
C. The average of the target variable
D. The first row of the test set

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_018]
"Mean Absolute Error" (MAE) measures:

A. The square of the error
B. The average of the absolute differences between predictions and actual observations
C. The percentage of correct guesses
D. The variance of the model

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What is "Data Leakage"?

A. When data is stolen by hackers
B. When information from outside the training dataset is used to create the model
C. When the CSV file has a hole in it
D. When the model is shared with unauthorized users

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [PREMOD_INTERMEDIATE_020]
Which of the following is an example of an "Ensemble" method?

A. Simple Linear Regression
B. Random Forest
C. Pivot Table
D. Single Decision Tree

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_001]
What is "K-Fold Cross-Validation"?

A. Folding the data on a piece of paper
B. Splitting the data into 'k' subsets and training/testing the model 'k' times to ensure stability
C. Multiplying the data by a factor of 'k'
D. Deleting 'k' rows from the dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_002]
What is "Lasso Regression" (L1 Regularization) primarily used for?

A. To make the model more complex
B. To prevent overfitting by penalizing the absolute size of coefficients, which can lead to feature selection
C. To predict time-series data
D. To increase the R-squared value manually

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Ridge Regression" (L2 Regularization)?

A. A regression used for mountainous terrain data
B. A technique that adds a penalty equal to the square of the magnitude of coefficients to prevent overfitting
C. A model that only uses the highest values
D. A way to join two regression models

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_004]
What is the "Bias-Variance Trade-off"?

A. A negotiation between two data scientists
B. The balance between error from overly simple assumptions (Bias) and error from high sensitivity to training data (Variance)
C. Trading a high accuracy for a low speed
D. Swapping training data for test data

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_005]
In the context of AUC-ROC, what does "AUC" stand for?

A. Average Unit Cost
B. Area Under the Curve
C. Actual User Count
D. Automated Universal Calculation

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_006]
What is "Hyperparameter Tuning"?

A. Increasing the speed of the CPU
B. Adjusting the settings of the algorithm (e.g., depth of a tree) to find the best model performance
C. Cleaning the features twice
D. Renaming the variables in the code

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_007]
"Random Forest" is an ensemble method based on:

A. Boosting
B. Bagging (Bootstrap Aggregating)
C. Stacking
D. Linearization

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Gradient Boosting"?

A. Making the charts look more colorful
B. An ensemble technique where new models are added to correct the errors made by existing models
C. A way to increase the data loading speed
D. A type of data encryption

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_009]
What is "Principal Component Analysis" (PCA) used for in modeling?

A. To predict the future
B. Dimensionality reduction (reducing the number of features while keeping the most information)
C. To join multiple databases
D. To find the mean of a column

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_010]
What is "SMOTE" (Synthetic Minority Over-sampling Technique) used for?

A. Identifying outliers
B. Handling imbalanced datasets by creating synthetic examples of the minority class
C. Smoothing time-series data
D. Encrypting sensitive information

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Elastic Net" regression?

A. A regression used for fishing industry data
B. A regularized regression method that linearly combines the L1 and L2 penalties
C. A model that automatically resizes the dataset
D. A way to combine SQL and Python

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_012]
What does "Heteroscedasticity" mean in regression analysis?

A. The residuals have a constant variance
B. The variance of the residuals is not constant across all levels of an independent variable
C. The variables are perfectly correlated
D. There are no missing values in the dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_013]
Which metric is best for evaluating a model where the cost of a "False Negative" is extremely high (e.g., cancer detection)?

A. Precision
B. Recall (Sensitivity)
C. Accuracy
D. Specificity

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_014]
"Stochastic Gradient Descent" is an optimization algorithm used to:

A. Clean the data

C. Randomly sort the rows
D. Visualize the decision tree

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Feature Engineering"?

A. Fixing a broken software feature
B. Creating new features or transforming existing ones to improve model performance
C. Building a computer server
D. Writing the documentation for a model

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Model Drift"?

A. When the server physically moves
B. The degradation of a model's predictive power over time as the environment/data changes
C. A slow data transfer process
D. A mistake in the model's logic

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_017]

A. Machines that provide technical support

C. A type of hardware used for big data
D. A social network for data engineers

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Time Series Decomposition"?

A. Deleting old time-based data
B. Separating a time series into trend, seasonality, and noise components
C. Joining two time-series datasets
D. Predicting the time it takes to run a model

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_019]
What is the "VIF" (Variance Inflation Factor) used for?

A. To measure model accuracy
B. To detect the presence and severity of multicollinearity
C. To scale the data between 0 and 1
D. To count the number of variables

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [PREMOD_ADVANCED_020]
What is "A/B Testing" in the context of predictive modeling?

A. Testing the model on two different computers

D. Deleting the bottom half of the results

Q18 [PANDAS_ADVANCED_018]

A. Calculate multiple different statistics at once (e.g., mean and sum)
B. Process data faster
C. Handle missing values automatically
D. Work with text columns only

Correct Answer: A

Q19 [PANDAS_ADVANCED_019]

A. It uses more memory than object/string types
B. It can significantly reduce memory usage and improve performance for columns with few unique values
C. It cannot be sorted
D. It is only for numerical data

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [PANDAS_ADVANCED_020]
What does `df.shift(1)` do?

A. Moves the whole DataFrame to a different server
B. Shifts the index by one position without changing the data
C. Shifts the data down by one row, leaving a NaN at the top
D. Deletes the first row

Correct Answer: C

C. TO_TYPE()
D. FORMAT()

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_001]
What is a Common Table Expression (CTE)?

A. A permanent table shared by all users
B. A temporary result set defined using the `WITH` clause
C. An index that spans multiple tables
D. A type of stored procedure

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_002]

A. ROW_NUMBER()
B. RANK()
C. DENSE_RANK()
D. NTILE()

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_003]
How does a 'Recursive CTE' work?

A. It runs the same query on different databases
B. It is a CTE that references itself, useful for hierarchical data like org charts
C. It is a CTE that never finishes
D. It is a CTE used for mathematical calculations only

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_004]
What is the purpose of the `EXPLAIN` command?

A. To explain the database schema to new users

C. To provide a summary of the data in a table
D. To comment out a block of code

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_005]
Which PostgreSQL feature allows you to perform calculations across a set of rows related to the current row?

A. GROUP BY
B. OVER() clause (Window Functions)
C. CROSS JOIN
D. AGGREGATE()

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_006]
What is the difference between `RANK()` and `DENSE_RANK()`?

B. `DENSE_RANK()` skips positions; `RANK()` does not
C. `RANK()` is only for numbers
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_007]
What is an 'Index' and why is it used?

A. A list of all table names used for documentation
B. A data structure that improves the speed of data retrieval operations
C. A constraint that prevents null values
D. A way to organize data alphabetically on the screen

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_008]

D. MERGE_ROWS()

Q9 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_009]

B. A virtual table based on the result-set of an SQL statement
C. A specific layout in pgAdmin
D. A type of database backup

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_010]

A. To delete the database
B. To reclaim storage occupied by deleted or updated rows (dead tuples)

D. To sort the tables alphabetically

Q11 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_011]

D. ASSERT

Q13 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_013]

D. A view used for 3D modeling

Q14 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_014]

D. Joins two tables

Q15 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_015]

D. GIN stands for "General Internal Network"

Q16 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_016]

Q17 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_017]

A. SAVE
B. COMMIT
C. ROLLBACK
D. END

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_018]

A. Selects the data and automatically updates it to zero
B. Locks the selected rows against concurrent updates or deletions by other transactions
C. Selects data only if it was recently updated
D. Updates the timestamp of the row

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_019]

A. To create a sequence of numbers or dates
B. To create a series of new tables
C. To generate random passwords
D. To count the number of series in a column

Q20 [POSTGRES_ADVANCED_020]

C. A mathematical formula used in Excel
D. A column that stores calculation results

## Skill 24: REST API

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between query parameters and path parameters?

A. Path parameters are used to identify a specific resource; Query parameters are used to sort or filter
B. Query parameters are used for security; Path parameters are for data
C. There is no difference
D. Query parameters are only used in POST requests

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_002]
Which status code is returned when a user tries to access a resource without providing a valid API key or token?

A. 400 Bad Request
B. 401 Unauthorized
C. 403 Forbidden
D. 404 Not Found

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is "Idempotency" in REST APIs?

A. The ability of the API to run on multiple servers
B. Making the same request multiple times has the same effect as making it once
C. The speed at which the API responds
D. The process of encrypting the JSON body

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_004]
Which of the following HTTP methods are considered idempotent?

A. GET, PUT, DELETE
B. POST, GET
C. POST only
D. DELETE and POST

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is the purpose of the `Content-Type` header?

A. To define the size of the file
B. To tell the server or client the media type of the resource (e.g., application/json)
C. To identify the user's name
D. To set the expiration date of the data

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is the difference between PUT and PATCH?

A. PUT updates a resource partially; PATCH replaces it entirely
B. PUT replaces a resource entirely; PATCH performs partial updates
C. PUT is for creating; PATCH is for deleting
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_007]
A user receives a 403 Forbidden error. What is the most likely cause?

A. The resource does not exist
B. The user is not logged in
C. The user is authenticated but does not have permission to access the resource
D. The server is down

Correct Answer: C

Q8 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is a "Payload" in the context of an API request?

A. The time it takes to get a response
B. The actual data transmitted in the body of the HTTP request or response
C. The cost of using the API
D. The security certificate of the server

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_009]
How are query parameters appended to a URL to filter for "color=red" and "size=large"?

A. /items?color=red&size=large
B. /items/color=red/size=large
C. /items#color=red&size=large
D. /items?color=red;size=large

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What does a 204 No Content status code mean?

A. The request failed because there was no data
B. The request was successful, but there is no representation to return (common for DELETE)
C. The server is empty
D. The user needs to send more data

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which header is typically used by a client to tell the server what format of data it can handle in response?

A. Content-Type
B. Accept
C. Authorization
D. User-Agent

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "Rate Limiting" in an API?

A. Restricting the size of the JSON body
B. Limiting the number of requests a client can make in a specific timeframe
C. Slowing down the server during maintenance
D. Charging users based on their usage

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_013]
Which status code is returned for a "Bad Request" (e.g., malformed JSON syntax)?

A. 400
B. 401
C. 404
D. 500

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is "API Documentation"?

A. The source code of the API
B. Instructions on how to effectively use and integrate with the API
C. A list of users who have access
D. The legal contract for the API usage

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_015]
True or False: The GET method should never be used to send sensitive data like passwords because it appears in the URL.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q16 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is the purpose of the `Authorization` header?

A. To tell the server what the content is
B. To carry credentials (like a Bearer Token) to authenticate the client
C. To list the server's IP address
D. To define the API version

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_017]
Which HTTP method is neither "Safe" nor "Idempotent"?

A. GET
B. DELETE
C. POST
D. PUT

Correct Answer: C

Q18 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does the "4xx" class of status codes represent?

A. Success
B. Redirection
C. Client Errors
D. Server Errors

Correct Answer: C

Q19 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What does the "5xx" class of status codes represent?

A. Success
B. Information
C. Client Errors
D. Server Errors

Correct Answer: D

Q20 [REST_API_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is a "Resource" in REST?

A. The memory used by the server
B. Any object or data that the API can provide information about or perform actions on
C. The physical server machine
D. The person who wrote the API

Correct Answer: B
