### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [REST_API_ADVANCED_001]
What is "OAuth 2.0"?

A. A new version of the HTTP protocol
B. An open standard for access delegation/authorization commonly used for APIs
C. A data format like JSON
D. A type of database index

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REST_API_ADVANCED_002]
What is a "JWT" (JSON Web Token)?

A. A tool for testing APIs
B. A compact, URL-safe means of representing claims to be transferred between two parties
C. A method for compressing JSON files
D. A type of REST endpoint

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REST_API_ADVANCED_003]
What is "HATEOAS" in REST architecture?

A. A high-speed data transfer protocol
B. A constraint where the client interacts with the application entirely through hypermedia provided dynamically by the server
C. A method for deleting multiple resources at once
D. A security vulnerability

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REST_API_ADVANCED_004]
Why is "API Versioning" important?

A. To allow developers to make breaking changes without interrupting existing users
B. To make the API run faster
C. To show how many people are using the API
D. To change the programming language of the API

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [REST_API_ADVANCED_005]
Which status code is used for "Rate Limit Exceeded"?

A. 401
B. 403
C. 429
D. 503

Correct Answer: C

Q6 [REST_API_ADVANCED_006]
What is "CORS" (Cross-Origin Resource Sharing)?

A. A way to share an API key with friends
B. A security feature that allows or restricts requested resources on a web page to be requested from another domain
C. A method for joining two different APIs
D. A type of cloud storage

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REST_API_ADVANCED_007]
What is the purpose of the `ETag` (Entity Tag) header?

A. To identify the user's name
B. To help with web cache validation and conditional requests (checking if a resource has changed)
C. To tag the data with a timestamp
D. To define the encoding of the body

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [REST_API_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Swagger" (now part of OpenAPI Specification)?

A. A programming language for APIs
B. A framework for describing, producing, consuming, and visualizing RESTful web services
C. A high-performance server
/D. A way to encrypt API responses

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REST_API_ADVANCED_009]
What is an "API Gateway"?

A. A physical door to the server room
B. A management tool that sits between a client and a collection of backend services
C. A type of internet browser
D. A backup server

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [REST_API_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Webhook"?

A. A method for fishing data
B. A way for an app to provide other applications with real-time information (user-defined HTTP callbacks)
C. A type of virus
D. A manual request made by an analyst

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REST_API_ADVANCED_011]
What is the difference between "Authentication" and "Authorization"?

A. Authentication is verifying who you are; Authorization is verifying what you have access to
B. Authorization is verifying who you are; Authentication is what you have access to
C. They are the same thing
D. Authentication is for humans; Authorization is for machines

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [REST_API_ADVANCED_012]
What is a "Bearer Token"?

A. A token used by animal researchers
B. A security token that gives access to the bearer without requiring further identification
C. A token that only works for 5 minutes
D. A token stored in a physical device

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REST_API_ADVANCED_013]
In a large-scale system, what is "API Orchestration"?

A. Playing music with API sounds
B. The process of integrating multiple APIs to complete a business process or transaction
C. Writing code in a single file
D. Deleting unused API endpoints

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REST_API_ADVANCED_014]
What is the purpose of the `Retry-After` header?

A. To tell the user to try again in a different language
B. To tell the client how long to wait before making a follow-up request (usually after a 429 or 503)
C. To refresh the page automatically
D. To indicate when the server will be deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REST_API_ADVANCED_015]
Which status code indicates "Service Unavailable" (e.g., server is overloaded or down for maintenance)?

A. 500
B. 502
C. 503
D. 504

Correct Answer: C

Q16 [REST_API_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Idempotent Key" (usually passed in headers)?

A. A key used to unlock the server
B. A unique value generated by the client which allows the server to recognize subsequent retries of the same request
C. A primary key in the database
D. A password for the API

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REST_API_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Pagination" in an API response?

A. Adding page numbers to a physical report
B. A technique for dividing a large set of results into smaller chunks (pages) to improve performance
C. Translating the API into different languages
D. Sorting the data by page size

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REST_API_ADVANCED_018]
What is "SOAP" in contrast to "REST"?

A. A cleaning product for servers
B. A protocol-based web service that is more rigid and uses XML exclusively
C. A newer version of REST
D. A method for testing APIs in the browser

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [REST_API_ADVANCED_019]
What is the "Request-Response Cycle"?

A. The time it takes to write an API
B. The entire process from a client sending a request to the server returning a response
C. A loop in the code that causes a crash
D. The way data is saved in a database

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REST_API_ADVANCED_020]
What is "Payload Compression" (e.g., Gzip)?

A. Reducing the physical size of the data being transferred to improve speed and reduce bandwidth
B. Encrypting the data
C. Deleting unnecessary columns
D. Changing JSON to CSV

Correct Answer: A

## Skill 25: JSON

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [JSON_BEGINNER_001]
What does JSON stand for?

A. Java Standard Object Notation
B. JavaScript Object Notation
C. JavaScript Offline Network
D. Java Source Object Name

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [JSON_BEGINNER_002]
JSON is primarily used for:

A. Styling web pages
B. Storing and transporting data
C. Creating database schemas physically
D. Compiling Python code

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [JSON_BEGINNER_003]
Which characters are used to surround a JSON object?

A. [ ]
B. ( )
C. { }
D. < >

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [JSON_BEGINNER_004]
Which characters are used to surround a JSON array?

A. { }
B. [ ]
C. < >
D. ( )

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [JSON_BEGINNER_005]
In JSON, "key": "value" pairs are separated by which character?

A. Semicolon (;)
B. Comma (,)
C. Colon (:)
D. Hyphen (-)

Correct Answer: C

Q6 [JSON_BEGINNER_006]
Which of the following is a valid JSON string?

A. { name: "John" }
B. { "name": "John" }
C. { 'name': 'John' }
D. { name = "John" }

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [JSON_BEGINNER_007]
True or False: JSON keys must be enclosed in double quotes.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q8 [JSON_BEGINNER_008]
Which of these is NOT a supported data type in JSON?

A. String
B. Number
C. Function
D. Boolean

Correct Answer: C

Q9 [JSON_BEGINNER_009]
How are multiple members of a JSON object separated?

A. With a pipe (|)
B. With a comma (,)
C. With a semicolon (;)
D. With a new line only

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [JSON_BEGINNER_010]
Which of the following represents a valid JSON boolean?

A. "true"
B. True
C. true
D. 1

Correct Answer: C

Q11 [JSON_BEGINNER_011]
Is JSON case-sensitive?

A. Yes
B. No

Correct Answer: A — Yes

Q12 [JSON_BEGINNER_012]
Which value represents an empty or non-existent value in JSON?

A. undefined
B. nil
C. null
D. void

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [JSON_BEGINNER_013]
Which file extension is commonly used for JSON files?

A. .js
B. .json
C. .txt
D. .xml

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [JSON_BEGINNER_014]
What is the output of this JSON snippet?
`["Apple", "Banana", "Cherry"]`

A. A JSON Object
B. A JSON Array
C. A JSON String
D. Invalid Syntax

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [JSON_BEGINNER_015]
Can a JSON object contain another JSON object?

A. Yes
B. No

Correct Answer: A — Yes

Q16 [JSON_BEGINNER_016]
Which of the following is a valid JSON number?

A. 1,000
B. 10.5
C. $100
D. "25"

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [JSON_BEGINNER_017]
Which character is used to escape special characters (like quotes) inside a JSON string?

A. /
B. \
C. %
D. #

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [JSON_BEGINNER_018]
True or False: A JSON file can contain comments using `//`.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q19 [JSON_BEGINNER_019]
In the pair `"age": 25`, what is "age"?

A. The Value
B. The Key
C. The Array
D. The Metadata

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [JSON_BEGINNER_020]
Is JSON language-independent?

A. Yes, it can be used with almost any programming language
B. No, it only works with JavaScript
C. No, it only works with web browsers
D. Yes, but it requires a special plugin for Python

Correct Answer: A

Q1 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_001]

A. Converts a JSON string into a JavaScript object

D. Validates the syntax of a JSON file

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_002]

A. Converts a JSON string into an object
B. Converts a JavaScript object into a JSON string
C. Formats a JSON file with colors
D. Searches for a key in a JSON object

Q3 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_003]

A. "id" should not have quotes
B. There is a trailing comma after "Test"
C. 1 should be in quotes
D. There is no error

A. text/json
B. application/json
C. application/javascript
D. text/javascript

B. user["address"]["city"]
C. Both A and B (in JavaScript)
D. city.address.user

Correct Answer: C

Q6 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_006]

A. More verbose

A. Object
B. Map
C. Array
D. Dictionary

Correct Answer: C

Q8 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_008]

A. Yes, using the Date type
B. No, dates are usually stored as strings (ISO 8601) or numbers (timestamps)
C. Yes, but only in curly braces
D. No, JSON cannot store time-related information

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_009]

B. Smith
C. Anna
D. Undefined

Q11 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_011]

Q12 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_012]

Q13 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_013]

Q14 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_014]

A. Yes
B. No

Q15 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_015]

C. To convert JSON to a CSV file
D. To encrypt the JSON file

Q16 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_016]

B. `{ [1, 2], [3, 4] }`

Q17 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_017]

B. ;
C. ,
D. /

Q18 [JSON_INTERMEDIATE_018]

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [JSON_ADVANCED_001]
What is "JSON Schema"?

A. A tool to create JSON files automatically
B. A declarative language that allows you to annotate and validate JSON documents
C. The original draft of the JSON specification
D. A database that only stores JSON

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [JSON_ADVANCED_002]
What is the purpose of the `reviver` parameter in `JSON.parse(text, reviver)`?

A. To fix broken JSON syntax
B. To transform the resulting object before it is returned (e.g., converting date strings to Date objects)
C. To speed up the parsing process
D. To ignore specific keys

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [JSON_ADVANCED_003]
What is the purpose of the `replacer` parameter in `JSON.stringify(value, replacer)`?

A. To replace quotes with apostrophes
B. To filter or transform the properties of the object before stringifying
C. To change the character encoding
D. To replace JSON with XML

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [JSON_ADVANCED_004]
How can you use `JSON.stringify` to format a JSON string with 4-space indentation?

A. `JSON.stringify(obj, null, 4)`
B. `JSON.stringify(obj, 4)`
C. `JSON.stringify(obj, "indent4")`
D. `JSON.stringify(obj, {space: 4})`

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [JSON_ADVANCED_005]
What is "JSONP" (JSON with Padding)?

A. A method to compress JSON files
B. A historical technique used to overcome cross-domain restrictions (CORS) by using `<script>` tags

D. A security vulnerability that must be avoided

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [JSON_ADVANCED_006]

B. contains

D. strict

Q7 [JSON_ADVANCED_007]

Correct Answer: C

Q8 [JSON_ADVANCED_008]

A. JSON for Large Datasets
B. JSON for Linked Data, a method of encoding linked data using JSON
C. JSON for Local Databases
D. A compressed version of JSON

Q9 [JSON_ADVANCED_009]

A. It creates an infinite string
B. It returns `null`
C. It throws a `TypeError: Converting circular structure to JSON`
D. It skips the circular part

Correct Answer: C

Q10 [JSON_ADVANCED_010]
What is the "BSON" format?

A. A way to store JSON on a CD-ROM
B. Binary JSON, a binary-encoded serialization of JSON-like documents used in MongoDB
C. Basic Standard Object Network
D. A version of JSON that supports comments

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [JSON_ADVANCED_011]

A. `obj.clone()`
B. `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(obj))`
C. `JSON.copy(obj)`
D. `Object.assign({}, obj)` (Note: This is shallow)

Q12 [JSON_ADVANCED_012]

Q13 [JSON_ADVANCED_013]

B. Control characters (U+0000 through U+001F)
C. Apostrophe (')
D. Exclamation mark (!)

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [JSON_ADVANCED_014]

A. JSON used in video files

C. Sending JSON via Bluetooth
D. Encrypting JSON in real-time

Q15 [JSON_ADVANCED_015]
In the context of security, what is "JSON Injection"?

C. Converting an XML file to JSON maliciously
D. Using a JSON file as a virus

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [JSON_ADVANCED_016]

/B. A format where each line is a separate, valid JSON object, often used for log files
C. A way to add comments to JSON
D. A method to shorten JSON keys

Q17 [JSON_ADVANCED_017]

C. It throws an error
D. It returns the length of the Map/Set

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [JSON_ADVANCED_018]

A. A method that converts the object to a string
B. A method that `JSON.stringify()` calls to determine how that specific object should be serialized
C. A method to validate JSON
D. A built-in browser tool

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [JSON_ADVANCED_019]

A. UTF-8
B. UTF-16
C. ASCII
D. Latin-1

Correct Answer: A

Q20 [JSON_ADVANCED_020]

B. It converts them to `null` during stringification
C. It throws an error
D. It converts them to the string "NaN"

D. A way to delete an issue automatically

Q3 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_003]
How can you use Trello for Agile Sprint planning as a Business Analyst?

D. All of the above

Q4 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_004]

Q5 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_005]

Q7 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_007]

Q8 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_008]

Q9 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_009]

Q11 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_011]

Q12 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_012]

Q13 [TRELLO_ADVANCED_013]

## Skill 28: Agile/Scrum

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary goal of the Agile methodology?

A. To follow a rigid plan from start to finish
B. To deliver high-quality software through iterative development and customer feedback
C. To minimize the number of meetings
D. To eliminate the need for documentation

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_002]
Which of the following is NOT one of the four core values of the Agile Manifesto?

A. Individuals and interactions over processes and tools
B. Working software over comprehensive documentation
C. Customer collaboration over contract negotiation
D. Following a plan over responding to change

Correct Answer: D

Q3 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_003]
How many roles are officially defined in the Scrum Guide?

A. 3
B. 5
C. 7
D. 9

Correct Answer: A

Q4 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_004]
In Scrum, who is responsible for maximizing the value of the product?

A. Scrum Master
B. Developers
C. Product Owner
D. Project Manager

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_005]
Which role is responsible for facilitating Scrum ceremonies and removing impediments for the team?

A. Product Owner
B. Scrum Master
C. Stakeholder
D. Team Lead

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_006]
What is a "Sprint" in Scrum?

A. A long-distance race
B. A fixed-length event of one month or less to create a "Done" increment
C. A meeting where the team demonstrates work
D. A document listing all requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_007]
What is the maximum recommended duration for a Daily Scrum (Daily Stand-up)?

A. 5 minutes
B. 15 minutes
C. 30 minutes
D. 1 hour

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_008]

A. Sprint Backlog
B. Product Backlog
C. Increment
D. Burnup Chart

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_009]
What are the three pillars of Empiricism in Scrum?

A. Transparency, Inspection, Adaptation
B. Planning, Execution, Delivery
C. Scope, Time, Budget
D. Commitment, Focus, Respect

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_010]
During which meeting does the Scrum Team plan the work to be performed in the current Sprint?

A. Sprint Review
B. Sprint Planning
C. Daily Scrum
D. Retrospective

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_011]
Who makes the final decision on the order (prioritization) of items in the Product Backlog?

A. Scrum Master
B. Developers
C. Product Owner
D. CEO

Correct Answer: C

Q12 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_012]
What happens to unfinished Sprint Backlog items at the end of a Sprint?

A. They are automatically marked as "Done"
B. They are deleted
C. They return to the Product Backlog
D. The Sprint is extended until they are finished

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_013]
The five values of Scrum are: Commitment, Focus, Openness, Respect, and:

A. Courage
B. Speed
C. Intelligence
D. Discipline

Q14 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_014]
Which event occurs at the end of the Sprint to inspect the Increment and adapt the Product Backlog?

A. Sprint Planning
B. Sprint Review
C. Sprint Retrospective
D. Daily Scrum

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_015]
Which event occurs at the end of the Sprint for the Scrum Team to inspect itself and plan improvements?

A. Sprint Review
B. Sprint Retrospective
C. Release Planning
D. Daily Scrum

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_016]
True or False: Scrum Teams should be cross-functional and self-managing.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q17 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_017]
What is the "Definition of Done"?

A. A list of features the customer wants
B. A shared understanding of what it means for work to be complete
C. The end date of the project
D. A status in Jira

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_018]

A. Scrum Master
B. Product Owner
C. Developers
D. External Consultants

Correct Answer: C

Q19 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_019]
What does "Timeboxing" mean in Scrum?

A. Recording the time spent on every task
B. Allocating a fixed maximum unit of time to an activity
C. Working overtime to meet a deadline
D. Punching a clock at the start of the day

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [AGILE_SCRUM_BEGINNER_020]
What is the primary difference between Agile and Waterfall?

A. Agile is for software; Waterfall is for construction
B. Agile is iterative; Waterfall is linear/sequential
C. Agile doesn't use developers; Waterfall does
D. Waterfall is faster than Agile

Correct Answer: B

Q1 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_001]

B. An ongoing activity to add detail, estimates, and order to items in the Product Backlog
C. A meeting where the customer accepts the work
D. A hardware upgrade for the server

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_002]

A. Product Owner
B. Scrum Master
C. Developers
D. Stakeholders

Q3 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_003]

A. A long technical document
B. A short, simple description of a feature told from the perspective of the user
C. A story about the company's history
D. A bug report

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What are "Story Points" used for?

A. To track the hours an employee works
B. To measure the relative effort or complexity of a task
C. To reward the fastest developer
D. To define the price of a feature

Q5 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_005]

A. 3
B. 5
C. 10
D. 15

C. A team meeting
D. A positive feedback from a client

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_008]

A. The Product Owner

D. The HR Department

Q12 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_012]

B. The set of Product Backlog items selected for the Sprint, plus a plan for delivering them
C. A list of bugs found in production
D. A record of work done in the previous Sprint

Q13 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_013]

B. Developers

D. Stakeholders

Q14 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_014]

A. The speed of the internet connection

C. The time it takes to release a feature
D. The number of meetings per week

Q15 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_015]

A. To explain the user's feelings

C. To list the developers' names
D. To set the font size of the UI

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_016]

B. How much did I work? Am I tired? When is lunch?
C. Is the boss happy? Is the code clean? Is the server up?
D. Who made the mistake? How to fix it? Whose fault is it?

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is the relationship between the Product Backlog and the Product Goal?

A. There is no relationship
B. The Product Goal is the long-term objective for the Scrum Team, and the Product Backlog describes what will fulfill it
C. The Product Goal is a subset of the Product Backlog
D. The Product Backlog is created only after the Product Goal is achieved

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_018]

A. A sudden increase in work
B. A research task used to explore a technical problem or reduce uncertainty
C. A high-priority bug
D. A team-building exercise

B. False

Q20 [AGILE_SCRUM_INTERMEDIATE_020]

Q1 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_001]

C. The cost of buying new computers
D. A loan taken to start a project

Correct Answer: B

A. Add it immediately to the Sprint Backlog

C. Ask the Developers to work overtime to finish it
D. Cancel the Sprint

D. The time the Product Owner spends planning

Q4 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_004]

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_005]

D. Waterfall is more flexible with scope than Scrum

Q6 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_006]

D. Increasing the length of the Sprint to 6 months

D. Burnup is only for the Scrum Master

Q8 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_008]

Q9 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_009]

Q10 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_010]

Q16 [AGILE_SCRUM_ADVANCED_016]

## Skill 29: Requirement Gathering

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of requirement gathering in a project?

A. To write the final code for the application
B. To identify the needs and constraints of stakeholders for a new or modified product
C. To design the marketing materials for the launch
D. To calculate the final profit of the company

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_002]
Who is a "Stakeholder" in the context of requirement gathering?

A. Only the person who pays for the project
B. Anyone who is affected by or has an interest in the project
C. Only the software developers
D. The competitors of the company

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_003]
Which of the following is a common technique used to gather requirements from a large group of people quickly?

A. One-on-one interviews
B. Surveys/Questionnaires
C. Prototyping
D. Observation

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_004]
What is a "Functional Requirement"?

A. A description of how the system should perform (e.g., speed, security)
B. A description of what the system should do (e.g., "The system must generate a monthly report")
C. The budget required for the project
D. The deadline for the project

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_005]
What is a "Non-functional Requirement"?

A. A requirement that is not important
B. A description of system attributes such as usability, reliability, and performance
C. A list of features the user wants
D. A bug report from the testing phase

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_006]
Which gathering technique involves watching a user perform their daily tasks in their actual work environment?

A. Brainstorming
B. Observation (Job Shadowing)
C. Focus Groups
D. Document Analysis

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_007]
True or False: Requirement gathering only happens once at the very start of a project and never again.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q8 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_008]
What does "Elicitation" mean in the context of requirements?

A. Deleting requirements that are too expensive
B. The process of drawing out requirements from stakeholders
C. Printing the final requirement document
D. Programming the user interface

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_009]
What is a "User Story" typically used for?

A. To tell a fictional story about the software
B. To capture a simplified requirement from the end-user's perspective
C. To list the server specifications
D. To record the meeting minutes

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_010]
Which document is usually the result of the requirement gathering phase?

A. Business Requirement Document (BRD)
B. User Manual
C. Marketing Plan
D. Source Code

Correct Answer: A

Q11 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_011]
What is "Brainstorming"?

A. A technique to solve a computer crash
B. A group activity to generate a large number of ideas or requirements in a short time
C. A method for testing software performance
D. A type of individual interview

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_012]
What is the "Scope" of a project?

A. The range of features and functions that are included in the project
B. The name of the project manager
C. The color theme of the application
D. The physical location of the office

Correct Answer: A

Q13 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_013]
Which technique is best for gathering detailed, qualitative information from a high-level executive?

A. Survey
B. One-on-one Interview
C. Benchmarking
D. Data Modeling

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_014]
What is "Document Analysis"?

A. Writing a new book
B. Reviewing existing business plans, process maps, or system manuals to identify requirements
C. Deleting old files
D. Checking for spelling errors in a report

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_015]
What is a "Constraint" in a requirement document?

A. A new feature request
B. A limitation or restriction on the solution (e.g., "The system must run on Linux")
C. An unlimited budget
D. A list of stakeholders

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_016]
What is the purpose of a "Focus Group"?

A. To interview one person for five hours
B. To gather feedback and requirements from a representative group of users in a guided discussion
C. To train the developers on how to use the software
D. To focus the camera during a video meeting

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_017]
Which of the following is a "Business Requirement"?

A. "The database must use SQL Server"
B. "The system must reduce order processing time by 20%"
C. "The button must be blue"
D. "The page should load in 2 seconds"

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_018]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q19 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_019]
What does "Prioritization" mean in requirement gathering?

A. Doing all requirements at the same time
B. Ranking requirements based on their importance or urgency
C. Deleting the most difficult requirements
D. Assigning a developer to each task

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REQ_GATHERING_BEGINNER_020]
What is "Prototyping" used for in elicitation?

A. To build the final production database
B. To create a preliminary model or sketch to help stakeholders visualize the requirement
C. To backup the requirements
D. To translate requirements into different languages

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between a "Business Requirement" and a "User Requirement"?

A. Business requirements focus on high-level goals; User requirements focus on what the user needs to do with the system
B. User requirements are for the CEO; Business requirements are for the customers
C. There is no difference
D. Business requirements are technical; User requirements are non-technical

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is a "JAD Session" (Joint Application Design)?

A. A solo coding marathon
B. A highly structured workshop where stakeholders and IT professionals collaborate to define requirements
C. A meeting to discuss the company's stock price
D. A training session for new interns

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is "Requirement Traceability"?

A. The ability to find who wrote a requirement
B. The ability to track a requirement from its origin through its development and testing
C. The speed at which requirements are gathered
D. The total number of pages in a BRD

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_004]
When is "Passive Observation" used?

A. When the analyst actively asks questions during the task
B. When the analyst watches the user without interrupting their workflow
C. When the analyst does the task for the user
D. When the user watches the analyst work

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Which technique is best for discovering "Unknown Unknowns" or requirements that stakeholders didn't realize they had?

A. Surveys
B. Job Shadowing/Observation
C. Document Analysis
D. Benchmarking

Q6 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is "Benchmarking"?

A. Designing a bench for the office
B. Comparing an organization’s processes or products against industry best practices or competitors
C. Calculating the average height of employees
D. Testing the strength of the server hardware

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_007]
In requirement gathering, what is a "Use Case"?

A. A box where the software is stored
B. A description of how a user interacts with a system to achieve a specific goal
C. A list of all hardware components
D. A legal case against the company

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is "Gap Analysis"?

A. Closing a physical gap in a server rack
B. The process of comparing the current state (As-Is) with the desired future state (To-Be) to identify requirements
C. Analyzing the age gap between employees
D. Finding missing rows in a SQL table

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_009]
How do you handle "Conflicting Requirements" from two different stakeholders?

A. Choose the requirement from the person with the higher salary
B. Delete both requirements
C. Facilitate a discussion to understand the underlying needs and find a consensus or compromise
D. Flip a coin

Correct Answer: C

Q10 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_010]

A. To show the internal code structure
B. To define the boundaries of the system and its interactions with external entities
C. To draw the company logo
D. To list the meeting attendees

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which of the following is a "Quality Attribute" (Non-functional)?

A. "The user must be able to log in"
B. "The system must be available 99.9% of the time"
C. "The user must be able to delete their account"
D. "The system must send an email notification"

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "Requirement Validation"?

A. Checking if the requirement is spelled correctly
B. Ensuring that the requirements accurately reflect the needs of the stakeholders
C. Ensuring the requirement is technically possible to build (Verification)
D. Counting the number of requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is "Requirements Creep" (Scope Creep)?

A. When requirements are gathered too slowly
B. Uncontrolled changes or continuous growth in project scope without adjustments to time or budget
C. When the analyst follows the user too closely during observation
D. When the BRD is too long

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_014]

A. Microsoft Word
B. Jira or Trello
C. Photoshop
D. Windows Media Player

Q15 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_015]

A. Specific, Measurable, Achievable, Relevant, Time-bound

C. Stakeholder, Management, Analyst, Resource, Team
D. Software, Model, Analysis, Requirement, Tool

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_016]

D. Deleting unused buttons

A. Interviews

D. Job Shadowing

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_018]

A. Writing the code

D. Preparing the final presentation

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What is the purpose of "Requirement Workshops"?

A. To fix computers
B. To bring together key stakeholders in a focused session to define and refine requirements
C. To teach employees how to type faster
D. To clean the office

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REQ_GATHERING_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is "Verification" in the context of requirements?

A. Checking if the stakeholders like the requirements
B. Checking if the requirements are technically sound and meet quality standards (e.g., no contradictions)
C. Translating requirements to JSON
D. Sending the BRD to the client

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "Requirements Traceability Matrix" (RTM)?

A. A matrix showing the cost of each requirement
B. A grid used to ensure that each requirement is linked to a business goal, design element, and test case
C. A list of all stakeholders and their phone numbers
D. A type of database table

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_002]
What is "Impact Analysis" in requirement management?

A. Measuring the impact of a computer on the desk
B. Assessing the consequences of changing or adding a requirement on the project's timeline, cost, and other requirements
C. Calculating the crash force of a hard drive
D. Evaluating the marketing impact of a new logo

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_003]
How does "Kano Modeling" help in requirement prioritization?

A. By sorting requirements alphabetically
B. By classifying requirements into categories like Basic, Performance, and Excitement based on customer satisfaction
C. By calculating the exact ROI of every line of code
D. By identifying which developer is the fastest

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Requirement Churn"?

A. When requirements are gathered very quickly
B. The frequency with which requirements change after they have been "finalized"
C. The process of deleting old requirements
D. A specific type of JAD session

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_005]
Which technique is used to prioritize requirements by assigning them to categories: Must have, Should have, Could have, and Won't have?

C. SWOT Analysis
D. Critical Path Method

Q6 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_006]

A. Drawing a map of the user's office

C. Mapping the GPS coordinates of users
D. A list of all user passwords

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_007]
What is the "Five Whys" technique used for in elicitation?

A. To ask five different people the same question
B. An iterative interrogative technique used to explore the cause-and-effect relationships underlying a problem (Root Cause)
C. To determine the five most important stakeholders
D. To list five reasons why the project will fail

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_008]
What is a "Non-functional Requirement" trade-off?

A. Deleting all non-functional requirements
B. The decision to prioritize one quality (e.g., Security) over another (e.g., Speed) when they conflict
C. Adding more functional requirements
D. Changing the project manager

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_009]
In a complex system, what is "NFR Elicitation"?

A. Gathering functional requirements
B. The specific process of identifying and defining constraints and quality attributes (Non-Functional Requirements)
C. Gathering only negative requirements
D. Eliciting requirements from new users only

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_010]

C. Hiring models for a commercial
D. Designing the user interface buttons

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Ambiguity Review"?

A. Reviewing requirements to ensure they are as vague as possible
B. A formal process where team members look for requirements that could be misunderstood or have multiple meanings
C. A review of the project's budget
D. A personality test for the analyst

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_012]

A. Deleting all requirements below a certain line
B. The process of establishing a fixed set of requirements that are agreed upon and can only be changed through formal control
C. Creating a base for the server
D. Writing requirements in a very simple font

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_013]
How do you elicit requirements for a "Legacy System" replacement?

A. By ignoring the old system
B. By reverse engineering the old system's functionality and interviewing users about its pain points
C. By asking the original developer who retired 20 years ago
D. By copying the source code of a competitor

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_014]

A. Finding the first person who joined the company
B. A method of problem-solving used for identifying the underlying causes of faults or problems
C. Calculating the square root of project costs
D. Analyzing the roots of trees on company property

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_015]

A. The salary of a stakeholder

C. The distance a stakeholder lives from the office
D. The stakeholder's technical knowledge

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_016]

C. Deleting the entire backlog
D. Writing the final user manual

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_017]

A. The list of requirements
B. A document that describes how requirements will be elicited, analyzed, documented, and managed throughout the project
C. The analyst's daily schedule
D. A plan to hire more analysts

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Functional Decomposition"?

A. When the software stops working
B. Breaking down complex business processes or functions into smaller, more detailed parts
C. Deleting old functions
D. Merging two systems into one

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Scenario-Based Elicitation"?

A. Writing a movie script
B. Using specific story-like descriptions of a system in use to help stakeholders identify requirements
C. Planning for a system crash scenario
D. Predicting the worst-case financial scenario

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REQ_GATHERING_ADVANCED_020]

## Skill 30: Requirement Analysis

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary objective of Requirement Analysis?

A. To write code for the software
B. To determine the exact needs of stakeholders and define system boundaries
C. To design the marketing strategy for the product
D. To manage the company's payroll system

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_002]
Which document typically serves as the foundation for all subsequent analysis and design?

A. Project Budget
B. Business Requirement Document (BRD)
C. User Manual
D. Weekly Status Report

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_003]
A requirement that describes what the system must do is called a:

A. Non-functional Requirement
B. Functional Requirement
C. Technical Requirement
D. UI Requirement

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_004]
A requirement that describes how the system should perform (e.g., speed or security) is called a:

A. Business Rule
B. Functional Requirement
C. Non-functional Requirement
D. Data Requirement

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_005]
What is "Gap Analysis" in the context of requirement analysis?

A. Measuring the distance between servers
B. Comparing the current state ("As-Is") with the desired future state ("To-Be")
C. Analyzing the age gap between users
D. Calculating the profit margin

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_006]
In requirement analysis, what does "Scope" define?

A. The total cost of the project
B. The list of employees involved
C. The boundaries of what is included and excluded in the project
D. The color of the application interface

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_007]
What is an "SRS" document?

A. System Resource Schedule
B. Software Requirement Specification
C. Standard Reporting System
D. Software Release Summary

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_008]
Which of the following is a characteristic of a good requirement?

A. Vague
B. Ambiguous
C. Testable
D. Long

Correct Answer: C

Q9 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_009]
A person or group with an interest or concern in the project is called a:

A. Competitor
B. Stakeholder
C. Developer
D. Intern

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_010]
What is the purpose of "Prioritization" in analysis?

A. To delete all requirements
B. To rank requirements based on importance and urgency
C. To finish the project in one day
D. To give everyone the same task

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_011]
"The system must allow users to reset their passwords." This is an example of:

A. A Functional Requirement
B. A Non-functional Requirement
C. A Design Constraint
D. A Bug

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_012]
"The system must load pages within 2 seconds." This is an example of:

A. A Functional Requirement
B. A Non-functional Requirement
C. A User Story
D. A Database Schema

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_013]
What does "In-Scope" mean?

A. Features that will be built in the current project
B. Features that might be built in 5 years
C. Features that are too expensive
D. Features requested by competitors

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_014]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q15 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_015]

B. Video players
C. Web browsers
D. Email clients

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_016]
What is a "User Story"?

A. A long technical manual
B. A short description of a feature from the perspective of an end user
C. A history of the company
D. A complaint from a customer

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_017]

A. New features to add

B. Business Analyst

Q19 [REQ_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_019]

D. It is already completed

D. A process used by competitors

Q10 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_010]

A. Add new features

D. Delete old code

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Requirement Reuse"?

A. Copying a requirement word-for-word for every project
B. Utilizing existing requirements from previous projects to save time and ensure consistency in a new context
C. Recycling old documents
D. Asking the user to repeat themselves

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_012]
"Scalability" is a non-functional requirement related to:

A. The color of the UI
B. The ability of the system to handle growth in users or data volume
C. The budget of the project
D. The number of developers

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_013]
A "State Machine Diagram" is most useful for:

A. Showing data flow
B. Modeling the lifecycle and transitions of an object (e.g., Order status: New -> Paid -> Shipped)
C. Calculating the project timeline
D. Listing user permissions

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_014]
What is a "Non-functional Trade-off"?

A. Deleting all non-functional requirements
B. Deciding between two conflicting qualities (e.g., High Security vs. High Speed)
C. Adding more functional requirements
D. Lowering the project cost

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_015]
"Maintainability" refers to:

A. The cost of the server
B. The ease with which a system can be modified to correct faults or improve performance
C. The speed of the login process
D. The number of years a system is used

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Domain Modeling"?

A. Buying a website domain
B. Identifying and representing the key entities and relationships within a specific business area
C. Creating a 3D model of the office
D. Writing code for the backend

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Requirement Management"?

A. Hiring a manager for the project
B. The process of documenting, tracking, and controlling requirements throughout the project lifecycle
C. Deleting requirements that are too hard
D. Presenting requirements to the CEO

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_018]
What is the "Stakeholder Matrix"?

A. A movie about requirements
B. A tool used to categorize stakeholders based on their influence and interest in the project
C. A list of all stakeholders' names
D. A database of user passwords

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_019]
"Feasibility Study" should be conducted:

A. After the project is finished
B. During or before the requirement analysis phase to ensure the project is viable
C. Only if the project has a high budget
D. By the marketing team

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REQ_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_020]
What is "Requirement Elicitation"?

A. The process of drawing out requirements from stakeholders
B. The process of deleting requirements
C. The process of writing code
/D. The process of testing software

Correct Answer: A

## Skill 31: Business Requirement Document (BRD)

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [BRD_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of a Business Requirement Document (BRD)?

A. To provide a detailed technical guide for developers
B. To describe the high-level business needs and what a solution should achieve
C. To list all the software bugs found during testing
D. To define the marketing budget for the project

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BRD_BEGINNER_002]
Who is the primary audience for a BRD?

A. Only the quality assurance team
B. Stakeholders, business managers, and project sponsors
C. Only the junior programmers
D. The company's competitors

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [BRD_BEGINNER_003]
Which section of the BRD provides a high-level overview of the project's purpose and goals?

A. Glossary
B. Executive Summary
C. Technical Specifications
D. Revision History

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BRD_BEGINNER_004]
In a BRD, what does "Project Scope" define?

A. The list of employees' salaries
B. The boundaries of the project, including what is included and what is excluded
C. The programming language to be used
D. The physical location of the server room

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BRD_BEGINNER_005]
What does the acronym "BRD" stand for?

A. Business Requirement Document
B. Binary Resource Data
/C. Business Report Details
D. Basic Requirement Description

Correct Answer: A

Q6 [BRD_BEGINNER_006]
True or False: A BRD should focus on "how" the system will be built rather than "what" the business needs.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q7 [BRD_BEGINNER_007]
Which of the following is typically found in a BRD?

A. Source code snippets
B. Business Objectives
C. Database schema diagrams
D. Unit test cases

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BRD_BEGINNER_008]
What is the purpose of the "Glossary" section in a BRD?

A. To list the names of all developers
B. To define technical or business terms and acronyms used in the document
C. To store the project's financial records
D. To list the document's references

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BRD_BEGINNER_009]
Who is usually responsible for writing and maintaining the BRD?

A. The lead developer
B. The Business Analyst
C. The HR manager
D. The end-user

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BRD_BEGINNER_010]
What does "Sign-off" mean in the context of a BRD?

A. Deleting the document
B. Formal approval and agreement by stakeholders that the requirements are correct
C. Sending the document via email
D. Printing the document

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BRD_BEGINNER_011]
Which section lists the specific problems the project intends to solve?

A. Scope
B. Problem Statement (or Background)
C. Functional Requirements
D. Appendix

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BRD_BEGINNER_012]
A BRD is created during which phase of the project lifecycle?

A. Testing phase
B. Initiation and Planning/Analysis phase
C. Deployment phase
D. Maintenance phase

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BRD_BEGINNER_013]
What is the difference between a BRD and a Functional Requirement Document (FRD)?

A. The BRD is technical; the FRD is business-oriented
B. The BRD describes "what" the business needs; the FRD describes "how" the system should function
C. There is no difference
D. The BRD is for testers; the FRD is for CEOs

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BRD_BEGINNER_014]
What is a "Business Objective"?

A. A specific result that a company aims to achieve within a timeframe
B. The name of the software
C. A list of hardware requirements
D. A bug report

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [BRD_BEGINNER_015]
In a BRD, "Stakeholders" are:

A. Only the software users
B. People or groups with an interest in the outcome of the project
C. Only the project managers
D. The shareholders of the company only

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BRD_BEGINNER_016]
Which section tracks changes made to the BRD over time?

A. Glossary
B. Revision History (or Version Control)
C. Scope
D. Summary

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BRD_BEGINNER_017]
"The system must be able to process 100 transactions per second." This is most likely a:

A. Business Goal
B. Requirement (specifically Non-functional/Performance)
C. Stakeholder Name
D. Project Budget

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BRD_BEGINNER_018]
What is an "Appendix" in a BRD?

A. The first page of the document
B. A section at the end for supplementary material like charts or research data
C. A list of all project meetings
D. The signature page

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BRD_BEGINNER_019]
True or False: Stakeholder expectations should be managed and documented in the BRD.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q20 [BRD_BEGINNER_020]
What happens if the BRD is not approved?

A. The project moves to the coding phase anyway
B. The requirements must be refined and revised until stakeholders agree
C. The project is immediately canceled
D. The Business Analyst is fired

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between an "Assumption" and a "Constraint" in a BRD?

A. Assumptions are facts; Constraints are guesses
B. Assumptions are factors believed to be true; Constraints are limitations imposed on the project
C. Constraints are for the budget; Assumptions are for the timeline
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_002]
Which section describes the current state of the business process before the project begins?

A. To-Be Process
B. As-Is Process
C. Future Scope
D. Executive Summary

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_003]
Why is "Requirement Prioritization" included in a BRD?

A. To make the document longer
B. To help the team understand which requirements are critical vs. nice-to-have
C. To decide which stakeholders to fire
D. To list the requirements in alphabetical order

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What are "Success Metrics" (or KPIs) in a BRD?

A. The number of pages in the BRD
B. Measurable values used to determine if the project has met its objectives
C. The names of the most successful employees
D. A list of all software tools used

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_005]
How does a BRD help in preventing "Scope Creep"?

A. By hiding requirements from the developers
B. By clearly defining what is "Out of Scope"
C. By increasing the budget automatically
D. By avoiding stakeholder meetings

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is a "Business Rule" in the context of a BRD?

A. A guideline for the office dress code
B. A statement that defines or constrains some aspect of the business logic (e.g., "Discount applies only to orders over $500")
C. The company's mission statement
D. A law passed by the government

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_007]
"Non-functional Requirements" in a BRD usually cover:

A. What the user clicks on
B. Performance, Security, Usability, and Reliability
C. The names of the marketing team
D. The daily schedule of the analyst

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is the purpose of a "Process Flow" diagram in a BRD?

A. To show the organizational chart
B. To visually represent the steps and logic of a business process
C. To draw the office layout
D. To show the database table relationships

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_009]
In a BRD, "In-Scope" items are:

A. Features that will be delivered in this project
B. Features that are too expensive to build
C. Features requested by competitors
D. Deleted requirements

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is "Data Requirement" in a BRD?

A. The size of the hard drive
B. Specification of what data needs to be captured, stored, or processed
C. A list of all user passwords
D. The speed of the internet connection

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Why is "Stakeholder Analysis" important before finalizing a BRD?

A. To see who has the highest salary
B. To ensure all relevant parties have provided their input and requirements
C. To assign tasks to the developers
D. To plan the office party

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What does "Traceability" mean in a BRD?

A. Following the analyst's footsteps
B. Linking requirements to business goals and eventually to design and testing
C. Finding the original author of the document
D. Counting the number of words in a sentence

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is a "User Persona" in a BRD?

A. The name of the CEO
B. A fictional character created to represent a user type that might use the system
C. A list of all employees in the company
D. The person who signs off the document

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_014]
Which section outlines the specific benefits the business expects to gain from the project?

A. Glossary
B. Business Value / Benefits
C. Revision History
D. Technical Specifications

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is "Gap Analysis" results doing in a BRD?

A. Showing the distance between two buildings
B. Highlighting the differences between current capabilities and future needs
C. Listing the missing rows in a spreadsheet
D. Calculating the age gap between users

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_016]
True or False: Technical architecture should be the main focus of a BRD.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q17 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is the role of "Acceptance Criteria" in a BRD?

A. To define how much the project will cost
B. To provide the specific conditions that must be met for a requirement to be considered satisfied
C. To list the hiring criteria for new staff
D. To describe the user's personality

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does the "Regulatory Requirements" section cover?

A. The company's internal rules
B. Compliance with laws, industry standards, or government regulations
C. The project manager's rules
D. The rules for using the office kitchen

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_019]
A "Risk" in a BRD refers to:

A. An event that has already happened
B. A potential future event that could negatively impact the project
C. A high-priority requirement
D. The chance of the analyst getting promoted

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BRD_INTERMEDIATE_020]
How should "Ambiguous" requirements be handled during BRD review?

A. Left as they are to save time
B. Clarified and rewritten to ensure a single, clear interpretation
C. Deleted from the document
D. Moved to the appendix

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [BRD_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "Requirements Traceability Matrix" (RTM) in relation to a BRD?

A. A tool to track the document's location
B. A mapping document that ensures every requirement in the BRD is addressed in design, development, and testing
C. A list of all project stakeholders
D. A chart showing the project's financial growth

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BRD_ADVANCED_002]
In a BRD, how do you handle "Conflicts" between requirements from different business units?

A. Prioritize the unit with the largest budget
B. Use a facilitation technique to negotiate a consensus and document the agreed-upon solution
C. Include both and let developers decide
D. Delete both requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [BRD_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Impact Analysis" for a BRD change request?

A. Measuring the impact of the document on the printer
B. Evaluating how a change to a requirement affects the project scope, cost, time, and other requirements
C. Calculating the physical impact of a hard drive crash
D. Assessing the marketing impact of the project

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BRD_ADVANCED_004]
How does a BRD differ in an "Agile" vs. "Waterfall" environment?

A. In Agile, the BRD is never created
B. In Waterfall, it's a fixed, comprehensive document; in Agile, it may be leaner or replaced by a living Product Backlog
C. Agile BRDs use only code; Waterfall BRDs use only text
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BRD_ADVANCED_005]
What is "Requirement Baselining"?

A. Deleting all requirements below a certain line
B. The process of establishing a fixed set of requirements that can only be changed through a formal change control process
C. Writing requirements in a very simple font
D. Setting the minimum number of requirements for a project

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BRD_ADVANCED_006]
What is "Functional Decomposition" in the context of requirement analysis for a BRD?

A. When the software stops working
B. Breaking down high-level business functions into smaller, more detailed sub-functions and requirements
C. Deleting old business units
D. Merging two companies

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BRD_ADVANCED_007]
How can "Feasibility Analysis" impact the BRD?

A. It cannot impact the BRD
B. It may lead to certain requirements being removed or modified if they are technically or financially impossible
C. It increases the number of pages in the BRD
D. It changes the project manager

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BRD_ADVANCED_008]
What is the "Verification" of a BRD?

A. Checking if stakeholders like the document
B. Ensuring the document is internally consistent, follows standards, and is technically sound
C. Translating the document to another language
D. Sending the document to the client

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BRD_ADVANCED_009]
What is the "Validation" of a BRD?

/A. Checking if the document is spelled correctly
B. Ensuring that the requirements documented actually meet the real business needs of the stakeholders
C. Counting the number of requirements
D. Getting the document notarized

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BRD_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Requirements Churn"?

A. The speed at which an analyst writes requirements
B. The percentage of requirements that change after the initial baseline
C. The process of deleting old documents
D. The total number of requirements in the backlog

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BRD_ADVANCED_011]
In a complex BRD, "System Interdependencies" refers to:

A. How the project team depends on each other
B. How the new system will interact with or depend on existing systems
C. The relationship between different departments
D. The link between the BRD and the budget

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BRD_ADVANCED_012]
What is "Context Diagramming" in a BRD?

A. Drawing the office environment
B. High-level visualization showing the system as a single process and its relationship with external entities
C. A chart showing the context of every meeting
D. A list of all user passwords

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BRD_ADVANCED_013]
"Scalability" and "Extensibility" are types of:

A. Functional Requirements
B. Non-functional Requirements (Architecture/Quality)
C. Business Objectives
D. Project Constraints

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BRD_ADVANCED_014]
What is a "Requirement Management Plan"?

A. A list of requirements
B. A document describing how requirements will be elicited, analyzed, documented, and managed throughout the lifecycle
C. The daily schedule of the Business Analyst
D. A plan to hire more analysts

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BRD_ADVANCED_015]
Why is "Business Value Alignment" critical in a BRD?

A. To make the document look professional
B. To ensure that every requirement directly contributes to the stated business goals and project ROI
C. To keep the project manager happy
D. To comply with HR rules

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BRD_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Entity Relationship Modeling" (ERM) in a BRD context?

A. Modeling the relationship between the analyst and the manager
B. Defining the high-level data entities and their relationships within the business domain
C. Creating a social network for the project
D. A type of project timeline

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BRD_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Gold Plating" and why should it be avoided in a BRD?

A. Making the document cover in gold
B. Adding unnecessary features not requested by stakeholders, which increases cost and time
C. Hiring only the most expensive consultants
D. Using very high-quality paper for reports

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BRD_ADVANCED_018]
What is "MoSCoW" prioritization in a BRD?

A. A method of ranking requirements as Must have, Should have, Could have, and Won't have
B. A way to translate the BRD into Russian
C. A meeting held in Moscow
D. A specific type of flow chart

Correct Answer: A

Q19 [BRD_ADVANCED_019]
What is the "Change Control Board" (CCB)?

A. A group that changes the office furniture
B. A formal group of stakeholders responsible for reviewing and approving/rejecting changes to the requirement baseline
C. The people who control the light switches
D. A software tool for versioning

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BRD_ADVANCED_020]
How can "User Story Mapping" be used in a BRD?

A. To draw a map of where the users live
B. To visually organize requirements into a logical sequence representing the user's journey or workflow
C. To track the GPS location of users
D. To list all the names of the users

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 32: Functional Requirement Document (FRD)

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [FRD_BEGINNER_001]
What does the acronym "FRD" stand for?

B. Functional Requirement Document
C. Formal Resource Description
D. Format Requirement Detail

Q2 [FRD_BEGINNER_002]
What is the primary purpose of an FRD?

A. To define the project's financial budget
B. To describe how a system must behave and what functions it must perform
C. To provide the actual source code to the client
D. To list the names and salaries of the developers

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [FRD_BEGINNER_003]
Who is the primary audience for an FRD?

A. Only the marketing department
B. Developers, testers, and technical stakeholders
C. Only the end-users
D. The company's legal team

Q4 [FRD_BEGINNER_004]

B. False

Q5 [FRD_BEGINNER_005]

B. Specific system inputs and outputs

D. Employee vacation policy

Q6 [FRD_BEGINNER_006]

C. How much the software costs
D. The speed of the hardware

Q7 [FRD_BEGINNER_007]

Q13 [FRD_BEGINNER_013]

Q14 [FRD_BEGINNER_014]

Q15 [FRD_BEGINNER_015]

Q16 [FRD_BEGINNER_016]

Q19 [FRD_BEGINNER_019]

Q20 [FRD_BEGINNER_020]

Q9 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What are "Data Requirements" within an FRD?

A. The weight of the data
B. Specification of the types, formats, and structures of data the system must handle
C. A list of all the user's personal files
D. The speed of the data transfer

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_010]
How does an FRD support the Quality Assurance (Testing) team?

A. It doesn't support them
B. It provides the basis for creating functional test cases and scripts
C. It lists the testers' work schedules
D. It replaces the need for testing

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is "Functional Decomposition" in the context of an FRD?

A. When the system stops working
B. Breaking down high-level functions into smaller, more specific sub-functions for clarity
C. Deleting old requirements
D. Merging two systems

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_012]
"User Role and Permissions" section in an FRD defines:

A. The salaries of the users
B. What different types of users can and cannot do within the system
C. The users' home addresses
D. The users' personal hobbies

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is the purpose of a "Screen Mockup" in an FRD?

A. To make the document look artistic
B. To provide a visual representation of the UI to clarify functional requirements
C. To mock the developers
D. To replace the actual software

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is a "Pre-condition" in a Use Case?

A. The state of the system after a process
B. The specific state the system must be in before a function can be executed
C. The weather on the day of development
D. The budget of the project

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is a "Post-condition" in a Use Case?

A. The state of the system before a process
B. The state the system will be in after the function has successfully finished
C. The feedback from the user
D. The final payment for the project

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_016]
True or False: Non-functional requirements (like performance or security) are often listed in a separate section of the FRD or an SRS.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q17 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is a "Business Rule" compared to a "Functional Requirement"?

A. They are identical
B. A Business Rule is a policy (e.g., "Must be 18"); a Functional Requirement is how the system enforces it (e.g., "System checks DOB field")
C. Business Rules are only for managers
D. Functional Requirements are only for coders

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does "Ambiguity" in an FRD lead to?

A. Faster development
B. Confusion and different interpretations of a requirement by developers and testers
C. Higher quality software
D. Lower project costs

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_019]
Which tool is commonly used to manage functional requirements in an iterative environment?

A. Microsoft Excel
B. Jira (Issue tracking and backlogs)
C. Adobe Photoshop
D. Windows Media Player

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [FRD_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is a "Workaround" in the context of system functions?

A. A path around the office
B. An alternative method to achieve a goal when a standard function is not available or working
C. A type of exercise for analysts
D. A way to avoid working

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [FRD_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "Requirements Traceability Matrix" (RTM) and its relationship to the FRD?

A. A list of everyone who read the FRD
B. A document that ensures every functional requirement in the FRD maps to a business goal and a test case
C. A database of user passwords
D. A chart showing the project's financial profit

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [FRD_ADVANCED_002]
How are "Complex Logical Workflows" typically documented in an advanced FRD?

A. Using plain text paragraphs only
B. Using state-transition diagrams or detailed activity diagrams (UML)
C. By writing the code directly in the document
D. By skipping the documentation and explaining it verbally

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [FRD_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Requirement Volatility"?

A. The speed of the server
B. The frequency and extent to which requirements change during the project lifecycle
C. The number of people writing the FRD
D. The file size of the FRD

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [FRD_ADVANCED_004]
In an FRD, how should "External API Integrations" be specified?

A. Just say "connect to API"
B. Specify the endpoints, request/response formats (JSON/XML), and error codes
C. List the API developer's phone number
D. Describe the API's marketing strategy

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [FRD_ADVANCED_005]
What is "Impact Analysis" for an FRD change request?

A. Measuring the physical impact of the document
B. Evaluating how a change to one function affects other functions, the database, and the project timeline
C. Calculating the crash force of a computer
D. Assessing the marketing impact of a new feature

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [FRD_ADVANCED_006]
What is a "System Constraint" in an FRD?

A. A new feature request
B. A technical or architectural limitation (e.g., "The system must be compatible with IE11")
C. An unlimited budget
D. A list of all system users

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [FRD_ADVANCED_007]
How do "Regression Testing" needs relate to the FRD?

A. They don't relate
B. The FRD provides the baseline functionality that must be re-tested when new functions are added to ensure nothing broke
C. Regression testing is only for bugs, not functions
D. The FRD is deleted after regression testing

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [FRD_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Data Mapping" in an advanced FRD?

A. Drawing a map of where data is stored physically
B. Defining how data moves from a source system to the target system's fields and tables
C. Sorting data alphabetically
D. Creating a visual chart of data growth

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [FRD_ADVANCED_009]
"The system must handle 10,000 concurrent requests without the response time exceeding 1 second." Where is this usually placed?

A. Functional section
B. Non-functional / Performance section of the FRD or SRS
C. The Glossary
D. The project introduction

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [FRD_ADVANCED_010]
What is "State Transition Modeling" in an FRD?

A. Changing the state of the office
B. Describing the different statuses an object can have (e.g., "Order: Pending, Shipped, Delivered") and the functions that trigger changes
C. Moving the server to another state
D. Tracking the analyst's mood

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [FRD_ADVANCED_011]
How can "User Story Mapping" enhance the creation of an FRD?

A. By drawing a map of the user's home
B. By visually organizing smaller functional requirements into a logical user journey or flow
C. By tracking the users via GPS
D. By listing all user names alphabetically

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [FRD_ADVANCED_012]
What is "Functional Verification"?

A. Checking if the users like the system
B. The process of ensuring the built system actually performs the functions exactly as specified in the FRD
C. Translating the FRD to a different language
D. Sending the FRD to the client for the first time

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [FRD_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Requirement Baselining"?

A. Deleting all requirements below a certain line
B. The process of formally agreeing on a set of functional requirements that can only be changed via a change control process
C. Writing requirements in a simple font
D. Setting the minimum number of requirements for a project

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [FRD_ADVANCED_014]
In a complex FRD, what is "Cross-Functional Dependency"?

A. When two departments don't like each other
B. When the completion or behavior of one system function depends on the outcome of another
C. The relationship between the software and the hardware
D. When the analyst depends on the project manager

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [FRD_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Requirement Churn" and why is it a risk for an FRD?

A. Writing requirements very fast
B. High rate of change in requirements, which can lead to project delays and "scope creep"
C. Deleting requirements by mistake
D. Having too many people involved in the FRD

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [FRD_ADVANCED_016]
"Audit Trail" functional requirements specify:

A. How to pay the company's taxes
B. How the system must record and store a history of user actions for security and compliance
C. A list of all project audits
D. The path the auditors take through the building

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [FRD_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Localization" vs "Internationalization" in an FRD?

A. They are the same thing
B. Internationalization is designing the system to support multiple languages; Localization is adapting it for a specific region
C. Localization is for local users only
D. Internationalization is for travel websites only

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [FRD_ADVANCED_018]
What is a "CRUD" matrix in an FRD?

A. A matrix for messy data
B. A table showing which user roles have permission to Create, Read, Update, or Delete specific data entities
C. A list of system errors
D. A specific type of bar chart

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [FRD_ADVANCED_019]
How do "Non-functional Trade-offs" affect the FRD?

A. They don't affect it
B. Decisions to prioritize one quality (e.g. Security) may limit certain functional possibilities (e.g. Speed of access)
C. They increase the project budget
D. They change the project manager

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [FRD_ADVANCED_020]
What is "Gap Analysis" between a Legacy System and a new FRD?

A. Measuring the distance between two servers
B. Identifying functional features in the old system that are missing or improved in the new FRD
C. Analyzing the age gap between developers
D. Finding missing rows in a SQL table

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 33: Software Requirement Specification (SRS)

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [SRS_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of a Software Requirement Specification (SRS) document?

A. To write the actual source code for the application
B. To provide a comprehensive description of the software system to be developed
C. To list the personal contact information of all developers
D. To define the marketing strategy for the next five years

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SRS_BEGINNER_002]
Which international standard is most commonly associated with the structure of an SRS?

A. ISO 9001
B. IEEE 830
C. IEEE 754
D. ISO 27001

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SRS_BEGINNER_003]
Which of the following is NOT typically a part of an SRS document?

A. Functional Requirements
B. System Architecture Design
C. Non-functional Requirements
D. Project Constraints

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SRS_BEGINNER_004]
In an SRS, a requirement that specifies a task the system must perform is called a:

A. Performance Requirement
B. Functional Requirement
C. Design Constraint
D. Security Requirement

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SRS_BEGINNER_005]
Requirements that describe system properties such as "99.9% uptime" or "Response time under 2 seconds" are:

A. Functional Requirements
B. Non-functional Requirements
C. Business Rules
D. External Interfaces

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SRS_BEGINNER_006]
Who is the primary audience for the SRS document?

A. Only the end-users
B. Customers, project managers, developers, and testers
C. The company's Human Resources department
D. Only the marketing team

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [SRS_BEGINNER_007]
What does the "Scope" section of an SRS define?

A. The total salary budget for the team
B. The software's capabilities and boundaries (what it will and won't do)
C. The physical dimensions of the server hardware
D. The color palette of the company logo

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SRS_BEGINNER_008]
Which section of the SRS describes how the software interacts with other software or hardware systems?

A. Internal Logic
B. External Interface Requirements
C. User Personas
D. Database Schema

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SRS_BEGINNER_009]
True or False: An SRS should focus on "what" the system should do rather than "how" it should be implemented technically.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q10 [SRS_BEGINNER_010]
What is the meaning of an "Ambiguous" requirement in an SRS?

A. A requirement that is very long
B. A requirement that can be interpreted in more than one way
C. A requirement that is written in code
D. A requirement that has already been completed

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SRS_BEGINNER_011]
Which of the following is a "User Requirement"?

A. The system shall use an Oracle database
B. The user shall be able to generate a monthly sales report
C. The code must be written in Python
D. The server must be hosted in the cloud

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SRS_BEGINNER_012]
What is the purpose of the "Glossary" in an SRS?

A. To list the names of the stakeholders
B. To define technical terms, acronyms, and abbreviations used in the document
C. To store the project's financial records
D. To list the software versions

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SRS_BEGINNER_013]
Which of these is a "Constraint" in an SRS?

A. The system must allow login via email
B. The system must be developed using the Java programming language
C. The system must support dark mode
D. The system must send notifications

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SRS_BEGINNER_014]
What does the acronym "SRS" stand for?

A. Software Resource Schedule
B. Software Requirement Specification
C. Standard Reporting System
D. System Release Summary

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SRS_BEGINNER_015]
A requirement that states "The system must be able to handle 1,000 users simultaneously" is a:

A. Functional Requirement
B. Performance Requirement (Non-functional)
C. Business Objective
D. Security Policy

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SRS_BEGINNER_016]
Which phase of the Software Development Life Cycle (SDLC) typically results in the creation of the SRS?

A. Design Phase
B. Requirement Analysis Phase
C. Testing Phase
D. Implementation Phase

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SRS_BEGINNER_017]
True or False: The SRS document acts as a legal agreement between the customer and the developer.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q18 [SRS_BEGINNER_018]
What is the purpose of "Sign-off" on an SRS?

A. To delete the document
B. To formally indicate that stakeholders agree with the documented requirements
C. To send the document to the printer
D. To change the document's font

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SRS_BEGINNER_019]
Which of the following describes a "Verifiable" requirement?

A. It is written in a beautiful font
B. It can be checked through a specific test or observation
C. It is agreed upon by at least ten people
D. It is written by the CEO

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SRS_BEGINNER_020]
What is a "Business Rule" in an SRS context?

A. The office dress code
B. A policy that dictates how a business operation is performed (e.g., "Discount applies only to orders over $500")
C. The project manager's work schedule
D. A rule about using the company car

Correct Answer: B
