### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What makes an SRS "Traceable"?

A. The document is easy to find on the server
B. Each requirement has a unique identifier and can be linked to its source and its implementation
C. The document is printed on traceable paper
D. The author's name is on every page

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_002]
Which of the following is a characteristic of a "Complete" SRS?

A. It has at least 500 pages
B. It contains all requirements requested by stakeholders and defines all system responses to inputs
C. It includes the source code for the main features
D. it is written in multiple languages

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_003]
How does an SRS differ from a BRD (Business Requirement Document)?

A. The SRS is higher level than the BRD
B. The BRD focuses on business goals; the SRS focuses on technical functional and non-functional specifications
C. The SRS is for management; the BRD is for developers
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_004]
A "Use Case" included in an SRS typically contains:

A. The budget for the feature
B. Actors, Pre-conditions, Post-conditions, and a Flow of Events
C. The programming logic in C++
D. A list of hardware vendors

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Why should an SRS be "Modifiable"?

A. So that anyone can change requirements at any time without notice
B. To allow for easy updates while maintaining a consistent structure and history
C. Because requirements should never be finalized
D. To reduce the file size of the document

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_006]
Which of the following is an example of a "Security Requirement" in an SRS?

A. The system shall use an 8-character minimum password with complexity rules
B. The system shall be blue
C. The system shall calculate tax
D. The system shall be hosted on AWS

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is "Requirement Validation" in the context of an SRS?

A. Checking the spelling and grammar
B. Ensuring the documented requirements accurately reflect the user's needs
C. Checking if the code is running correctly
D. Determining the cost of development

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is "Requirement Verification" in the context of an SRS?

A. Checking if the customer likes the requirements
B. Ensuring the SRS meets quality standards and is internally consistent and feasible
C. Asking the manager for a signature
D. Publishing the document on a website

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_009]
"The system must be compatible with both Windows 10 and macOS." This is an example of:

A. A Functional Requirement
B. An Availability Requirement
C. A Portability / Compatibility Requirement
D. A User Story

Correct Answer: C

Q10 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is the "Change Control Process" for an SRS?

A. A way to stop all changes to the document
B. A formal procedure for requesting, reviewing, and approving changes to the baseline requirements
C. A keyboard shortcut to edit the text
D. A method for deleting old versions

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_011]
In an SRS, what is "Prioritization"?

A. Doing the easiest tasks first
B. Categorizing requirements (e.g., Must have, Should have) to guide development sequence
C. Putting the CEO's requirements at the top of every page
D. Deleting requirements that take too long to write

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_012]
Which tool is commonly used to manage and version control an SRS in a modern development environment?

A. Microsoft Paint
B. Jira, Confluence, or Doors
C. VLC Media Player
D. Windows Calculator

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is the purpose of an "Entity Relationship Diagram" (ERD) in or attached to an SRS?

A. To show the organizational chart
B. To model the data structure and relationships between different entities in the system
C. To draw the user interface
D. To list the project team members

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What are "Acceptance Criteria" in an SRS?

A. The criteria for hiring the Business Analyst
B. Specific conditions that must be met for a requirement to be considered successfully implemented
C. The total number of pages allowed in the document
D. The minimum budget required to start the project

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_015]
A requirement stating "The system shall provide a user manual in PDF format" is a:

A. Functional Requirement
B. Documentation Requirement
C. Design Constraint
D. Performance Requirement

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What does "Requirement Volatility" refer to?

A. The speed of the server
B. The tendency for requirements to change frequently during the project
C. The number of errors in the document
D. The file format of the SRS

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_017]
Which section of the SRS describes the "User Classes and Characteristics"?

A. Product Functions
B. General Description
C. System Features
D. Glossary

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is a "Data Dictionary" in an SRS?

A. A list of all project managers
B. A centralized repository of information about data (meanings, relationships, origin, usage, and format)
C. A translation of the SRS into other languages
D. A database password list

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_019]
True or False: An SRS should include every possible technical detail, including variable names and specific algorithms.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q20 [SRS_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is the "Baseline" of an SRS?

A. The bottom margin of the document
B. The version of the SRS that has been formally reviewed and agreed upon
C. The first draft of the document
D. The lowest priority requirement

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [SRS_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "Requirements Traceability Matrix" (RTM) and its link to the SRS?

A. A tool used to find the author of the SRS
B. A document that maps SRS requirements to business goals, design components, and test cases
C. A database used to store the SRS source code
D. A chart showing the project's financial profit

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SRS_ADVANCED_002]
In an advanced SRS, how are "Conflicts" between requirements handled?

A. The Analyst picks their favorite
B. Through a formal negotiation process with stakeholders to reach a consensus, which is then documented
C. By including both and letting the developers choose
D. By deleting both requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SRS_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Impact Analysis" for a change request to an SRS?

A. Measuring the physical weight of the document
B. Assessing the consequences of a change on the project scope, schedule, budget, and other requirements
C. Calculating the crash force of a hard drive
D. Evaluating the marketing impact of the software logo

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SRS_ADVANCED_004]
How does the role of an SRS change in an "Agile" vs. "Waterfall" methodology?

A. In Agile, the SRS is never written
B. In Waterfall, it is a fixed baseline; in Agile, it is often a "living document" or replaced by a Product Backlog
C. Agile SRS uses only code; Waterfall SRS uses only text
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SRS_ADVANCED_005]
What is "Functional Decomposition" in the context of SRS analysis?

A. When the software stops working
B. The process of breaking down high-level system functions into smaller, more manageable sub-functions
C. Deleting old requirements
D. Merging two different software products

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SRS_ADVANCED_006]
Which of the following is a "Reliability" metric in an SRS?

A. Mean Time Between Failures (MTBF)
B. Number of lines of code
C. Number of pages in the manual
D. Font size of the user interface

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [SRS_ADVANCED_007]
How should "External API" integrations be specified in an SRS?

A. Just write "connect to API"
B. Detail the endpoints, request/response formats, authentication methods, and error handling
C. List the API developer's home address
D. Describe the API's marketing strategy

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SRS_ADVANCED_008]
What is a "Constraint" vs. a "Requirement" in an SRS?

A. They are identical
B. A requirement is a desired function; a constraint is a restriction on the solution (e.g., regulatory compliance)
C. A constraint is for the budget; a requirement is for the code
D. A constraint is a bug; a requirement is a feature

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SRS_ADVANCED_009]
In a complex system, what is "NFR Quantification"?

A. Writing more functional requirements
B. Turning vague non-functional requirements (e.g., "Fast") into measurable metrics (e.g., "Process 500 requests/sec")
C. Counting the number of stakeholders
D. Calculating the total number of words in the SRS

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [SRS_ADVANCED_010]
What is the purpose of "Requirement Elicitation" in relation to the SRS?

A. The process of deleting requirements
B. The process of gathering, discovering, and drawing out requirements from stakeholders
C. The process of writing code
D. The process of software testing

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SRS_ADVANCED_011]
How can "User Story Mapping" assist in developing an SRS?

A. By drawing a map of the users' offices
B. By visually organizing features into a sequence that represents the user's journey, which informs the SRS structure
C. By tracking the users via GPS
D. By listing user names alphabetically

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SRS_ADVANCED_012]
What is "Requirement Churn" and why is it problematic for an SRS?

A. Writing requirements very fast
B. High rate of change in requirements after the baseline, leading to scope creep and project delays
C. Deleting requirements by mistake
D. Having too many people involved in the SRS

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SRS_ADVANCED_013]
"Scalability" requirements in an SRS define:

A. The color of the UI
B. The system's ability to handle increasing amounts of work or its potential to be enlarged to accommodate that growth
C. The project's budget
D. The number of developers on the team

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SRS_ADVANCED_014]
What is "State Machine Modeling" used for in an SRS?

A. Modeling the physical state of the server
B. Describing the different states of a system or object and the transitions between them based on events
C. Moving the server to another state
D. Tracking the analyst's workflow

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SRS_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Derived Requirement"?

A. A requirement that is copied from a competitor
B. A requirement that is not explicitly stated but is necessary to satisfy a higher-level requirement
C. A requirement that is deleted
D. A requirement that is written in a different language

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SRS_ADVANCED_016]
What does "Regulatory Compliance" in an SRS entail?

A. Following the company's internal rules
B. Ensuring the software meets laws and standards like GDPR, HIPAA, or PCI-DSS
C. Following the lead developer's rules
D. The rules for using the office kitchen

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SRS_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Feasibility Study" impact on SRS?

A. It has no impact
B. It identifies requirements that are technically or financially impossible, leading to SRS adjustments
C. It increases the number of pages in the SRS
D. It changes the project manager

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [SRS_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Requirement Attribute" (e.g., Status, Priority, Risk)?

A. A description of the analyst's personality
B. Metadata attached to each requirement to help manage and track it throughout the lifecycle
C. A list of software features
D. A type of database index

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SRS_ADVANCED_019]
What is a "Formal Review" (Inspection) of an SRS?

A. A casual conversation over coffee
B. A rigorous, structured meeting where peers and stakeholders examine the SRS for defects
C. Checking the SRS for viruses
D. A meeting to discuss the project budget

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SRS_ADVANCED_020]
How do "System Interdependencies" affect an SRS?

A. They don't affect it
B. They require the SRS to specify how the new system will rely on or impact existing systems
C. They increase the project budget
D. They change the project manager

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 34: User Stories

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_001]
What is a User Story?

A. A detailed technical specification for developers
B. A short, simple description of a feature told from the perspective of the person who desires the new capability
C. A legal document describing the contract between a client and a vendor
D. A fictional narrative about how a company was founded

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_002]
Which of the following is the most common standard template for writing a User Story?

A. When [event], I want to [action], so that [outcome]
B. As a [persona], I want [action], so that [value]
C. The system shall [function] for the [user]
D. If [condition], then [result] for [user]

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_003]
In the template "As a [role], I want to [goal], so that [benefit]", which part explains the "Why" or the value of the story?

A. As a [role]
B. I want to [goal]
C. So that [benefit]
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_004]
True or False: User Stories are meant to replace all detailed documentation in a project.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q5 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_005]
What are "Acceptance Criteria" in a User Story?

A. The list of people who approved the story
B. The specific conditions that must be met for the story to be considered complete and working
C. The programming language used to build the story
D. The total cost of developing the feature

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_006]
Who is the primary focus of a User Story?

A. The Developer
B. The Database Administrator
C. The End User or Customer
D. The Project Manager

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_007]
Where are User Stories typically stored and managed in an Agile environment?

A. In a locked physical safe
B. In the Product Backlog
C. In the company's annual financial report
D. In the source code comments

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_008]
What does the "I" in the INVEST acronym for high-quality User Stories stand for?

A. Intelligent
B. Independent
C. Immediate
D. Internal

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_009]
What are the "Three Cs" of User Stories?

A. Code, Control, Completion
B. Card, Conversation, Confirmation
C. Cost, Customer, Category
D. Create, Change, Cancel

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: A User Story should be written in highly technical language so that developers understand it immediately.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q11 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_011]
What is a "Persona" in the context of User Stories?

A. A fictional character representing a specific type of user for the system
B. The real name of the lead developer
C. A software bug that behaves like a human
D. A type of database index

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_012]
Which part of the "Three Cs" refers to the Acceptance Criteria?

A. Card
B. Conversation
C. Confirmation
D. None of the above

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_013]
What is the primary benefit of using User Stories?

A. They ensure developers never have to talk to users
B. They shift the focus from writing about requirements to talking about them
C. They make the project 100% cheaper
D. They eliminate the need for testing

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_014]
Which of the following is a "Goal" in a User Story?

A. As a Registered User
B. I want to reset my password
C. So that I can regain access to my account
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_015]
True or False: A User Story is a promise for a future conversation.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q16 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_016]
Which of these is a better User Story "Role"?

A. The System
B. A frequent traveler
C. Database Table
D. Java Class

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_017]
When should Acceptance Criteria be defined?

A. After the code is written
B. Before the story is started (during refinement or planning)
C. Only if the story fails the first test
D. After the project is finished

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_018]
What is the "Card" in the Three Cs?

A. A credit card for project expenses
B. A physical or digital medium representing the story's basic description
C. A greeting card for the customer
D. A graphic card for the server

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_019]
If a User Story is too large to fit into a single Sprint, it is often called:

A. A Saga
B. An Epic
C. A Legend
D. A Novel

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [USER_STORIES_BEGINNER_020]
Can a User Story have more than one Acceptance Criterion?

A. Yes, usually it has several to cover different scenarios
B. No, it must only have one to stay simple
C. Only if the Product Owner is not present
D. Only for technical stories

Correct Answer: A

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What does the "INVEST" acronym stand for?

A. Integrated, Notable, Valuable, Effective, Small, Timely
B. Independent, Negotiable, Valuable, Estimable, Small, Testable
C. Internal, Necessary, Verifiable, Essential, Secure, Trackable
D. Important, New, Vital, Easy, Simple, Tested

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_002]
Why is "Negotiable" an important characteristic of a User Story?

A. Because the customer should be able to bargain for a lower price
B. Because it is not a rigid contract; the details should be co-created during the conversation
C. Because developers should be able to ignore parts they don't like
D. Because the deadline is always moving

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is the "Given-When-Then" format often used for?

A. Writing the User Story headline
B. Writing Acceptance Criteria (Behavior Driven Development)
C. Calculating the project budget
D. Naming database columns

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What is "Backlog Refinement" (Grooming)?

A. Deleting the entire backlog
B. The process where the team reviews stories to ensure they are clear, estimated, and prioritized
C. A performance review for the Business Analyst
D. Designing the user interface in Photoshop

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is "Sizing" or "Estimating" a User Story?

A. Measuring the physical size of the story card
B. Determining the relative effort or complexity of a story, often using Story Points
C. Counting the number of characters in the story text
D. Deciding how many people can read the story at once

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_006]
A Business Analyst notices a story is "Dependent" on three other stories. Which INVEST principle is being violated?

A. Independent
B. Negotiable
C. Estimable
D. Testable

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is the purpose of "Splitting" a User Story?

A. To assign half of the story to one developer and half to another
B. To break down a large story (Epic) into smaller, manageable stories that fit in a Sprint
C. To delete the "Why" part of the story
D. To move the story to a different project

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_008]
Which of the following is a common technique for estimating User Stories?

A. Measuring lines of code
B. Planning Poker (using Fibonacci sequence)
C. Calculating the manager's weight
D. Random number generation

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_009]
In the "Given-When-Then" format, what does "Given" represent?

A. The action the user takes
B. The initial context or state of the system
C. The expected outcome
D. The name of the user

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What makes a User Story "Small" according to INVEST?

A. It uses a small font
B. It can be completed within a single iteration/Sprint
C. it has fewer than 10 words
D. It only involves one database table

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_011]
True or False: The Business Analyst is the only person allowed to write User Stories.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q12 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What happens if a story is not "Testable"?

A. It becomes a bug automatically
B. It is impossible to know when the story is truly "Done"
C. The developers will build it faster
D. It is moved to the "Non-functional" list

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_013]
"As a user, I want a fast website, so that I don't wait." Why is this a poor User Story?

A. The role is too generic
B. The goal is a non-functional requirement that isn't specific or easily testable
C. The value is obvious
D. Both A and B

Correct Answer: D

Q14 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is the "Definition of Ready"?

A. When the developer is ready for coffee
B. A set of criteria (like INVEST) that a story must meet before it can be pulled into a Sprint
C. When the computer is turned on
D. When the customer pays for the feature

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is a "Spike" story?

A. A story that is very short
B. A time-boxed research task used to gather information or test a technical approach
C. A story about high-heeled shoes
D. A story that was deleted accidentally

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_016]
Which role is ultimately responsible for prioritizing User Stories in the Product Backlog?

A. Scrum Master
B. Developers
C. Product Owner
D. Business Analyst

Correct Answer: C

Q17 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_017]
How do User Stories differ from traditional System Requirements?

A. Stories focus on the user's need; Requirements focus on system behavior
B. Stories are shorter
C. Stories encourage verbal communication over written documentation
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q18 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is a "Technical Story"?

A. A story about a robot
B. A story that describes infrastructure or architectural work needed to support user-facing features
C. A story written in binary code
D. A story for the CEO only

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_019]
If a story is "Estimable," it means:

A. We know exactly how many seconds it will take
B. The team has enough information to provide a relative estimate of effort
C. The story is already finished
D. The story has a fixed price in dollars

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [USER_STORIES_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is the primary role of the Business Analyst during User Story refinement?

A. To tell the developers exactly how to code
B. To facilitate communication between stakeholders and the team to clarify requirements and criteria
C. To decide the Sprint deadline
D. To take meeting minutes only

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_001]
What is "User Story Mapping"?

A. A tool to track the GPS location of users
B. A collaborative exercise that arranges stories along a horizontal axis (user journey) and vertical axis (priority/detail)
C. A way to draw the company's organizational chart
D. A method for naming database tables

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_002]
What is "Vertical Slicing" in the context of User Stories?

A. Dividing a story by architectural layers (e.g., UI story, DB story)
B. Creating stories that deliver a small piece of end-to-end functionality across all layers (UI, Logic, DB)
C. Cutting the story card in half
D. Assigning stories to different departments

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_003]
Which of the following is a "Story Splitting" pattern by "Business Rule"?

A. Splitting by the type of data entered
B. Splitting a complex calculation into "Standard calculation" and "Edge case handling"
C. Splitting by browser type
D. Splitting by user persona

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_004]
How should "Non-functional Requirements" (like Security or Performance) be handled in User Stories?

A. They should be ignored
B. As constraints in the Definition of Done or as specific Acceptance Criteria for relevant stories
C. As a single story called "Make it work well"
D. They should be written in a separate BRD only

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_005]
What is a "User Story Anti-pattern"?

A. A story that is very successful
B. A common but ineffective way of writing or using stories (e.g., stories that are too technical or have no value)
C. A template for writing stories
D. A type of automated test

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_006]
What is the "MoSCoW" method used for in User Story management?

A. Translation
B. Prioritization (Must have, Should have, Could have, Won't have)
C. Estimation
D. Story Splitting

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_007]
In Story Mapping, the horizontal row representing the main steps of the user's workflow is called the:

A. Backbone
B. Ribs
C. Skull
D. Feet

Correct Answer: A

Q8 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_008]
When splitting a story by "Happy Path" vs "Error Paths", you are focusing on:

A. Success scenarios first, then handling exceptions in separate stories
B. The most expensive features first
C. The stories with the most words
D. The stories requested by the manager

Correct Answer: A

Q9 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_009]
What is "Horizontal Slicing" and why is it usually avoided?

A. Splitting by UI/Backend/DB; it's avoided because it doesn't deliver standalone business value to the user
B. Splitting by priority; it's avoided because it's too slow
C. Splitting by person; it's avoided because it causes conflict
D. There is no such thing as horizontal slicing

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_010]
What is the "Cone of Uncertainty" related to User Story estimation?

A. The fact that estimates become more accurate as the team learns more over time
B. A visual for a weather app
C. The fear of estimating incorrectly
D. The total number of stories in the backlog

Correct Answer: A

Q11 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_011]
A story that reads "As a Developer, I want to optimize the SQL queries, so the DB runs faster" is:

A. A perfect User Story
B. A Technical Debt/Enabler story (focuses on the 'How' or internal quality)
C. A Persona story
D. An Epic

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_012]
What is the risk of "Story Inflation" (constantly increasing story point estimates)?

A. It makes the team look faster than they are
B. It breaks the "Estimable" principle
C. It distorts velocity and makes long-term planning unreliable
D. Both A and C

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Theme" in User Story hierarchy?

A. The color scheme of the board
B. A collection of related Epics
C. The name of the Product Owner
D. A single sentence at the top of a card

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_014]
How can "Impact Mapping" help a Business Analyst?

A. By identifying the goals, actors, and impacts that lead to the creation of the right User Stories
B. By drawing a map of the building
C. By calculating the impact of a server crash
D. By listing user salaries

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_015]
When a story is "Negotiable," it means the team can:

A. Change the Sprint deadline
B. Negotiate the scope and implementation details with the Product Owner to fit the timebox
C. Negotiate their own salary
D. Decide not to do the story at all without asking anyone

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Relative Estimation"?

A. Estimating in exact hours and minutes
B. Comparing stories against each other to determine which is larger or smaller (e.g., this is twice as big as that)
C. Asking your relatives for their opinion
D. Estimating based on the current date

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_017]
A "Walking Skeleton" in Agile is:

A. A Halloween decoration
/B. A minimal end-to-end implementation (a set of stories) that covers the main architectural components
C. A developer who works too much
D. A story that has been archived

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Gold Plating" in a User Story context?

A. Making the card out of gold
B. Adding extra functionality that wasn't requested or doesn't add value to the end user
C. Hiring premium testers
D. Using high-quality fonts

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_019]
Why should "Testable" criteria be objective rather than subjective?

A. To make it easier for the analyst to write
B. So that there is no argument between developers and testers about whether the criterion is met
C. To reduce the file size
D. Because Agile requires objective math

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [USER_STORIES_ADVANCED_020]
What is the relationship between "User Stories" and "Use Cases" in complex systems?

A. They are the same thing
B. Use cases often provide the detailed functional context from which multiple User Stories can be derived
C. Stories are for software; Use Cases are for hardware
D. Stories are for Agile; Use Cases are for Waterfall (no exceptions)

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 35: Use Cases

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_001]
What is a Use Case?

A. A detailed piece of programming code
B. A description of how a system interacts with external users to achieve a specific goal
C. A legal document used in court
D. A list of all hardware components in a server

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_002]
Who or what is an "Actor" in a Use Case?

A. A famous person hired for marketing
B. A person, organization, or external system that interacts with the system
C. The lead developer of the project
D. A bug in the software

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_003]
What does the "System Boundary" represent in a Use Case diagram?

A. The physical wall of the office
B. The scope of the system being developed, separating it from the actors
C. The internet firewall
D. The end of the document

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_004]
Which of the following is a "Primary Actor"?

A. The actor who initiates the interaction to achieve a goal
B. An actor who only receives reports
C. The system database
D. The project manager

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_005]
True or False: Use Cases are primarily represented using visual diagrams and narrative text.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q6 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_006]
What is the "Goal" of a Use Case?

A. To finish the project on time
B. The specific outcome the primary actor wants to achieve
C. To reduce the cost of development
D. To use as many actors as possible

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_007]
In a Use Case diagram, how is an Actor typically represented?

A. A square box
B. A stick figure
C. A circle
D. A dashed line

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_008]
In a Use Case diagram, how is the Use Case itself represented?

A. A stick figure
B. An oval or ellipse
C. A rectangle
D. A diamond shape

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_009]
What is the "Basic Flow" (or Happy Path)?

A. The process of deleting a use case
B. The most common, error-free sequence of steps to achieve the goal
C. The list of all technical errors
D. The background color of the diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: Use Cases should describe *how* the system works internally (e.g., specific database triggers).

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q11 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_011]
Which of the following is an example of a Use Case title?

A. Java Database Connection
B. Withdraw Cash
C. Pentium i7 Processor
D. $500 Budget

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_012]
What does a "Secondary Actor" (or Supporting Actor) do?

A. Initiates the Use Case
B. Provides a service to the system or receives information from it
C. Writes the Use Case narrative
D. Fixes bugs found in the Use Case

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_013]
A Use Case is a tool used primarily in which phase?

A. Software Deployment
B. Requirements Analysis
C. Hardware Maintenance
D. Marketing Research

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_014]
What is the difference between a User Story and a Use Case?

A. There is no difference
B. User Stories are short and focus on value; Use Cases are more detailed and focus on interaction steps
C. Use Cases are only for Waterfall; User Stories are only for Agile
D. User Stories are for machines; Use Cases are for humans

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_015]
What is a "Brief Use Case"?

A. A Use Case that is only one sentence long
B. A high-level summary of the Use Case, usually a single paragraph
C. A Use Case with no actors
D. A Use Case written in code

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_016]
What is the "Subject" of a Use Case?

A. The person reading the document
B. The system or subsystem to which the use cases apply
C. The programming language used
D. The email subject line

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_017]
Can an external software system be an Actor?

A. Yes
B. No

Correct Answer: A — Yes

Q18 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_018]
What does an "Association" line in a diagram represent?

A. A conflict between two people
B. Communication or interaction between an actor and a use case
C. The speed of the system
D. A link to a website

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_019]
What is the "Main Success Scenario"?

A. When the project is sold for a high price
B. Another term for the Basic Flow or Happy Path
C. The list of all secondary actors
D. The conclusion of the user manual

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [USE_CASES_BEGINNER_020]
Why are Use Cases valuable for Business Analysts?

A. They help identify and document functional requirements from a user perspective
B. They automatically generate code
C. They replace the need for stakeholders
D. They manage the project budget

Correct Answer: A

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is an "Alternative Flow" in a Use Case?

A. A flow that happens on a different computer
B. A sequence of steps that handles a variation or a common deviation from the basic flow
C. The process of restarting the server
D. A flow used only by developers

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is an "Exception Flow"?

A. A flow that describes an error condition and how the system handles it
B. A flow for the CEO only
C. A flow that deletes the database
D. A flow with no steps

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_003]
In UML, what does the "<<include>>" relationship mean?

A. The base use case *might* use the behavior of the included use case
B. The base use case *always* incorporates the behavior of the included use case to complete its task
C. The included use case is optional
D. The included use case is a bug fix

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_004]
In UML, what does the "<<extend>>" relationship mean?

A. The base use case always uses the extending use case
B. The extending use case is optional and only happens under specific conditions
C. The system is expanding its memory
D. One actor is replacing another

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is a "Pre-condition"?

A. The state of the system after the Use Case finishes
B. The specific state the system must be in before the Use Case can begin
C. The temperature of the server room
D. The cost of the Use Case

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is a "Post-condition"?

A. The state the system must be in before starting
B. The state of the system after the Use Case has successfully completed
C. A condition that causes an error
D. A list of actors

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is "Actor Generalization"?

A. Giving all actors the same name
B. A relationship where a specialized actor inherits the roles and use cases of a more general actor
C. Deleting unnecessary actors
D. Using actors from a different company

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is an "Extension Point"?

A. A physical plug on the computer
B. A specific point in a base use case where an extending use case can be inserted
C. The last step of the basic flow
D. A way to add more pages to the document

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_009]
How do you document "Business Rules" within a Use Case?

A. They are usually listed in a separate section or referenced within the flow steps
B. They are written in the code comments
C. They are ignored in Use Case documentation
D. They are only mentioned in the budget

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is a "Fully Dressed" Use Case?

A. A Use Case with a lot of images
B. A highly detailed Use Case that includes all possible flows, conditions, and metadata
C. A Use Case approved by the legal department
D. A Use Case used in a presentation

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which of the following is a valid "Post-condition" for a "Login" Use Case?

A. User is on the login page
B. User is authenticated and redirected to the dashboard
C. User has a computer
D. Internet is working

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_012]
If a Use Case "Process Payment" is used by both "Order Online" and "Pay Bill", it is likely an:

A. Extended use case
B. Included use case
C. Alternative flow
D. Exception flow

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is the purpose of "Scoping" Use Cases?

A. To define which interactions are part of the system and which are outside
B. To choose the font size
C. To count the number of developers
D. To translate the document

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_014]
True or False: A single Actor can participate in multiple Use Cases.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q15 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_015]
True or False: A single Use Case can have multiple Actors.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q16 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is "Scenario Analysis"?

A. Analyzing the lighting of the room
B. Examining specific instances of Use Case execution to find all possible paths and outcomes
C. Predicting the stock market
D. Testing the hardware speed

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_017]
Which relationship is better for handling optional complexity without cluttering the basic flow?

A. Include
B. Extend
C. Association
D. Generalization

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is a "Trigger" in a Use Case?

A. A button on the keyboard
B. The event that causes the Use Case to start
C. An error that stops the system
D. The last line of the basic flow

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What are "Non-functional Requirements" in the context of Use Cases?

A. They are usually documented as supplemental constraints attached to the Use Case (e.g., performance)
B. They are ignored
C. They are written as steps in the basic flow
D. They are only for the database administrator

Correct Answer: A

Q20 [USE_CASES_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is "Use Case Slicing"?

A. Cutting the diagram into pieces
B. Breaking a large Use Case into smaller, implementable pieces for Agile Sprints
C. Deleting alternative flows
D. Removing actors from the diagram

Correct Answer: B

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_001]
What is the difference between a "Business Use Case" and a "System Use Case"?

A. Business Use Case describes a business process; System Use Case describes a software interaction
B. System Use Case is for hardware; Business is for software
C. There is no difference
D. Business Use Case is for managers; System is for developers

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_002]
What is "Use Case Realization"?

A. Realizing that Use Cases are hard to write
/B. Describing how a Use Case is implemented within the design model using collaborating objects
C. Finishing the Use Case document
D. Presenting the Use Case to the client

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_003]
In complex systems, what is a "Use Case Package"?

A. A ZIP file containing documents
B. A grouping of related use cases to manage complexity and organization
C. A box of software CDs
D. A set of hardware components

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_004]
What is "CRUD" analysis in Use Case modeling?

A. Analyzing messy data
B. Ensuring that for every entity, there are Use Cases to Create, Read, Update, and Delete it
C. A method for finding bugs
D. A specific type of actor

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_005]
How does "Use Case Prioritization" impact project planning?

A. It determines the order in which features are developed, often based on risk or value
B. It helps choose the programming language
C. It determines the office seating chart
D. It calculates the final profit

Correct Answer: A

Q6 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_006]
What is a "Abstract Use Case"?

A. A Use Case with no title
B. A Use Case that is never executed directly but provides common behavior for other Use Cases
C. A Use Case about art
D. A Use Case that has been deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_007]
What is the risk of "Use Case Over-specification"?

A. Making the document too short
B. Including too much technical detail (implementation bias), which limits design flexibility
C. Using too many colors in the diagram
D. Having too many actors

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Misuse Case"?

A. A Use Case with spelling errors
B. A Use Case from the perspective of a hostile actor (e.g., a hacker) to identify security requirements
C. A Use Case that was used for the wrong project
D. A Use Case that nobody reads

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_009]
What is "Impact Analysis" for a Use Case change?

A. Measuring the impact of the document on the printer
B. Evaluating how a change to one Use Case affects other Use Cases, actors, and the system design
C. Calculating the speed of the system
D. Assessing the marketing impact of the feature

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_010]
In a large project, who maintains the "Use Case Model"?

A. The Lead Developer
B. The Business Analyst or Requirements Engineer
C. The Database Administrator
D. The End User

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_011]
What is the relationship between "Use Case Points" and estimation?

A. Use Case Points is a method to estimate project size and effort based on the complexity of Use Cases
B. It is the number of dots in a diagram
C. It is a reward system for analysts
D. It is the number of pages in the document

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_012]
How can "State Machine Diagrams" complement Use Cases?

A. By showing the background colors
B. By providing a detailed view of the internal state changes of a system object during a Use Case
C. By replacing the stick figures
D. By listing user salaries

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Goal-Level" in Use Case modeling (e.g., Cockburn's levels)?

A. The height of the analyst
B. The categorization of Use Cases into levels like Summary, User-Goal, or Subfunction
C. The number of steps in a flow
D. The priority of the requirement

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_014]
What is a "Concrete Use Case"?

A. A Use Case about construction
B. A Use Case that can be initiated by an actor and represents a complete goal
C. A Use Case written on a wall
D. A Use Case that never changes

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_015]
What is the purpose of a "Use Case Narrative" compared to a "Use Case Diagram"?

A. The narrative provides the textual detail and logic; the diagram provides the high-level visual relationships
B. The narrative is for managers; the diagram is for developers
C. The narrative is longer
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Verification" of a Use Case model?

A. Checking if the client likes the Use Cases
B. Ensuring the model is internally consistent, follows UML standards, and is technically correct
C. Translating the Use Cases into code
D. Deleting duplicate Use Cases

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_017]
What is "Validation" of a Use Case model?

A. Checking if the stick figures are drawn correctly
B. Ensuring the Use Cases accurately capture the actual needs and goals of the business stakeholders
C. Printing the document
D. Checking for spelling errors

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Implementation Bias" in a Use Case?

A. When the analyst prefers one developer over another
B. When the Use Case specifies *how* a technology will be used rather than *what* the system must do
C. When the Use Case is only written in English
D. When the Use Case is too long

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_019]
How should "System Interdependencies" be handled in Use Case modeling?

A. By ignoring other systems
B. By representing external systems as Actors and documenting the interfaces in the flows
C. By combining all systems into one Use Case
D. By using a different diagram for every system

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [USE_CASES_ADVANCED_020]
What is the "Use Case Survey"?

A. A questionnaire for actors
B. A summary document listing all Use Cases and their brief descriptions for a project
C. A test for analysts
D. A marketing survey

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 36: Process Mapping

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary definition of Process Mapping?

A. Writing a detailed technical manual for a database
B. A visual representation of a sequence of actions that results in a specific outcome
C. A list of all employees and their contact numbers
D. Creating a marketing plan for a new software product

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_002]
Which symbol is most commonly used in a flowchart to represent a "Decision" point?

A. Rectangle
B. Oval
C. Diamond
D. Circle

Correct Answer: C

Q3 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_003]
What does a "Rectangle" symbol typically represent in a process map?

A. The start or end of a process
B. A decision point
C. A specific task, action, or step
D. A delay in the system

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_004]
Which symbol is used to represent the "Start" or "End" of a process?

A. Triangle
B. Oval (or Rounded Rectangle)
C. Square
D. Star

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_005]
What is an "As-Is" process map?

A. A map of how the process will look in the future
B. A map of how the process currently operates
C. A map of a competitor's process
D. A map of a process that was deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_006]
What is a "To-Be" process map?

A. A map of how the process currently operates
B. A map of the proposed or future-state process after improvements
C. A historical record of the process
D. A list of requirements for a new system

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_007]
In a process map, what do "Arrows" represent?

A. The direction of flow and the sequence of steps
B. The speed of the process
C. The importance of a task
D. The cost of a decision

Correct Answer: A

Q8 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_008]
What is the purpose of "Swimlanes" in a cross-functional flowchart?

A. To show the water temperature
B. To distinguish between different departments, roles, or actors involved in the process
C. To highlight the most expensive steps
D. To list the hardware requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_009]
What is a "Bottleneck"?

A. The cap of a bottle
B. A point in the process where the flow is restricted or slowed down
C. A successful completion of a task
D. A decision that leads to two different paths

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: Process mapping is only useful for IT projects.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q11 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_011]
What does "Redundancy" mean in a process?

A. A step that is critical for safety
B. Unnecessary repetition of a task or step
C. A step that uses 3D graphics
D. The final step of a process

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_012]
Which of the following is a common tool used for Process Mapping?

A. Microsoft Excel
B. Microsoft Visio
C. Adobe Photoshop
D. VLC Media Player

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_013]
What is the "Input" of a process?

A. The final result given to the customer
B. The information, materials, or triggers that start the process
C. The person who manages the process
D. An error that occurs during a step

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_014]
What is the "Output" of a process?

A. The starting point
B. The final result or product delivered at the end of the process
C. The computer used to draw the map
D. A meeting held to discuss the process

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_015]
A high-level process map that shows only the major steps is often called a:

A. Detailed Flowchart
B. Level 1 Map (or Value Chain)
C. Code diagram
D. Database Schema

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_016]
What does "Scope" refer to in process mapping?

A. The brand of camera used to film the process
B. The boundaries and extent of the process being mapped
C. The number of colors used in the diagram
D. The salary of the process owner

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_017]
Which term describes a step that adds no value to the final output?

A. Value-added step
B. Waste (or Non-value-added)
C. Critical path
D. Primary task

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_018]
What is the standard direction for reading a process map?

A. Bottom-to-top
B. Left-to-right and Top-to-bottom
C. Randomly
D. Right-to-left only

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_019]
True or False: Stakeholders should be involved in validating the accuracy of a process map.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q20 [PROCESS_MAPPING_BEGINNER_020]
What is a "Process Owner"?

A. The person who draws the map
B. The person responsible for the performance and improvement of the process
C. The customer who buys the product
D. The CEO of the software company

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What does the acronym SIPOC stand for?

A. Simple, Input, Process, Output, Control
B. Supplier, Input, Process, Output, Customer
C. System, Integration, Program, Order, Client
D. Software, Interface, Path, Output, Category

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_002]
When is it most appropriate to use a "Sub-process" (or child process) block?

A. When the map is too colorful
B. To simplify a high-level map by grouping detailed steps into a single reusable component
C. When a decision has only one outcome
D. To hide errors from management

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_003]

A. By looking at the space between shapes
B. By finding where the current "As-Is" process fails to meet the desired "To-Be" business objectives
C. By counting the number of decision diamonds
D. By checking the internet connection

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_004]
In a "To-Be" map, why is it important to minimize the number of "Hand-offs"?

A. Hand-offs increase the beauty of the chart
B. Hand-offs between roles or departments often introduce delays and increase the risk of errors
C. Hand-offs require too much paper to print
D. Developers don't like hand-offs

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is "Cycle Time" in process analysis?

A. The time it takes to cycle a bicycle
B. The total time from the start of a process to the end for a single unit of work
C. The time the analyst spends drawing the map
D. The interval between two team meetings

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_006]
Which of the following describes a "Non-value-added but necessary" step?

A. Checking a customer's ID for legal compliance
B. Entering the same data into two different systems
C. Moving a file from one folder to another for no reason
D. Printing a report that no one reads

Correct Answer: A

Q7 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_007]

B. An informal, undocumented process that employees use instead of the official one
C. The outline of a shape in Visio
D. A backup process for the server

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_008]
When creating a cross-functional flowchart, vertical swimlanes usually represent:

A. Time periods
B. Functional areas, departments, or individual job roles
C. System versions
D. Cost categories

Q9 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_009]

C. It replaces the need for a BRD
D. It lists all the bugs in the code

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_010]

A. By hiding the problem steps
B. By visually tracing back the flow to identify where an error or defect originated
C. By assigning blame to the process owner
D. By deleting the steps that caused the error

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_011]

C. Reading the code only
D. Benchmarking

Q12 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_012]

C. An optional step
D. A deleted path

Q13 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_013]

A. The total cost of the process
B. The number of units of work passing through a process in a given period of time

D. The number of swimlanes in a diagram

Q14 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_014]

D. The speed of the lead developer

Q15 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_015]

D. To draw a 3D model of the office

Q16 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_016]

C. To increase the word count

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_017]

Q18 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_018]

Q19 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_019]

D. A football term used in Scrum

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [PROCESS_MAPPING_INTERMEDIATE_020]

Q2 [PROCESS_MAPPING_ADVANCED_002]

## Skill 37: Business Process Modeling

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPM_BEGINNER_001]

D. To track the daily attendance of employees

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BPM_BEGINNER_002]

B. Business Process Model and Notation

Q3 [BPM_BEGINNER_003]

Q11 [BPM_BEGINNER_011]

Q12 [BPM_BEGINNER_012]

Q14 [BPM_BEGINNER_014]

C. It reduces the need for a project manager
D. It speeds up the computer's processor

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BPM_BEGINNER_015]
A task that cannot be broken down into further detail is called:

A. An Atomic Task
B. A Sub-process
C. A Gateway
D. A Data Store

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [BPM_BEGINNER_016]
What is the "Context" of a business process?

A. The internal code logic
B. The environment and boundaries within which the process operates
C. The font size of the labels
D. The office furniture layout

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BPM_BEGINNER_017]
Which role is typically responsible for creating business process models?

A. Software Developer
B. Business Analyst
C. Security Guard
D. Database Administrator

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BPM_BEGINNER_018]
What does a "Dashed Line" (Message Flow) represent in BPMN?

A. A very fast task
B. Communication between two different Pools (participants)
C. An error in the sequence
D. A deleted activity

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BPM_BEGINNER_019]
True or False: Business Process Modeling can help identify manual tasks that can be automated.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q20 [BPM_BEGINNER_020]
What is a "Flowchart" in relation to Business Process Modeling?

A. A specific type of 3D visualization
B. A basic precursor to more complex modeling notations like BPMN
C. A list of employee salaries
D. A tool for editing videos

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between an "Exclusive Gateway" (X) and a "Parallel Gateway" (+)?

A. Exclusive allows only one path; Parallel allows all outgoing paths simultaneously
B. Parallel is for managers; Exclusive is for staff
C. Exclusive is faster than Parallel
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What does an "Inclusive Gateway" (O) signify?

A. One or more paths can be taken based on conditions
B. Only the first path is taken
C. All paths must be taken regardless of conditions
D. The process stops at this point

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is a "Sub-process"?

A. A process that is less important than others
B. An activity that contains its own internal process model
C. A process used by the HR department only
D. A task that has been automated

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_004]
In BPMN, what is an "Intermediate Event" (double-lined circle)?

A. The start of the process
B. The end of the process
C. An event that occurs during the course of a process
D. An error that deletes the model

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is "Gap Analysis" in process modeling?

A. Identifying the distance between two lanes
B. Comparing the As-Is and To-Be models to identify what needs to change
C. Counting the number of gateways
D. Checking for missing arrows

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is a "User Task" in BPMN?

A. A task performed by a software robot
B. A task performed by a human user with the assistance of a software application
C. A task that involves writing code
D. A task that is completed automatically

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What is a "Service Task"?

A. A task performed by a customer service agent
B. A task where the process uses a service, such as a Web service or automated application
C. A task that requires physical labor
D. A task that is optional

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is an "Artifact" in a process model?

A. A historical piece of software
B. Graphical elements like Groups or Text Annotations that provide extra information without affecting flow
C. A bug in the system
D. A physical document used in the office

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_009]
How do you represent a "Data Store" (Database) in BPMN?

A. A circle
B. A cylinder-shaped icon
C. A square
D. A cloud

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is "Token Flow" in the context of process simulation?

A. The speed of the internet
B. A theoretical concept used to trace the execution path through a process model
C. A type of cryptocurrency
D. The order of employee shifts

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which gateway is used when the decision is based on an external event (like a message) rather than data?

A. Exclusive Gateway
B. Event-Based Gateway
C. Parallel Gateway
D. Complex Gateway

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What does "BPR" (Business Process Re-engineering) involve?

A. Minor changes to a process
B. Fundamental rethinking and radical redesign of business processes
C. Hiring more engineers
D. Changing the company's logo

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is a "Manual Task" in BPMN?

A. A task that requires a user manual
B. A task performed without the aid of any business process execution engine or application
C. A task performed by the analyst
D. A task that is written by hand

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is the purpose of a "Boundary Event" attached to a task?

A. To define the edge of the paper
B. To handle exceptions or interruptions that occur while the task is running (e.g., a timeout)
C. To start a new lane
D. To change the task's color

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_015]
In BPMN, what does a "Message Event" (envelope icon) represent?

A. A standard company email
B. The receipt or sending of a communication from/to an external participant
C. A deleted comment
D. A notification about a software update

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is "Process Validation" in modeling?

A. Checking if the model follows the technical rules of the notation (e.g., no dead ends)
B. Checking if the manager likes the model
C. Printing the model for a meeting
D. Translating the model into code

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is a "Loop" in a process model?

A. A circle in the drawing
B. A sequence of activities that repeats until a specific condition is met
C. A mistake in the logic
D. A connection between two pools

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is "Throughput" in process analysis?

A. The total cost of the process
B. The rate at which a system or process produces its products or services
C. The number of lanes in a pool
D. The speed of the analyst's mouse

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What is the benefit of "Standardization" in business processes?

A. It makes every employee look the same
B. It reduces variability, improves quality, and makes processes easier to manage and scale
C. It allows anyone to change the process at any time
D. It increases the number of gateways

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BPM_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What does "Cycle Time" measure?

A. The time taken to draw a circle
B. The total time from the start to the end of a single process instance
C. The time between two project meetings
D. The age of the process

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPM_ADVANCED_001]

A. A dance routine for the team
B. A type of model that focuses on the interactions between participants rather than internal steps
C. A way to animate the process model
D. A method for hiring new staff

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BPM_ADVANCED_002]
What is the difference between "Orchestration" and "Choreography" in BPM?

A. Orchestration is internal to one participant; Choreography is the global interaction between participants
B. Choreography is for 3D; Orchestration is for 2D
C. Orchestration uses SQL; Choreography uses Java
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [BPM_ADVANCED_003]
What is a "Compensation Event" in BPMN?

A. A reward for finishing a task

C. A salary increase
D. A type of gateway

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BPM_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Process Simulation"?

A. A video game about the process
B. Running a model through software to predict performance, bottlenecks, and costs based on data

D. Creating a 3D model of the office

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BPM_ADVANCED_005]
In BPMN, what is a "Non-Interrupting Boundary Event"?

D. An event that deletes the task

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BPM_ADVANCED_006]

Q7 [BPM_ADVANCED_007]
What is a "Complex Gateway"?

A. A gateway with many arrows

Q8 [BPM_ADVANCED_008]

D. The project manager's schedule

Q9 [BPM_ADVANCED_009]

A. Digging for data in a mine

D. A type of cryptocurrency mining

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BPM_ADVANCED_010]

D. A model used by executives only

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BPM_ADVANCED_011]

A. A light on the dashboard

D. The end of a process

Q12 [BPM_ADVANCED_012]

Q13 [BPM_ADVANCED_013]

A. By running the process faster

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BPM_ADVANCED_014]

Q16 [BPM_ADVANCED_016]

Q17 [BPM_ADVANCED_017]

C. A deleted message
D. A message used for errors only

Q18 [BPM_ADVANCED_018]

Q19 [BPM_ADVANCED_019]

## Skill 38: Gap Analysis

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary definition of Gap Analysis in business?

A. A method to measure the physical distance between two offices
B. A technique to compare the actual performance with the desired performance
C. A process for deleting old customer records
D. A tool for designing company logos

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_002]
In Gap Analysis, what does the "As-Is" state represent?

A. The desired future goals
B. The current state of the business or process
C. A process that has been canceled
D. The state of a competitor’s business

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_003]

A. The historical record of the company
B. The desired future state or target goal
C. The current daily operations
D. A list of software bugs

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_004]
What is the "Gap" in a Gap Analysis?

A. The total number of employees
B. The difference between the current state and the desired future state
C. The physical space in a database
D. The time it takes to finish a meeting

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_005]

A. Implementing a solution

C. Calculating the final profit
D. Hiring new developers

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_006]

B. False

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_008]

C. An employee’s vacation request

Q9 [GAP_ANALYSIS_BEGINNER_009]

B. 1000 units

B. By deleting old data automatically
C. By designing a better logo
D. By managing the employee payroll

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_014]
What is a "Cultural Gap"?

A. Difference in language between employees
B. The misalignment between the organization’s current culture and the culture required to succeed in the future state
C. The number of holidays in a year
D. The difference in food preferences in the office

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Variance Analysis" in relation to Gap Analysis?

A. Analyzing the variation in the analyst's salary
B. Quantifying the difference between planned and actual performance (usually in financials)
C. Calculating the square root of the gap
D. Measuring the spread of data points

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_016]
Which framework looks at "Business, Data, Application, and Technology" to find gaps?

A. TOGAF (Enterprise Architecture)
B. Scrum
C. Six Sigma
D. Waterfall

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_017]
"Closing the information gap" involves:

A. Deleting top-secret files
B. Ensuring decision-makers have the right data at the right time
C. Buying a new server
D. Hiring more secretaries

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Supply Chain Gap Analysis"?

A. Analyzing the distance between suppliers
B. Identifying inefficiencies and risks in the flow of goods and services from source to customer
C. Counting the number of delivery trucks
D. Predicting the price of raw materials

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Scenario Planning" in Gap Analysis?

A. Writing a movie script
B. Defining multiple possible "To-Be" states based on different external conditions
C. Planning the office party
D. Calculating the project's worst-case budget

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [GAP_ANALYSIS_ADVANCED_020]
The "Service Quality Gap Model" (SERVQUAL) identifies gaps between:

A. Customer expectations and management perceptions
B. The price of a service and its cost
C. Two different software vendors
D. The speed of two different networks

Correct Answer: A

## Skill 39: SWOT Analysis

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [SWOT_BEGINNER_001]
What does the acronym SWOT stand for?

A. Systems, Works, Operations, Tools
B. Strengths, Weaknesses, Opportunities, Threats
C. Strategy, Workforce, Objectives, Tactics
D. Sales, Weekly, Orders, Targets

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SWOT_BEGINNER_002]
Which components of SWOT analysis are considered internal factors?

A. Opportunities and Threats
B. Strengths and Opportunities
C. Strengths and Weaknesses
D. Weaknesses and Threats

Correct Answer: C

Q3 [SWOT_BEGINNER_003]
Which components of SWOT analysis are considered external factors?

A. Strengths and Weaknesses
B. Opportunities and Threats
C. Strengths and Opportunities
D. Weaknesses and Threats

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SWOT_BEGINNER_004]
In a SWOT analysis, a "Strength" is best described as:

A. A favorable external trend
B. Something an organization is good at or a characteristic that gives it an advantage
C. A potential risk in the market
D. A lack of resources within the team

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SWOT_BEGINNER_005]
In a SWOT analysis, a "Weakness" is best described as:

A. An external competitor's success
B. An internal factor that places the organization at a disadvantage
C. A new government regulation
*D. An untapped market segment*

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SWOT_BEGINNER_006]
Which of the following is an example of an "Opportunity"?

A. High employee turnover
B. A well-known brand name
C. Emergence of new technology that the company can use
D. Inefficient production processes

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [SWOT_BEGINNER_007]
Which of the following is an example of a "Threat"?

A. Patents owned by the company
B. Entering a new geographic market
C. Economic recession affecting consumer spending
D. Specialized technical expertise of the staff

Correct Answer: C

Q8 [SWOT_BEGINNER_008]
What is the primary purpose of conducting a SWOT analysis?

A. To calculate the exact net profit of a company
B. To identify internal and external factors that can impact a project or business
C. To design the technical architecture of a database
D. To track the daily attendance of employees

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SWOT_BEGINNER_009]
True or False: SWOT analysis should only be performed by the CEO of a company.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q10 [SWOT_BEGINNER_010]
How is a SWOT analysis typically visualized?

A. A 2x2 grid/matrix
B. A pie chart
C. A Gantt chart
D. A scatter plot

Correct Answer: A

Q11 [SWOT_BEGINNER_011]
Which quadrant of the SWOT matrix represents internal helpful factors?

A. Weaknesses
B. Opportunities
C. Strengths
D. Threats

Correct Answer: C

Q12 [SWOT_BEGINNER_012]
Which quadrant of the SWOT matrix represents external harmful factors?

A. Weaknesses
B. Threats
C. Opportunities
D. Strengths

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SWOT_BEGINNER_013]
"Limited budget for marketing" would most likely be categorized as a:

A. Strength
B. Weakness
C. Opportunity
D. Threat

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SWOT_BEGINNER_014]
"Excellent customer service ratings" would most likely be categorized as a:

A. Strength
B. Weakness
C. Opportunity
D. Threat

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [SWOT_BEGINNER_015]
True or False: An "Opportunity" is something that has already happened and is fully utilized.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q16 [SWOT_BEGINNER_016]
Which of the following is the best time to perform a SWOT analysis?

A. Only after a project fails
B. During the strategic planning phase of a project
C. Every single day at 9 AM
D. Only when a new intern joins the team

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SWOT_BEGINNER_017]
"A new competitor entering the market" is a:

A. Weakness
B. Threat
C. Opportunity
D. Strength

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [SWOT_BEGINNER_018]
"Access to a unique distribution network" is an example of a:

A. Threat
B. Weakness
C. Strength
D. Opportunity

Correct Answer: C

Q19 [SWOT_BEGINNER_019]
True or False: SWOT analysis can be applied to individuals as well as organizations.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q20 [SWOT_BEGINNER_020]
What is the "O" in SWOT?

A. Operation
B. Organization
C. Opportunity
D. Objective

Correct Answer: C

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is a common pitfall when conducting a SWOT analysis?

A. Making the list too short
B. Being subjective rather than objective/data-driven
C. Involving too many stakeholders
D. Using a 2x2 grid

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_002]
How can a "Strength" be turned into an "Opportunity"?

A. By ignoring the strength
B. By leveraging that internal strength to exploit a gap in the external market
C. By making the strength a weakness
D. It is not possible to link the two

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is the difference between a "Weakness" and a "Threat"?

A. Weakness is external; Threat is internal
B. Weakness is an internal deficiency; Threat is an external challenge
C. They are exactly the same
D. A weakness is always more dangerous than a threat

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_004]
Which of the following is a "Matching" strategy in SWOT?

A. Using a weakness to stop a threat
B. Using a strength to take advantage of an opportunity
C. Ignoring opportunities to focus on strengths
D. Turning a threat into a strength directly

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Why is it important to prioritize items within each SWOT category?

A. To make the report look cleaner
B. To focus management attention on the most impactful factors
C. To delete the items at the bottom of the list
D. Because the SWOT matrix only allows 3 items per box

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_006]
"Changing consumer preferences towards healthier food" is an example of what for a fast-food chain?

A. Internal Weakness
B. External Threat (or Opportunity depending on adaptation)
C. Internal Strength
D. External Strength

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_007]
A company discovers its main competitor has a pending patent expiration. This is an:

A. Internal Strength
B. External Opportunity
C. Internal Weakness
D. External Threat

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What does a "Converting" strategy in SWOT involve?

A. Converting strengths into weaknesses
B. Converting weaknesses or threats into strengths or opportunities
C. Converting currency for international business
D. Converting the SWOT analysis into a PDF

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_009]
True or False: A SWOT analysis should be static and never updated once the project starts.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q10 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_010]
Which of the following data sources is most useful for identifying "Threats"?

A. Internal employee surveys
B. Competitive intelligence and market research reports
C. Company financial statements from 5 years ago
D. The company's own mission statement

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_011]
If a company has "High Production Costs" compared to the industry average, this is a:

A. Strength
B. Weakness
C. Opportunity
D. Threat

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_012]
"Strategic Fit" occurs when:

A. The SWOT analysis is finished on time
B. An organization's internal strengths match the external opportunities
C. All employees agree on the weaknesses
D. The threats are completely eliminated

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_013]
Which of these is a "Defensive" strategy derived from SWOT?

A. Expanding into new markets
B. Minimizing weaknesses to avoid external threats
C. Increasing the R&D budget
D. Hiring more sales staff

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_014]
True or False: SWOT analysis provides a clear plan of action automatically.

A. True
B. False (It provides the analysis, but strategy must be formulated from it)

Correct Answer: B — False

Q15 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_015]
"Proprietary technology" is categorized as a:

A. Strength
B. Weakness
C. Opportunity
D. Threat

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is the "External Factor Evaluation" (EFE) matrix's relationship to SWOT?

A. It replaces SWOT
B. It is a tool used to quantify and further analyze the Opportunities and Threats
C. It is used to analyze the Strengths and Weaknesses
D. It is a type of bar chart

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_017]
"Poor location of a retail store" is a:

A. Weakness
B. Threat
C. Opportunity
D. Strength

Correct Answer: A

Q18 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_018]
How does PESTLE analysis complement SWOT?

A. PESTLE provides the internal strengths
B. PESTLE provides a structured way to identify the external Opportunities and Threats
C. PESTLE is for small companies; SWOT is for large ones
D. They cannot be used together

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_019]
A "Vulnerability" in SWOT terms is often defined as:

A. A strength that is not being used
B. An external threat that targets an internal weakness
C. A weakness that is easily fixed
D. An opportunity that is too expensive

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SWOT_INTERMEDIATE_020]
Which of the following should a Business Analyst avoid when documenting a SWOT?

A. Specificity
B. Generalizations and vague statements
C. Actionable insights
D. Citations of data sources

Correct Answer: B
