### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [SWOT_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "TOWS Matrix"?

A. A typo for SWOT
B. An advanced version of SWOT that focuses on generating strategic options by combining internal and external factors
C. A matrix used only for financial auditing
D. A method for tracking time on a project

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [SWOT_ADVANCED_002]
In a TOWS Matrix, a "SO" strategy (Strengths-Opportunities) aims to:

A. Use strengths to overcome threats
B. Use internal strengths to capitalize on external opportunities
C. Overcome weaknesses by taking advantage of opportunities
D. Minimize weaknesses and avoid threats

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [SWOT_ADVANCED_003]
In a TOWS Matrix, what is a "WT" strategy (Weaknesses-Threats)?

A. An aggressive growth strategy
B. A defensive strategy aimed at reducing internal weaknesses and avoiding external threats
C. A strategy to hire new talent
D. A strategy to ignore the market

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [SWOT_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Dynamic SWOT Analysis"?

A. Analysis performed while running
B. An approach that considers how SWOT factors change over time rather than looking at a static snapshot
C. A SWOT analysis with 3D graphics
D. A SWOT analysis performed by an AI

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [SWOT_ADVANCED_005]
Which framework is best used to identify "Strengths" and "Weaknesses" by evaluating resources and capabilities?

A. PESTLE
B. VRIO (Value, Rarity, Imitability, Organization)
C. Porter’s 5 Forces
D. BCG Matrix

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [SWOT_ADVANCED_006]
How can "Confirmation Bias" negatively impact a SWOT session?

A. By making the session too fast
B. Participants may only look for data that supports their existing beliefs about the company's strengths
C. By requiring a signature for approval
D. By focusing too much on the threats

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [SWOT_ADVANCED_007]
A "ST" strategy (Strengths-Threats) in the TOWS matrix involves:

A. Using internal strengths to minimize the impact of external threats
B. Turning threats into strengths
C. Deleting the threats
D. Investing in new opportunities

Correct Answer: A

Q8 [SWOT_ADVANCED_008]
What is the "Internal Factor Evaluation" (IFE) matrix?

A. A list of employee birthdays
B. A strategic management tool used to audit and evaluate major strengths and weaknesses in functional areas
C. A type of database table
D. A diagram of the office layout

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [SWOT_ADVANCED_009]
In a complex SWOT, what does "Weighting" involve?

A. Measuring the physical weight of the document
B. Assigning a numerical value to each factor to indicate its relative importance to the business's success
C. Hiring heavy-weight consultants
D. Sorting the list by font size

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [SWOT_ADVANCED_010]
What is the "Power-Interest Grid"'s relationship to SWOT?

A. It replaces SWOT
B. It is used to identify which stakeholders should provide input for the SWOT
C. It is a type of electricity meter
D. It is used to color the SWOT matrix

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [SWOT_ADVANCED_011]
How does a Business Analyst handle "Conflicting Perspectives" during a SWOT workshop?

A. Only record the perspective of the manager
B. Document the different viewpoints and seek evidence-based consensus or perform further research
C. Delete the conflicting items
D. Pick the perspective that is easiest to write

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [SWOT_ADVANCED_012]
A "WO" strategy (Weaknesses-Opportunities) in TOWS focuses on:

A. Ignoring weaknesses
B. Improving internal weaknesses by using external opportunities
C. Selling the company
D. Fighting competitors directly

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [SWOT_ADVANCED_013]
What is "SWOT-C"?

A. SWOT for Chemists
B. SWOT with an added focus on "Challenges" (often merging threats and weaknesses)
C. SWOT performed in C++
D. A version of SWOT used only in Canada

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [SWOT_ADVANCED_014]
How can "Porter's Five Forces" inform a SWOT analysis?

A. It provides internal strengths
B. It helps identify external "Threats" by analyzing industry competition and supplier/buyer power
C. It lists the 5 people who should perform the SWOT
D. It is a set of 5 different SWOT matrices

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [SWOT_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Blind Spot" in the context of SWOT analysis?

A. A part of the eye
B. A critical weakness or threat that the organization is unaware of or chooses to ignore
C. A hidden opportunity
D. A typo in the SWOT matrix

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [SWOT_ADVANCED_016]
Which of the following is a quantitative extension of SWOT?

A. Decision Tree Analysis
B. SPACE Matrix (Strategic Position and Action Evaluation)
C. Fishbone Diagram
D. User Persona

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [SWOT_ADVANCED_017]
"Strategic Alignment" is achieved when:

A. The SWOT matrix is perfectly square
B. Business strategies are developed that leverage Strengths to exploit Opportunities while mitigating Weaknesses and Threats
C. All SWOT items are written in the same font
D. The project is under budget

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [SWOT_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Cross-Impact Analysis" in SWOT?

A. Analyzing the impact of two different SWOT matrices
B. Evaluating how one SWOT factor affects another (e.g., how a threat might create a new weakness)
C. Deleting items that appear in two different categories
D. Asking two different departments to perform the same SWOT

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [SWOT_ADVANCED_019]
A "Leverage Strategy" is another name for:

A. WT Strategy
B. SO Strategy
C. ST Strategy
D. WO Strategy

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [SWOT_ADVANCED_020]
What is the main limitation of SWOT analysis for long-term strategic planning?

A. It is too difficult to understand
B. It is a snapshot in time and can quickly become outdated in a rapidly changing environment
C. It requires too much data
D. It only works for the software industry

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 40: Stakeholder Management

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary definition of a stakeholder in a project?

A. Only the people who own shares in the company
B. Any individual, group, or organization that can affect or be affected by a project
C. Only the software developers writing the code
D. The competitors who are trying to build a similar product

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_002]
Which of the following is considered an "Internal Stakeholder"?

A. Government regulators
B. Project team members
C. End customers
D. External suppliers

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_003]
Which of the following is considered an "External Stakeholder"?

A. The Project Manager
B. The Company CEO
C. A local community group affected by the project
D. The Business Analyst

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_004]
When is the best time to identify stakeholders in a project?

A. At the very end of the project
B. Only when a problem occurs
C. At the start of the project and continuously throughout
D. Only during the testing phase

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_005]
What is the primary goal of stakeholder management?

A. To make sure everyone does exactly what the Project Manager says
B. To identify stakeholder needs and ensure their effective engagement for project success
C. To hide project risks from the client
D. To reduce the number of employees working on a project

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_006]
Who is the "Project Sponsor"?

A. The lead programmer
B. The person or group that provides resources and support for the project and is accountable for success
C. A customer who bought the product 5 years ago
D. The HR department

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_007]
True or False: All stakeholders have the same level of interest and influence in a project.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q8 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_008]

A. Budget Sheet
B. Stakeholder Register
C. Source Code Log
D. Employee Handbook

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_009]
In stakeholder management, "Influence" refers to:

A. How much money the stakeholder has
B. The power a stakeholder has to facilitate or impede project tasks
C. The stakeholder's personality type
D. The number of emails a stakeholder sends

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_010]
In stakeholder management, "Interest" refers to:

A. The interest rate on the project loan
B. The level of concern or desire a stakeholder has regarding the project outcomes
C. How many hobbies the stakeholder has
D. The length of the stakeholder's career

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_011]
Why is communication important in stakeholder management?

A. To keep stakeholders informed and manage their expectations
B. To fill up time during the workday
C. To ensure the Business Analyst is the most popular person
D. To create more documentation

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_012]
What does "Sign-off" represent in stakeholder relations?

A. A stakeholder quitting the company
B. Formal approval and agreement on a specific deliverable or requirement
C. The end of a project meeting
D. Deleting a stakeholder from the email list

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_013]
Which of the following is a common way to gather feedback from stakeholders?

A. Ignoring their emails
B. Surveys and Interviews
C. Guessing what they want
D. Asking the competitors

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_014]
True or False: A stakeholder can be a single person or an entire department.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q15 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_015]
What is a "Key Stakeholder"?

A. Someone who holds the physical keys to the office
B. A person with significant influence or importance regarding project success
C. Any person who has ever heard of the company
D. An intern on their first day

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_016]
"Managing Expectations" means:

A. Telling stakeholders exactly what they want to hear, even if it's not true
B. Ensuring stakeholders have a realistic understanding of what the project will deliver and when
C. Changing the project scope every time a stakeholder asks
D. Deleting requirements that are hard to meet

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_017]

A. Database Administrator
B. Business Analyst
C. Security Officer
D. Hardware Technician

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_018]
True or False: Stakeholders' needs and levels of influence can change over time.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q19 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_019]
What is the purpose of "Meeting Minutes"?

A. To record who attended and the key decisions/actions agreed upon during a stakeholder meeting
B. To time how long each person speaks
C. To list the snacks provided at the meeting
D. To write a script for a company video

Correct Answer: A

Q20 [STAKEHOLDER_BEGINNER_020]
If a stakeholder is "Unaware" of a project, the management goal is usually to:

A. Keep them unaware to avoid questions
B. Make them "Aware" and potentially "Supportive"
C. Remove them from the organization
D. Assign them the most difficult tasks

Correct Answer: B

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_001]
Which tool is used to categorize stakeholders based on their power and interest?

A. Gantt Chart
B. Power-Interest Grid
C. Fishbone Diagram
D. Pareto Chart

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_002]
In a Power-Interest Grid, how should you manage a stakeholder with **High Power** and **High Interest**?

A. Monitor (Minimum effort)
B. Keep Informed
C. Keep Satisfied
D. Manage Closely

Correct Answer: D

Q3 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_003]
In a Power-Interest Grid, how should you manage a stakeholder with **High Power** but **Low Interest**?

A. Manage Closely
B. Keep Satisfied
C. Monitor
D. Keep Informed

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_004]

A. Responsible, Accountable, Consulted, Informed
B. Real, Active, Central, Integrated
C. Ready, Action, Change, Impact
D. Resource, Analyst, Consultant, Inspector

Q5 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_005]

A. Responsible
B. Accountable
C. Consulted
D. Informed

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_006]

A. Counting the number of stakeholders
B. The process of gathering and evaluating information to determine whose interests should be considered
C. Predicting the stakeholders' future salaries
D. Testing the software for stakeholders

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_007]

A. Ignore them and hope they go away
B. Understand their concerns and try to mitigate their negative impact or win their support
C. Tell the CEO to fire them
D. Stop the project immediately

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is the purpose of a "Communication Management Plan"?

A. To list everyone's personal cell phone numbers
B. To define how, when, and by whom information will be distributed to stakeholders
C. To record every conversation in the office
D. To design the company website

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is the difference between "Consulted" and "Informed" in a RACI matrix?

A. Consulted stakeholders provide input; Informed stakeholders are just kept updated on progress
B. Informed stakeholders do the work; Consulted stakeholders pay for it
C. There is no difference
D. Consulted is for managers; Informed is for interns

Q10 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_010]

B. Negotiation and Empathy
C. Python Programming
D. Hardware Repair

Q11 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_011]

A. A tool to compare current vs. desired engagement levels of stakeholders
B. A chart showing stakeholder birthdays
C. A list of software bugs reported by stakeholders
D. A ranking of stakeholders by their office size

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_012]
When two stakeholders have conflicting requirements, the Business Analyst should:

A. Pick the requirement from the person they like more
B. Facilitate a meeting to find a compromise or determine the higher business value
C. Include both requirements and let the developers figure it out
D. Delete both requirements to avoid conflict

Q13 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_013]

A. Taking notes as fast as possible
B. Fully concentrating, understanding, responding, and remembering what is being said
C. Interrupting the speaker to correct them
D. Recording the meeting without participating

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_014]
"Latent Stakeholders" are those who:

A. Have already left the company
B. Have high power but low interest currently, but could become active if the project changes
C. Are the most supportive of the project
D. Write the technical documentation

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is the "Keep Informed" strategy used for in the Power-Interest Grid?

A. Stakeholders with High Power/Low Interest
B. Stakeholders with Low Power/High Interest
C. Stakeholders with Low Power/Low Interest
D. All stakeholders

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_016]
Which technique helps in identifying stakeholders by looking at the project from different organizational levels?

A. Brainstorming
B. Organizational Chart Review
C. Market Research
D. Prototyping

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_017]
A stakeholder who is "Neutral" is:

A. Aware of the project but neither supportive nor resistant
B. Actively trying to stop the project
C. The person who pays the bills
D. A competitor

Correct Answer: A

Q18 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_018]
Why is "Transparency" important in stakeholder management?

A. To make the project look perfect
B. To build trust and ensure stakeholders are aware of both progress and challenges
C. To show off the technical skills of the team
D. To ensure no one asks any questions

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_019]
Which of the following is a "Barrier" to effective stakeholder engagement?

A. Regular updates
B. Cultural or language differences
C. Clear requirements
D. Shared goals

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [STAKEHOLDER_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What should be included in a "Stakeholder Profile"?

A. Role, influence level, preferred communication method, and key concerns
B. Personal home address and home phone number
C. A list of every project they have ever worked on
D. Their social media login details

Correct Answer: A

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "Salience Model" in stakeholder analysis?

A. A model based on the stakeholder's salary
B. Categorizing stakeholders based on their Power, Legitimacy, and Urgency
C. A model used for 3D visualization
D. A ranking of stakeholders by their age

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_002]
In the Salience Model, a "Definitive Stakeholder" possesses:

A. Only Power
B. Power, Legitimacy, and Urgency
C. Only Urgency
D. Power and Interest

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_003]
How do you navigate "Organizational Politics" when managing stakeholders?

A. By ignoring the political landscape entirely
B. By identifying informal networks of influence and understanding the underlying motives of key players
C. By picking a side and staying with it
D. By reporting all political discussions to HR

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Influencing Without Authority"?

A. Forcing people to do tasks
B. Using logic, relationship building, and negotiation to lead stakeholders when you have no direct power over them
C. Using the CEO's name to get things done
D. Threatening stakeholders with bad performance reviews

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_005]
A "Steering Committee" is a group of:

A. Junior developers
B. High-level stakeholders who provide strategic direction and governance for the project
C. Customers who use the software
D. People who drive the company cars

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_006]
How does "Change Management" integrate with stakeholder management?

A. It doesn't; they are separate fields
B. Stakeholder management identifies who needs to change; Change management provides the tools to help them transition
C. Change management is only for IT systems
D. Stakeholder management replaces change management

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_007]
What is a "Shadow Stakeholder"?

A. A stakeholder who only works at night
B. A person who is not officially recognized but has significant indirect influence over a decision-maker
C. A deleted contact in the register
D. An undercover security guard

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_008]
When performing "Impact Analysis" for a project change, what is the stakeholder's role?

A. To do the technical work
B. To evaluate how the change affects their specific business area and provide approval or feedback
C. To pay for the change personally
D. Stakeholders should not be involved in impact analysis

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_009]
In the Salience Model, "Dormant Stakeholders" have:

A. High Power but no Legitimacy or Urgency
B. High Urgency but no Power
C. High Interest and High Power
D. Low Power and Low Interest

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Ethical Stakeholder Management"?

A. Only following the laws that are convenient
B. Balancing the interests of all stakeholders fairly and transparently, even when they conflict
C. Prioritizing the interests of the shareholders above all else at all times
D. Hiding information that might make stakeholders unhappy

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_011]
How should a Business Analyst handle a "Detractor" who is a high-level executive?

A. Avoid them at all costs
B. Seek to understand the root of their opposition and involve them in low-risk decision-making to build trust
C. Challenge their authority in public meetings
D. Resign from the project

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_012]
What is the "Halo Effect" in stakeholder perception?

A. When a stakeholder is considered perfect
B. The tendency for a stakeholder's positive impression in one area to influence opinion in another area
C. A type of visual error in a report
D. When a stakeholder is religious

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Upward Management"?

A. Managing the staff who work for you
B. The process of consciously managing your relationship with your superiors or high-power stakeholders
C. Moving the office to a higher floor
D. Increasing the project budget

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_014]
"Stakeholder Fatigue" refers to:

A. When the analyst is tired of talking to stakeholders
B. Disengagement caused by excessive requests for input, meetings, or updates without clear value
C. When stakeholders work too many hours
D. When a stakeholder retires

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_015]
In the context of "Global Projects," what is a major stakeholder challenge?

A. Using the same software
B. Managing time zones, cultural nuances, and varying regulatory environments
C. Buying office supplies
D. Choosing a project name

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_016]
What is a "Primary" vs "Secondary" stakeholder in advanced analysis?

A. Primary are those essential to project survival; Secondary are those who affect or are affected but aren't essential
B. Primary are internal; Secondary are external
C. Primary are the ones who speak English; Secondary speak other languages
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_017]
How can "Data Visualization" be used specifically for stakeholder management?

A. To create artistic portraits of stakeholders
B. To create "Stakeholder Maps" that visually represent relationships and influence levels
C. To show stakeholders how to use Excel
D. To hide project delays

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Conflict De-escalation" technique?

A. Ignoring the conflict
B. Strategies used to reduce the intensity of a disagreement (e.g., cooling-off periods, finding common ground)
C. Winning the argument at any cost
D. Escalating the issue to the CEO immediately

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Psychological Safety" in a stakeholder workshop?

A. Wearing a helmet
B. A shared belief that it's safe to take risks, ask questions, and share concerns without fear of negative consequences
C. Locking the doors during the meeting
D. Having a doctor present

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [STAKEHOLDER_ADVANCED_020]
What is the "ROI of Stakeholder Management"?

A. The cost of the stakeholder register
B. The value generated by reducing risks, avoiding rework, and ensuring project alignment through effective engagement
C. The interest earned on the project budget
D. The salary of the Business Analyst

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 41: KPI Analysis

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [KPI_BEGINNER_001]
What does the acronym KPI stand for?

A. Key Process Integration
B. Key Performance Indicator
C. Knowledge Performance Index
D. Key Profitability Increment

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [KPI_BEGINNER_002]
What is the primary purpose of a KPI?

A. To record every single transaction in a business
B. To measure how effectively a company is achieving key business objectives
C. To replace the company's financial accounting system
D. To list the names of all employees in a department

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [KPI_BEGINNER_003]
True or False: Every metric used by a business is considered a KPI.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q4 [KPI_BEGINNER_004]
What is a "Target" in the context of KPI analysis?

A. The name of a major retail competitor
B. The specific value or level of performance the business aims to reach for a KPI
C. The first row of a data table
D. The person responsible for data entry

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [KPI_BEGINNER_005]
The "SMART" criteria for KPIs stands for: Specific, Measurable, Achievable, Relevant, and:

A. Technical
B. Time-bound
C. Total
D. Transparent

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [KPI_BEGINNER_006]
Which of the following is a common Sales KPI?

A. Server Uptime
B. Monthly Recurring Revenue (MRR)
C. Employee Satisfaction Score
D. Number of code bugs fixed

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [KPI_BEGINNER_007]
What is "Churn Rate"?

A. The speed at which a machine operates
B. The percentage of customers who stop using a service over a given time period
C. The total profit earned per customer
D. The number of new employees hired

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [KPI_BEGINNER_008]
Which type of indicator looks at past data to see what has already happened?

A. Leading Indicator
B. Lagging Indicator
C. Real-time Indicator
D. Future Indicator

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [KPI_BEGINNER_009]
"Customer Acquisition Cost (CAC)" measures:

A. The total revenue from one customer
B. The total cost spent on marketing and sales to win a new customer
C. The salary of the sales manager
D. The price of the product

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [KPI_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: A KPI should be clearly aligned with the strategic goals of the organization.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q11 [KPI_BEGINNER_011]
If a website had 1,000 visitors and 50 of them made a purchase, what is the "Conversion Rate"?

A. 0.5%
B. 5%
C. 10%
D. 50%

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [KPI_BEGINNER_012]
What is the "Net Promoter Score" (NPS) used to measure?

A. Employee payroll
B. Customer loyalty and satisfaction
C. Physical weight of products
D. Internet connection speed

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [KPI_BEGINNER_013]
Which of the following is a "Leading" indicator for a sales team?

A. Total revenue last quarter
B. Number of sales calls made this week
C. Annual profit margin
D. Yearly tax payment

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [KPI_BEGINNER_014]
What is "ROI"?

A. Return on Investment
B. Rate of Information
C. Record of Income
D. Risk of Inactivity

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [KPI_BEGINNER_015]
How often should KPIs typically be reviewed?

A. Once every 10 years
B. Regularly (e.g., weekly, monthly, or quarterly) depending on the business need
C. Only when the company is losing money
D. Only when a new CEO is hired

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [KPI_BEGINNER_016]
Which of the following is a "Quantitative" KPI?

A. "Our brand looks modern"
B. "Total sales reached $1,000,000"
C. "Customers seem happy"
D. "The office atmosphere is good"

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [KPI_BEGINNER_017]
What does "Average Order Value" (AOV) track?

A. The average number of orders per day
B. The average dollar amount spent each time a customer places an order
C. The speed of the delivery truck
D. The weight of the packaging

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [KPI_BEGINNER_018]
True or False: Having too many KPIs (e.g., 100 per department) is usually better than having a few.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q19 [KPI_BEGINNER_019]
A "Dashboard" in KPI analysis is used to:

A. Store physical files
B. Visually display and track the status of multiple KPIs in one place
C. Write the company's code
D. Hide data from stakeholders

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [KPI_BEGINNER_020]
What is "Gross Profit Margin"?

A. Total revenue minus the cost of goods sold, divided by revenue
B. The total number of units sold
C. The salary of the CEO
D. The total tax paid by the company

Correct Answer: A

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between a "Leading Indicator" and a "Lagging Indicator"?

A. Leading indicators predict future performance; Lagging indicators measure past results
B. Lagging indicators are faster to calculate
C. Leading indicators are for small businesses only
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What is a "Vanity Metric"?

A. A metric that is very expensive to track
B. A metric that looks good on paper but doesn't correlate with actual business success
C. A metric used by the fashion industry
D. A metric that is 100% accurate

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_003]
How do you calculate "Year-over-Year (YoY) Growth"?

A. (Current Year Value - Last Year Value) / Last Year Value * 100
B. Current Year Value + Last Year Value
C. Current Year Value / 12
D. Last Year Value - Current Year Value

Correct Answer: A

Q4 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What is "ARPU" in a subscription-based business?

A. Annual Revenue Per User
B. Average Revenue Per User
C. Automated Resource Processing Unit
D. Actual Real Profit Utility

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_005]
Why is "Data Integrity" crucial for KPI analysis?

A. It makes the charts look more colorful
B. Inaccurate data leads to misleading KPIs and poor business decisions
C. It reduces the file size of the reports
D. It is only required for legal reasons

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_006]
In a SaaS (Software as a Service) business, "LTV" stands for:

A. Long Term View
B. Lifetime Value (of a customer)
C. Lead Time Variance
D. Last Transaction Volume

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What does a "Variance Analysis" of a KPI involve?

A. Calculating the square root of the KPI
B. Comparing actual KPI performance against the planned budget or target
C. Changing the KPI definition every month
D. Deleting outliers from the dataset

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_008]
Which of the following is a KPI for operational efficiency?

A. Customer Satisfaction Score
B. Cost Per Unit Produced
C. Social Media Followers
D. Brand Awareness percentage

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is the "Quick Ratio" used to measure?

A. The speed of a website
B. A company's ability to meet its short-term financial obligations
C. The rate of employee promotions
D. The speed of the production line

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_010]
How can a Business Analyst identify if a KPI is "Actionable"?

A. If the KPI is written in bold
B. If the metric provides clear insights that allow management to make specific changes or improvements
C. If the KPI never changes
D. If the KPI is only viewed by the CEO

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_011]
"Bounce Rate" in web analytics refers to:

A. The percentage of users who leave a site after viewing only one page
B. The number of emails that were not delivered
C. The speed at which a page loads
D. The rate of returned physical goods

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "Benchmarking" in KPI analysis?

A. Designing a bench for the office
B. Comparing your company's KPIs against industry standards or competitors
C. Calculating the average of all KPIs
D. Setting a target that is impossible to reach

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_013]
Which KPI would be most useful for measuring the effectiveness of a Customer Support team?

A. Average Handle Time (AHT)
B. Total Sales Revenue
C. Marketing Spend
D. Inventory Turnover

Correct Answer: A

Q14 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is "Customer Retention Rate"?

A. The percentage of new customers acquired
B. The percentage of existing customers who remain customers over a period
C. The total number of customers in the database
D. The cost of hiring a customer service agent

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_015]
A "Balanced Scorecard" approach involves looking at KPIs from which four perspectives?

A. Financial, Customer, Internal Process, and Learning/Growth
B. Sales, Marketing, HR, and IT
C. Past, Present, Future, and Estimated
D. Domestic, International, Online, and Offline

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_016]
What is "Inventory Turnover"?

A. The number of times a company has sold and replaced its inventory during a specific period
B. The physical rotation of items on a shelf
C. The total value of the warehouse
D. The cost of storing goods

Correct Answer: A

Q17 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_017]
"Employee Turnover" measures:

A. The percentage of employees who leave the organization in a year
B. How many times an employee switches desks
C. The total number of hours employees work
D. The rate of employee promotions

Correct Answer: A

Q18 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does "First Call Resolution" (FCR) indicate?

A. How many calls a support agent makes
B. The percentage of customer issues resolved during the first interaction
C. The duration of the first call of the day
D. The cost of the telephone system

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_019]
If a company's CAC is $100 and the LTV is $50, the business is:

A. Highly profitable
B. Losing money on every customer in the long run
C. Breaking even
D. Growing too fast

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [KPI_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is the purpose of "Weighting" different KPIs in a summary score?

A. To make the report heavier
B. To reflect that some goals are more important to the business strategy than others
C. To hide poor performance
D. To simplify the math

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [KPI_ADVANCED_001]
What is the "LTV to CAC Ratio" and why is it important?

A. It compares the lifetime value of a customer to the cost of acquiring them to determine business viability
B. it is a way to calculate tax
C. It shows the ratio of leads to sales
D. It measures the speed of product delivery

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [KPI_ADVANCED_002]
What is "CAGR"?

A. Compound Annual Growth Rate
B. Current Annual Gross Revenue
C. Calculated Average Growth Ratio
D. Company Annual Gains Record

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [KPI_ADVANCED_003]
How does "Seasonality" affect KPI interpretation?

A. It has no effect on KPIs
B. It can cause fluctuations in metrics (like ice cream sales in winter) that might be mistaken for performance issues
C. It requires the company to change its name
D. It only affects agricultural businesses

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [KPI_ADVANCED_004]
In advanced analytics, what is "Root Cause Analysis" when a KPI misses a target?

A. Blaming the data analyst
B. A structured process to identify the underlying reason for the performance gap
C. Deleting the target so the KPI is no longer missing it
D. Increasing the budget for the next month

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [KPI_ADVANCED_005]
What is "Cohort Analysis" in the context of KPI tracking?

A. Analyzing the performance of all customers together
B. Breaking down data into related groups (cohorts) to see how behavior changes over time (e.g., users who joined in January vs. February)
C. Calculating the average salary of a group
D. A type of social media marketing

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [KPI_ADVANCED_006]
What is "Payback Period" in customer acquisition?

A. The time it takes for a customer to pay their invoice
B. The amount of time it takes for a customer to generate enough revenue to cover their acquisition cost (CAC)
C. The time it takes to fire a customer
D. The duration of a project

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [KPI_ADVANCED_007]
"Net Profit Margin" is calculated by:

A. (Net Income / Revenue) * 100
B. Revenue - Expenses
C. Gross Profit / Total Employees
D. Revenue / Tax Rate

Correct Answer: A

Q8 [KPI_ADVANCED_008]
What is the "SaaS Rule of 40"?

A. A company must have 40 employees to be successful
B. A principle that a healthy SaaS company's combined growth rate and profit margin should exceed 40%
C. Revenue must grow by 40% every year
D. Churn must be below 40%

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [KPI_ADVANCED_009]
In financial KPI analysis, "EBITDA" stands for:

A. Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, Depreciation, and Amortization
B. Every Business Income Total Daily Average
C. Estimated Budget Including Total Debt Analysis
D. Earnings Based on Internal Team Data Assets

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [KPI_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Attribution Modeling" and how does it affect Marketing KPIs?

A. Assigning credit to different marketing channels for a final sale or conversion
B. Deleting the marketing department
C. Calculating the cost of a logo
D. Predicting the stock price of a competitor

Correct Answer: A

Q11 [KPI_ADVANCED_011]
How can "Statistical Significance" be applied to KPI changes (e.g., in A/B testing)?

A. By guessing if the change was big enough
B. Using mathematical tests to determine if a change in a KPI is due to a specific action or just random noise
C. By asking the manager for their opinion
D. Statistical significance is not applicable to business

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [KPI_ADVANCED_012]
What is a "Composite KPI"?

A. A KPI that is very simple
B. An index that combines multiple underlying metrics into a single score
C. A KPI that has been deleted
D. A KPI for the construction industry

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [KPI_ADVANCED_013]
What is "DSO" (Days Sales Outstanding)?

A. The number of days a sale takes to close
B. The average number of days it takes a company to collect payment after a sale has been made
C. The total number of sales in a year
D. The number of days the store is open

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [KPI_ADVANCED_014]
"Sensitivity Analysis" on a KPI model helps to:

A. Protect the feelings of the team
B. Understand how much the final KPI value changes based on variations in different input assumptions
C. Scale the charts correctly
D. Find errors in the data entry

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [KPI_ADVANCED_015]
What is "Linear Regression" used for in KPI forecasting?

A. To group customers together
B. To predict future values of a KPI based on its historical relationship with other variables
C. To delete outliers
D. To change the color of the line chart

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [KPI_ADVANCED_016]
What is the "Denominator Effect" in percentage-based KPIs?

A. When the result is too large
B. When a KPI looks better or worse not because of the performance (numerator) but because the total population (denominator) changed
C. When the denominator is zero
D. When the calculation is performed in fractions

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [KPI_ADVANCED_017]
"Operational Gearing" in financial analysis relates to:

A. The speed of the gears in a machine
B. The relationship between fixed costs and variable costs and how they impact profit KPIs as revenue scales
C. The hiring process
D. The project management software

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [KPI_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Predictive KPI Modeling"?

A. Reviewing last year's reports
B. Using machine learning to forecast what a KPI will be in the future based on leading indicators
C. Guessing the future
D. Creating a dashboard for the CEO

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [KPI_ADVANCED_019]
A "Self-Correcting KPI" mechanism involves:

A. The KPI fixing itself in Excel
B. Automating business processes to trigger when a KPI falls below a threshold to bring it back on track
C. The analyst manually correcting errors
D. Changing the target every day

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [KPI_ADVANCED_020]
What is the main challenge of "Global KPI Standardization"?

A. Language translation
B. Ensuring metrics mean the same thing across different regions with different currencies, laws, and market conditions
C. Different time zones for reporting
D. The cost of the software licenses

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_009]

Q10 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_010]

A. Deleting all the data

Q11 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_011]
Which of the following is a "Dimension" in a dashboard dataset?

A. Total Sales Amount
B. Region Name
C. Average Temperature
D. Profit Margin

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_012]
Which of the following is a "Measure" or "Metric" in a dashboard?

A. Product Category
B. Customer Name
C. Total Revenue
D. Order Date

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_013]
What is "White Space" (Negative Space) in dashboard design?

A. The space where data is missing
B. The empty space between elements used to improve readability and focus
C. A white-colored background for all charts
D. The space used for the company logo only

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_014]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q15 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_015]

A. A suggestion for a new project

D. The password for the data source

Correct Answer: B

A. Line Chart
B. Bar Chart
C. Area Chart
D. Scatter Plot

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_017]

A. The person who views the dashboard

C. The screen used to display the dashboard
D. The folder where PDF exports are saved

Q18 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_018]

A. To decide which font to use

D. To collect the users' personal email addresses

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_019]
A "Scorecard" on a dashboard typically shows:

A. A single high-level metric (e.g., Total Sales) and its status against a goal

C. A detailed map of the world
D. A 3D model of a product

Q20 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_BEGINNER_020]

A. A story about the data

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is "Drill-down" capability in a dashboard?

A. The ability to delete the dashboard
B. The ability to click on a high-level data point to see more granular details
C. The ability to rotate a 3D chart
D. The ability to change the data source manually

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_002]
Which feature allows you to link multiple charts so that filtering one affects all others on the dashboard?

A. Cross-filtering (or Interactions)
B. Data Blending
C. Conditional Formatting
D. Tooltips

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is the purpose of "Conditional Formatting" in a table or chart?

A. To hide data that doesn't meet a condition
B. To automatically change the visual appearance (e.g., color) of data based on its value
C. To protect the dashboard with a password
D. To format the dates to a specific time zone

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_004]

A. Those colors are considered unprofessional
B. It is the most common form of color blindness

D. They are only used for financial data

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is "Data Blending"?

A. Mixing numbers and text in the same column
B. Combining data from multiple different data sources into a single view or dashboard

D. Changing a line chart into a bar chart

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_006]

B. Box Plot
C. Treemap
D. Gauge Chart

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_007]

A. Showing hierarchical data as a set of nested rectangles

D. Showing the relationship between two variables

Correct Answer: A

Q8 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What does "Data-Ink Ratio" (Edward Tufte's principle) suggest?

C. Print the dashboard on high-quality paper
D. Use thick borders around every chart

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_009]

D. Create an animation

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_010]

A. Letting the user write the code

D. Limiting the dashboard to only one user

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_011]

A. By locking the data source

C. By increasing the resolution of images
D. By managing user passwords

Q12 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_012]

A. A failing grade for a dashboard

C. A type of mathematical distribution
D. A way to group charts in folders

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_013]

Q15 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_INTERMEDIATE_015]

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_001]
What is "Row-Level Security" (RLS) in a published dashboard?

A. Locking the physical rows of the server
B. Restricting data access for specific users so they only see rows relevant to them (e.g., their own region)
C. Encrypting the first row of every table
D. Preventing users from adding new rows

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_002]

A. The age of the data analysts
B. The delay between a user action (like clicking a filter) and the visuals updating
C. The time it takes to build the dashboard
D. The number of charts on a page

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_003]

B. Use "Extracts" or "Aggregated" tables instead of querying raw transaction data

D. Increase the number of global filters

Correct Answer: B

C. A list of all data sources
D. A type of bar chart

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_005]

D. Converting a chart to a table

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_006]

A. Putting a dashboard inside a physical folder

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_007]

B. The path showing the origin of data and how it has been transformed before reaching the dashboard
C. The speed of data transfer
D. The alphabetical list of columns

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_008]

C. A dashboard showing today's delivery status
D. A clock showing the current time

Q9 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_009]

A. To delete data rows
B. To trigger an action (like opening a website or filtering another report) based on a user's selection in a visual
C. To record the user's screen
D. To update the software version

Q10 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_010]

B. Use "Progressive Disclosure" (showing high-level metrics first and providing drill-through for details)
C. Use smaller fonts to fit more data
D. Remove all filters

Q11 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Cross-Database Joining" in a dashboard tool?

A. Copying data from one database to another
B. Creating a single visual that draws and links data from two different sources (e.g., SQL and Excel)
C. Using two different dashboard tools at once
D. Deleting the connection to the database

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_012]
What is the "Z-Pattern" in user interface design?

A. A way to draw a 3D chart
B. A scanning pattern for dashboards with less text, where the eye follows a Z-shape
C. A method for encrypting data
D. A failure in the rendering engine

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_013]

A. The charts are too colorful

C. The database is too small
D. The dashboard is viewed on a high-resolution screen

Q14 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_014]
What is "Embedded Analytics"?

A. Memorizing the data
B. Integrating dashboard visuals directly into business applications or websites

D. Hiding the analysis from the user

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_015]

A. Checking if the user's photo looks good

C. Creating a password for every user
D. Predicting the user's future behavior

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_016]

A. A group of people who do math

C. A folder for storing Excel files
D. A type of pie chart

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_017]

A. The size of the screen

C. The price of the software
D. The number of colors used

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_018]
What is the "McKinsey/MECE" principle applied to dashboarding?

A. Using bright neon colors
B. Ensuring metrics are Mutually Exclusive and Collectively Exhaustive to avoid overlap or gaps in insight
C. Including raw data tables on every page
D. Using 3D charts for every visual

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DASHBOARD_CREATION_ADVANCED_019]

A. It is too hard to create

C. It only works with 3D data
D. It deletes the legend

## Skill 43: Reporting

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of a business report?

A. To tell a fictional story
B. To provide factual information and data-driven insights for decision-making
C. To replace the need for oral communication
D. To list every single internal email sent by employees

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_002]

A. Scheduled Report
B. Ad-hoc Report
C. Annual Report
D. Inventory Report

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_003]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

B. Microsoft Excel
C. Windows Media Player
D. Notepad

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_005]

A. A report that includes every single transaction detail

C. A report consisting only of images
D. A report written by an intern

Q6 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_006]

C. The person who reads the report

Correct Answer: B

B. Monthly

B. Executive Summary
C. Appendix
D. Glossary

Correct Answer: B

A. A report that changes every time you look at it

Q15 [REPORTING_BEGINNER_015]

Q18 [REPORTING_INTERMEDIATE_018]

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_001]
What is "Reporting Governance"?

A. A political party for data analysts
B. The framework of rules, roles, and processes used to manage an organization's reporting environment and ensure data consistency
C. The government regulations for financial reporting
D. The process of electing a head of reporting

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_002]
How does "Real-time Streaming Reporting" differ from standard automated reporting?

A. It uses more colors
B. It processes and displays data continuously as it is generated, rather than waiting for batch updates
C. It is only available on mobile phones
D. It is always accurate

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_003]
What is "Data Lineage" in advanced reporting?

A. The family tree of the reporting team
B. A visual or documented path showing where data originates and how it is transformed before it reaches the final report
C. The speed of data transfer
D. The history of SQL versions used

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_004]

A. Making all charts the same size
B. Standardizing metrics across different regions (e.g., converting all currencies to USD) to allow for fair comparison
C. Deleting outliers in every region
D. Using the same font for every language

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_005]

A. Financial accounting
B. Agile Project Management
C. HR performance reviews
D. Supply chain logistics

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_006]
In complex reporting, what is a "Snapshot" table?

A. A high-resolution photo of a report
B. A database table that stores periodic captures of data to track historical changes over time
C. A table that only has one row
D. A table that is deleted after use

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_007]
What is "Paginated Reporting"?

A. Adding page numbers to Excel
B. Reports designed to be printed or shared as PDFs, where the layout is fixed and optimized for page breaks
C. Reports that only have one page
D. A report about website pages

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_008]
What is the purpose of "Row-Level Security" (RLS) in report distribution?

A. To lock the physical rows of the server
B. To ensure that different users see only the data they are authorized to see when viewing the same report
C. To encrypt the first row of every table
D. To prevent users from editing the data

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_009]
How can "Predictive Analytics" be integrated into a business report?

A. By reviewing last year's sales
B. By including forecasts and probability-based estimates for future outcomes alongside historical data
C. By guessing the future
D. By including a "Predict" button that does nothing

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_010]
What is "Cohort Analysis" reporting?

A. Analyzing the performance of all customers together
B. Breaking down data into related groups (cohorts) to compare how their behavior changes over time
C. Analyzing the reporting team's productivity
D. A type of social media report

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_011]
What is the "Single Source of Truth" (SSOT) principle in reporting?

A. Only the CEO is allowed to tell the truth
B. The practice of structuring data models so that every data element is mastered in only one place
C. Writing only one report for the whole company
D. Making sure the report has no errors

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_012]
What is "Embedded Reporting"?

A. Memorizing the report data
B. Integrating reports and charts directly into other business applications or portals
C. Putting a report in a physical envelope
D. Hiding a report from the user

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_013]
"Data Latency" in reporting refers to:

A. The time it takes for a user to read the report
B. The time delay between when data is generated in the real world and when it appears in the report
C. The speed of the analyst's typing
D. The total file size of the report

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_014]
What is "Metadata" management in reporting?

A. Storing data in the cloud
B. Managing the information that describes the data (e.g., definitions, data types, ownership) to ensure clarity
C. Deleting data that is not useful
D. Changing the colors of the charts

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_015]
A "Scorecard" typically differs from a standard report by:

A. Being much longer
B. Focusing on progress toward specific strategic goals or targets using status indicators (e.g., red/yellow/green)
C. Using only text and no charts
D. Being updated only once a year

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Report Bursting"?

A. Deleting a report by mistake
B. The process of generating a single report and automatically sending different filtered versions to different recipients
C. A report that is too large to open
D. A high-speed printing technique

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_017]
"Sensitivity Analysis" in a financial report helps to:

A. Protect the feelings of the stakeholders
B. Show how different values of an independent variable affect a particular dependent variable under certain assumptions
C. Calculate the standard deviation of the report's page count
D. Detect spelling errors in numbers

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Regulatory Reporting" (e.g., for GDPR or SOX)?

A. Reporting on the company's internal rules
B. Reporting required by law to demonstrate compliance with government or industry regulations
C. Reporting on the number of rules in the office
D. A report about the legal team's schedule

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Exploratory Reporting"?

A. A report about a trip to a new country
B. Providing users with tools to interactively query and explore data themselves rather than just viewing static numbers
C. Finding new data sources to use in reports
D. Deleting data that is not interesting

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [REPORTING_ADVANCED_020]
The "McKinsey/MECE" principle in reporting ensures that information is:

A. Beautiful and modern
B. Mutually Exclusive and Collectively Exhaustive (no overlaps, no gaps)
C. Long and detailed
D. Written in a professional tone only

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 44: Data Interpretation

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_001]
What is the first step in interpreting a new data chart?

A. Drawing a conclusion immediately
B. Checking the title, axes labels, and legend
C. Deleting the outliers
D. Changing the colors of the bars

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_002]
If a bar chart shows "Revenue" on the Y-axis and "Months" on the X-axis, what does a taller bar indicate?

A. More time passed
B. Higher revenue for that month
C. Lower expenses
D. A software error

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_003]
On a line graph, a line that slopes upward from left to right generally indicates:

A. A decreasing trend
B. An increasing trend
C. No change
D. A random fluctuation

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_004]
In a pie chart representing market share, what does the largest slice represent?

A. The smallest competitor
B. The category with the highest percentage or value
C. The most recent data point
D. An error in calculation

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_005]
What is the "Legend" of a chart used for?

A. To tell a story about the data
B. To explain what different colors or symbols represent
C. To show the total sum of the data
D. To list the names of the analysts

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_006]
If a table shows sales for four quarters and the values are 100, 120, 150, and 200, what is the overall trend?

A. Decreasing
B. Stable
C. Increasing
D. Fluctuating

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_007]
What does a "Flat Line" on a time-series graph indicate?

A. High growth
B. No change over time
C. Data is missing
D. The system crashed

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_008]
If a chart title is "Average Temperature 2023," what is the "Unit of Measure" likely to be?

A. Dollars
B. Percentages
C. Degrees (Celsius or Fahrenheit)
D. Kilograms

Correct Answer: C

Q9 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_009]
What is an "Outlier" in a visual dataset?

A. A data point that follows the trend perfectly
B. A data point that is significantly far away from the other observations
C. The first data point in a series
D. The label on the X-axis

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_010]
True or False: Data interpretation involves explaining what the data means in a specific context.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q11 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_011]
Which of the following is a "Quantitative" observation?

A. The chart looks professional
B. Sales increased by 15%
C. The colors are easy on the eyes
D. The manager likes the report

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_012]
If a bar chart has a "Gap" between two bars on a timeline, it usually suggests:

A. A very successful period
B. Missing data for that specific time period
C. A zero value
D. A change in the company logo

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_013]
What is the "Mean" value in a set of data?

A. The most common value
B. The difference between max and min
C. The average of all values
D. The middle value when sorted

Correct Answer: C

Q14 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_014]
If 25% of a pie chart is shaded red, what fraction does that represent?

A. One-half
B. One-third
C. One-quarter
D. One-fifth

Correct Answer: C

Q15 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_015]
When reading a table, the "Header Row" typically contains:

A. The final totals
B. The names of the variables or categories in each column
C. The date the table was printed
D. Random numbers

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_016]
A scatter plot with dots forming a tight line suggests:

A. No relationship between variables
B. A strong correlation
C. A mistake in the data
D. That all values are identical

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_017]
What does "N/A" typically mean in a data table?

A. Now Active
B. Not Available / Not Applicable
C. Next Analysis
D. Number Average

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_018]
If a company's goal was $1M and the chart shows $800k, the "Variance" is:

A. $200k under target
B. $200k over target
C. $1.8M total
D. 0

Correct Answer: A

Q19 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_019]
Which chart is best for showing "Parts of a Whole"?

A. Line Chart
B. Pie Chart
C. Scatter Plot
D. Histogram

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_BEGINNER_020]
True or False: Correlation always means that one thing caused another to happen.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the risk of a "Truncated Y-Axis" (an axis that doesn't start at zero)?

A. It makes the chart hard to see
B. It can visually exaggerate small differences between data points
C. It deletes the data source
D. It only works for negative numbers

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_002]
If Sales increase from $100 to $150, what is the percentage increase?

A. 15%
B. 50%
C. 150%
D. 25%

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_003]
How do you interpret a "Negative Correlation" in a scatter plot?

A. As one variable increases, the other also increases
B. As one variable increases, the other decreases
C. Both variables stay the same
D. There is no pattern at all

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_004]
A "Double-Y Axis" chart is used to:

A. Make the chart twice as large
B. Show two variables with different scales/units on the same X-axis
C. Backup the data on two servers
D. Show the same data in two different colors

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_005]
If a line graph shows "Seasonality," you would expect to see:

A. A straight line upward
B. Recurring patterns of highs and lows at specific intervals (e.g., every December)
C. Random dots everywhere
D. A single sharp spike that never repeats

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is "Skewness" in a distribution (Histogram)?

A. The total height of the bars
B. The degree of asymmetry in the distribution
C. The number of categories
D. The color intensity

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_007]
If the "Median" is much lower than the "Mean," what does it suggest about the data?

A. The data is perfectly symmetrical
B. There are a few very high outliers pulling the average up
C. There are many zero values
D. The sample size is too small

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What does a "Cumulative" line on a bar chart represent?

A. The average of each bar
B. The running total of the values up to that point
C. The difference between the highest and lowest bar
D. The projected future value

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_009]
"Data Contextualization" involves:

A. Deleting old data
B. Comparing data against benchmarks, past performance, or goals to give it meaning
C. Changing the file format
D. Translating the chart into another language

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_010]
If "Conversion Rate" drops while "Website Traffic" increases, what is a likely interpretation?

A. The website is performing perfectly
B. The new traffic might be of lower quality or not finding what they need
C. The traffic increase is causing the site to crash
D. Fewer people are visiting the site

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Which of the following describes "Standard Deviation" in simple terms?

A. The average value
B. How much the data points vary or "spread out" from the average
C. The total count of data points
D. The highest value minus the lowest value

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_012]
A "Heatmap" uses color intensity to represent:

A. The temperature of the office
B. The magnitude or frequency of data values across two dimensions
C. The speed of the analyst's work
D. A geographic location only

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_013]
What is "Benchmarking"?

A. Designing a bench for the lobby
B. Comparing your data against industry standards or best practices
C. Calculating the square root of the mean
D. Sorting data by the date it was collected

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_014]
If a "Box Plot" has a very long "whisker" at the top, it indicates:

A. The data is very consistent
B. There is a wide range of values or potential outliers in the higher end of the data
C. The median is zero
D. The data is missing

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is "Year-over-Year" (YoY) comparison?

A. Comparing Monday to Tuesday
B. Comparing a metric in one period to the same period in the previous year
C. Calculating the total revenue for 10 years
D. Comparing two different companies in the same year

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_016]
How do you identify a "Bottleneck" using a process flow chart?

A. Look for where the most arrows are
B. Look for a step where work accumulates and takes much longer than others
C. Look for the start node
D. Look for the most colorful step

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_017]
"Inference" in data interpretation means:

A. Copying the data exactly
B. Drawing a logical conclusion that goes beyond the literal data provided
C. Deleting the data after reading it
D. Changing the data to fit a theory

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does a "Correlation Coefficient" of 0.0 indicate?

A. A perfect relationship
B. No linear relationship between the variables
C. A negative relationship
D. An error in the SQL query

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_019]
A "Stacked Bar Chart" is most useful for:

A. Showing trends over 100 years
B. Comparing the sub-components of different categories as well as the totals
C. Identifying individual outliers in a large set
D. Showing the relationship between two continuous variables

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_INTERMEDIATE_020]
"Data Triangulation" involves:

A. Using three different charts for one report
B. Using multiple sources or methods to cross-verify the interpretation of data
C. Drawing a triangle around the most important data point
D. Deleting two-thirds of the data

Correct Answer: B

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_001]
What is "Simpson's Paradox"?

A. A bug in the Microsoft Excel calculation engine
B. A phenomenon where a trend appears in different groups of data but disappears or reverses when groups are combined
C. A type of visual illusion in a 3D bar chart
D. The tendency for analysts to favor their own data

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_002]
"Statistical Significance" (P-value < 0.05) tells an analyst that:

A. The result is 100% true
B. The observed result is unlikely to have occurred by random chance alone
C. The business will definitely make a profit
D. The sample size was too large

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_003]
How should an analyst interpret "Survivor Bias" in a dataset of successful companies?

A. The data is perfectly representative of all companies
B. The data ignores the many companies that failed, potentially leading to wrong conclusions about success factors
C. The data is outdated
D. The companies are all in the same industry

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Causality" vs "Correlation" in advanced interpretation?

A. They are identical in a business context
B. Correlation shows a relationship; Causality proves that one event is the direct result of the other
C. Correlation is for numbers; Causality is for text
D. Causality is easier to prove than correlation

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_005]
"Root Cause Analysis" (RCA) is performed to:

A. Find the person to blame for a mistake
B. Identify the underlying reason why a data trend or problem is occurring
C. Calculate the square root of the variance
D. Determine the project's end date

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_006]
When interpreting "Cohort Analysis," you are looking at:

A. All customers as a single group
B. How the behavior of groups sharing common characteristics (e.g., join date) changes over time
C. The behavior of the company's competitors
D. The physical location of the server

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_007]
"Confirmation Bias" in interpretation occurs when:

A. The data is confirmed by a manager
B. An analyst looks for or interprets data in a way that confirms their pre-existing beliefs
C. Two different charts show the same result
D. The database sends a confirmation email

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_008]
What is the "Pareto Principle" (80/20 rule) interpretation?

A. 80% of data is always incorrect
B. Roughly 80% of effects (e.g., revenue) come from 20% of causes (e.g., customers)
C. 80% of a project's time is spent on 20% of the tasks
D. You should only interpret 20% of the data

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_009]
"Multicollinearity" in a regression model means:

A. There are too many colors in the chart
B. Independent variables are highly correlated with each other, making it hard to determine their individual effects
C. The target variable is missing
D. The model has too many rows of data

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_010]
How can "Data Granularity" affect interpretation?

A. It doesn't; totals are always enough
B. High-level summaries can hide important underlying patterns or issues visible only at a detailed level
C. It only affects the file size
D. It makes the charts look less professional

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_011]
"Sensitivity Analysis" on a business model involves:

A. Protecting the feelings of the team members
B. Determining how different values of an independent variable impact a specific dependent variable
C. Checking the accuracy of the sensors
D. Measuring the speed of the data transfer

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_012]
What does "Homoscedasticity" refer to in residual analysis?

A. The errors have a constant variance across all levels of an independent variable
B. The variables are all identical
C. The data has no outliers
D. The mean is always zero

Correct Answer: A

Q13 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_013]
"Lagging Indicators" (like last year's profit) are best used for:

A. Predicting tomorrow's sales
B. Confirming long-term trends and evaluating past performance
C. Identifying immediate operational bottlenecks
D. Planning a weekly schedule

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_014]
"Leading Indicators" (like new sales leads) are best used for:

A. Recording historical tax data
B. Predicting future performance or trends
C. Auditing past expenses
D. Deleting old records

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_015]
What is a "False Positive" (Type I Error) in data interpretation?

A. Finding an effect or relationship that does not actually exist
B. Missing an effect or relationship that does actually exist
C. A software bug that shows a plus sign
D. A positive revenue number that is actually negative

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_016]
What is a "False Negative" (Type II Error) in interpretation?

A. Finding an effect that doesn't exist
B. Failing to detect an effect or relationship that does actually exist
C. A decrease in revenue
D. An error message in a SQL query

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_017]
"Extrapolation" in data interpretation means:

A. Deleting data from the edges
B. Estimating values outside the original observation range based on known trends
C. Summarizing the data into a single point
D. Moving data to a different database

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Data Overfitting" in a predictive model?

A. Using too much data
B. Creating a model that is so complex it fits the noise of the training data rather than the underlying pattern
C. Using a computer that is too small for the data
D. Printing the data on paper that is too small

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_019]
A "Logarithmic Scale" is interpreted differently than a linear scale because:

A. It shows values as colors
B. Equal distances represent equal ratios or percentages rather than equal absolute amounts
C. It is only for negative numbers
D. It is twice as accurate

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [DATA_INTERPRETATION_ADVANCED_020]
What is "Actionable Insight"?

A. Any piece of data that is interesting
B. Information derived from data that provides a clear direction for a business decision or change
C. A chart that has interactive buttons
D. A report that has been approved by a manager

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 48: UML (Unified Modeling Language)

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [UML_BEGINNER_001]
What does the acronym UML stand for?

A. Unified Method Logic
B. Universal Modeling Layout
C. Unified Modeling Language
D. United Model List

Correct Answer: C

Q2 [UML_BEGINNER_002]
What is the primary purpose of UML in software and business analysis?

A. To write executable code for applications
B. To provide a standardized visual way to document and design system models
C. To manage project budgets and timelines
D. To test the speed of internet connections

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [UML_BEGINNER_003]
Which of the following is considered a "Behavioral" diagram in UML?

A. Class Diagram
B. Use Case Diagram
C. Object Diagram
D. Package Diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [UML_BEGINNER_004]
Which of the following is considered a "Structural" diagram in UML?

A. Sequence Diagram
B. Class Diagram
C. Activity Diagram
D. State Machine Diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [UML_BEGINNER_005]
In a Use Case diagram, what does a "stick figure" represent?

A. A specific software function
B. An Actor (user or external system)
C. A system boundary
D. A line of code

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [UML_BEGINNER_006]
In a Use Case diagram, how is a Use Case itself visually represented?

A. A rectangle
B. A circle
C. An oval or ellipse
D. A diamond

Correct Answer: C

Q7 [UML_BEGINNER_007]
What does a "Rectangle" typically represent in most UML structural diagrams?

A. An action
B. A Class or Object
C. A decision point
D. The start of a process

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [UML_BEGINNER_008]
Which UML diagram is best suited for showing the chronological order of interactions between objects?

A. Class Diagram
B. Sequence Diagram
C. Deployment Diagram
D. Component Diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [UML_BEGINNER_009]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q10 [UML_BEGINNER_010]

A. Class Diagram
B. Activity Diagram
C. Object Diagram
D. Use Case Diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [UML_BEGINNER_011]
What represents a "Decision" in an Activity Diagram?

A. A rectangle
B. A diamond
C. An oval
D. A bold line

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [UML_BEGINNER_012]
In a Class Diagram, what is usually listed in the top section of a class box?

A. Attributes
B. Methods
C. Class Name
D. Relationships

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [UML_BEGINNER_013]
Which diagram provides a static view of the system showing classes, attributes, and operations?

A. Sequence Diagram
B. Class Diagram
C. State Diagram
D. Timing Diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [UML_BEGINNER_014]
What does an "Actor" do in a Use Case?

A. It describes how the code works
B. It initiates or interacts with a specific function of the system
C. It stores data in the database
D. It represents a physical server

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [UML_BEGINNER_015]
What represents the "System Boundary" in a Use Case diagram?

A. A large rectangle surrounding the use cases
B. A dashed line between actors
C. A circle at the end of a line
D. A bold arrow

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [UML_BEGINNER_016]
Which UML version is the current industry standard (widely used)?

A. UML 1.0
B. UML 2.x
C. UML 5.0
D. UML Python Edition

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [UML_BEGINNER_017]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q18 [UML_BEGINNER_018]
What does a solid line between an Actor and a Use Case represent?

A. An error
B. A relationship or association
C. A data backup
D. A transition over time

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [UML_BEGINNER_019]

A. Deleting old documents

D. Setting up the office hardware

B. No

Correct Answer: A — True

Q1 [UML_INTERMEDIATE_001]
In a Class Diagram, what does the "Generalization" relationship represent?

D. A data flow

Q2 [UML_INTERMEDIATE_002]

B. A solid (filled) diamond

D. A dashed line

Q3 [UML_INTERMEDIATE_003]

A. There is no difference
B. Aggregation is a "has-a" relationship where parts can exist independently; Composition is stronger ownership

D. Aggregation uses a solid diamond; Composition uses a hollow one

Correct Answer: B

B. Static

D. Addition

Q1 [UML_ADVANCED_001]

Q3 [UML_ADVANCED_003]

D. There is no visual difference

Correct Answer: A

Q4 [UML_ADVANCED_004]

Q6 [UML_ADVANCED_006]

Q7 [UML_ADVANCED_007]

D. A transition between states

Q8 [UML_ADVANCED_008]
