A. Realizing a project is over budget
B. A relationship between an interface and the class that implements its behavior
C. A link between two actors
D. A type of composition

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [UML_ADVANCED_009]
In an Activity Diagram, what is the difference between a "Central Buffer" and a "Data Store"?

A. Data Store persists data; Central Buffer is transient/temporary
B. Central Buffer is for hardware
C. Data Store is only for SQL
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [UML_ADVANCED_010]
What does a "Combined Fragment" (like alt, opt, or loop) do in a Sequence Diagram?

A. It combines two diagrams into one
B. It represents logic like branching, optionality, or iteration within the interaction
C. It deletes the objects
D. It renames the lifelines

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [UML_ADVANCED_011]
What is "Model Driven Architecture" (MDA)?

A. Building models of physical buildings
B. An approach to software development where models are used as the primary artifacts to drive code generation
C. Managing a project using only Excel
D. A type of hardware architecture

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [UML_ADVANCED_012]
In a Class Diagram, what is a "Template Class" (Parameterized Class)?

A. A class with a lot of comments
B. A class that defines a family of classes by using parameters (like generics in Java)
C. A class that is used as a standard for all other projects
D. A class that has been deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [UML_ADVANCED_013]
What is a "History State" in a State Machine Diagram?

A. A record of past versions of the diagram
B. A mechanism to remember the last active sub-state of a composite state when it was exited
C. A list of all state changes
D. The first state ever created

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [UML_ADVANCED_014]
In a Deployment Diagram, what is a "Node"?

A. A single point in a flowchart
B. A physical execution environment (hardware or software environment like a server or OS)
C. A line connecting two objects
D. A specific attribute of a class

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [UML_ADVANCED_015]
What is the "Meta-Object Facility" (MOF)?

A. A factory for building physical objects
B. An OMG standard for model-driven engineering that provides a framework for defining UML itself (a meta-model)
C. A type of database index
D. A tool for drawing UML on mobile devices

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [UML_ADVANCED_016]
What does a "Dashed Arrow with a Hollow Head" represent in a Class Diagram?

A. Dependency
B. Generalization
C. Interface Realization
D. Aggregation

Correct Answer: C

Q17 [UML_ADVANCED_017]
What is an "Information Flow" in UML?

A. The speed of the internet
B. A high-level abstraction representing the exchange of information between entities without specifying the mechanism
C. A type of Sequence Diagram
D. A list of all project emails

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [UML_ADVANCED_018]
In a State Diagram, what is the difference between an "Entry" and an "Exit" action?

A. Entry happens when entering a state; Exit happens when leaving a state
B. Exit happens when the system crashes
C. Entry is for users; Exit is for admins
D. They are the same thing

Correct Answer: A

Q19 [UML_ADVANCED_019]
What is a "Signal" in UML?

A. A phone notification
B. An asynchronous stimulus sent from one object to another
C. A type of gateway in an activity diagram
D. A bold line in a use case diagram

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [UML_ADVANCED_020]
Which UML diagram would be most useful for modeling the runtime configuration of software components on server clusters?

A. Class Diagram
B. Use Case Diagram
C. Deployment Diagram
D. Object Diagram

Correct Answer: C

## Skill 49: BPMN (Business Process Model and Notation)

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPMN_BEGINNER_001]
What is the primary purpose of BPMN?

A. To write technical code for databases
B. To provide a standard notation that is readily understandable by all business stakeholders
C. To design the physical layout of an office
D. To manage project financial accounts

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BPMN_BEGINNER_002]
Which symbol represents a "Task" in BPMN?

A. A circle
B. A rectangle with rounded corners
C. A diamond
D. A triangle

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [BPMN_BEGINNER_003]
How is a "Start Event" visually represented in a BPMN diagram?

A. A bold-lined circle
B. A thin-lined circle
C. A solid square
D. A dashed diamond

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BPMN_BEGINNER_004]
Which element is used to represent a decision point where the flow can diverge?

A. Activity
B. Event
C. Gateway
D. Artifact

Correct Answer: C

Q5 [BPMN_BEGINNER_005]
What does a "Pool" represent in a BPMN collaboration diagram?

A. A specific software bug
B. A participant or a logical container for a process (e.g., an organization)
C. The time taken to finish a task
D. A database table

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BPMN_BEGINNER_006]
What is a "Lane" (Swimlane) used for within a Pool?

A. To show the sequence of events
B. To sub-partition activities within a pool based on roles or departments
C. To represent external organizations
D. To define the start and end of a process

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BPMN_BEGINNER_007]
How is an "End Event" visually represented?

A. A thin-lined circle
B. A bold-lined circle
C. A circle with a double border
D. A solid black circle

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BPMN_BEGINNER_008]
What does a solid arrow represent in BPMN?

A. Message Flow
B. Association
C. Sequence Flow
D. Data Input

Correct Answer: C

Q9 [BPMN_BEGINNER_009]
True or False: Every BPMN process should ideally have at least one Start Event and one End Event.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q10 [BPMN_BEGINNER_010]
What is the purpose of a "Text Annotation" artifact?

A. To execute code
B. To provide additional information for the reader of the diagram
C. To set the priority of a task
D. To show the data storage location

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BPMN_BEGINNER_011]
Which of the following represents a "Decision" in its simplest form?

A. Parallel Gateway
B. Exclusive Gateway
C. Inclusive Gateway
D. Event-based Gateway

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BPMN_BEGINNER_012]
What does "BPMN" stand for?

A. Business Process Management Network
B. Business Process Model and Notation
C. Basic Process Modeling Node
D. Business Product Method Name

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BPMN_BEGINNER_013]
Which symbol is used for a "Gateway"?

A. Rectangle
B. Diamond
C. Circle
D. Cylinder

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BPMN_BEGINNER_014]
In BPMN, what is an "Activity"?

A. The start of a process
B. Work that is performed within a business process
C. A decision point
D. A data database

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BPMN_BEGINNER_015]
What is a "Token" in the context of BPMN execution logic?

A. A piece of physical hardware
B. A theoretical concept used to trace the path of a process through the model
C. A security password
D. An error message

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BPMN_BEGINNER_016]
How do you represent a "Group" artifact?

A. A solid black rectangle
B. A dashed-lined box with rounded corners
C. A cylinder
D. A stick figure

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BPMN_BEGINNER_017]
Which BPMN element shows the order in which activities are performed?

A. Message Flow
B. Sequence Flow
C. Association
D. Data Input

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BPMN_BEGINNER_018]
True or False: Message Flows (dashed arrows) can be used to connect elements within the same Pool.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q19 [BPMN_BEGINNER_019]
What is a "Sub-process"?

A. A task that is not important
B. An activity that can be decomposed into finer levels of process detail
C. A process that runs on a different computer
D. A list of software requirements

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BPMN_BEGINNER_020]
What does a "Cylinder" icon usually represent in BPMN?

A. A physical task
B. A Data Store (Database)
C. A decision point
D. The end of a process

Correct Answer: B

------------------------------------------------------------------------

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is the difference between an "Exclusive Gateway" and a "Parallel Gateway"?

A. Exclusive allows only one path; Parallel allows all outgoing paths to be taken simultaneously
B. Parallel is for managers; Exclusive is for staff
C. Exclusive uses a '+' sign; Parallel uses an 'X'
D. There is no difference

Correct Answer: A

Q2 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_002]
What does an "Inclusive Gateway" (O) do?

A. It requires all paths to be taken
B. It allows one or more paths to be taken based on conditions
C. It stops the process
D. It merges all incoming flows into one task

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_003]
How is an "Intermediate Event" represented?

A. A thin circle
B. A bold circle
C. A circle with a double thin-line border
D. A circle with a lightning bolt

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_004]
What is a "Message Event" (envelope icon)?

A. A system crash notification
B. An event that involves the receipt or sending of a message to an external participant
C. A text annotation
D. A task that involves writing an email

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is a "Timer Event" (clock icon) used for?

A. To measure the speed of the user
B. To indicate a specific time-date or a cycle-time delay within a process
C. To show the deadline of the project
D. To log the time spent on a task

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_006]
What is the purpose of an "Event-Based Gateway"?

A. To make a decision based on data
B. To make a decision based on which event (e.g., a message or timer) occurs first
C. To start a parallel process
D. To end a sub-process

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_007]
What does a "Dashed Arrow" (Message Flow) represent?

A. Flow of work between departments in the same pool
B. Flow of information or communication between two different Pools/participants
C. An optional step in the sequence
/D. A data backup procedure

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_008]
What is a "User Task" in BPMN?

A. A task done by a robot
B. A task performed by a human with the aid of a software application
C. A task where the user types code
D. A task that requires no software

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is a "Service Task"?

A. A task done by a waiter
B. A task that uses a Web service or automated application to perform work
C. A manual task
D. A customer service phone call

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_010]
How do you represent "Data Objects"?

A. A rectangle
B. A piece of paper with a folded corner icon
C. A circle
D. A diamond

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_011]
What is a "Default Flow" in a gateway?

A. The flow taken if no other conditions are met
B. The first flow in the diagram
C. A flow that cannot be changed
D. A flow for administrators only

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What does the "O" symbol inside a gateway diamond represent?

A. Exclusive
B. Parallel
C. Inclusive
D. Complex

Correct Answer: C

Q13 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_013]
Which task type is used for a task performed purely by a human without software (e.g., packing a box)?

A. User Task
B. Service Task
C. Manual Task
D. Script Task

Correct Answer: C

Q14 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is a "Catching Event"?

A. An event that triggers a message to be sent
B. An event that waits for a trigger (e.g., an incoming message) to happen
C. An error that is ignored
D. The last event in a process

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is a "Throwing Event"?

A. An event that generates a trigger (e.g., sends a message or signal)
B. An event that stops the process
C. A decision point
D. A data object

Correct Answer: A

Q16 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_016]
In BPMN, what is an "Ad-hoc Sub-process" (indicated by a tilde ~)?

A. A sub-process with no tasks
B. A group of activities that have no required sequence
C. A sub-process for errors only
D. A sub-process that runs only once a year

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_017]
What is an "Association" (dotted line) used for?

A. To show the order of tasks
B. To link information and artifacts (like Text Annotations) to BPMN elements
C. To connect two pools
D. To represent a database join

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What does a "Script Task" do?

A. It records a video
B. It executes a script (code) defined within the business process engine
C. It prints a user manual
D. It sends an email manually

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What is "Looping" in BPMN?

A. A circle in the diagram
B. An activity that repeats until a condition is met
C. A mistake in the logic
D. A sequence flow that goes backward

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BPMN_INTERMEDIATE_020]
What is a "Send Task"?

A. A task that sends a message to an external participant
B. A task that sends an invoice to a printer
C. A task that moves a file to a new folder
D. A task for the shipping department

Correct Answer: A

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [BPMN_ADVANCED_001]
What is a "Boundary Event"?

A. An event at the edge of the paper
B. An event attached to the boundary of an activity (like a task or sub-process) to handle exceptions
C. The start of a new lane
D. A message between pools

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [BPMN_ADVANCED_002]
What is the difference between an "Interrupting" and "Non-Interrupting" boundary event?

A. Interrupting stops the main activity; Non-interrupting allows it to continue while starting a secondary flow
B. Non-interrupting is faster
C. Interrupting is for messages only
D. There is no difference in behavior

Correct Answer: A

Q3 [BPMN_ADVANCED_003]
What is a "Signal Event" (triangle icon)?

A. A phone call
B. A broadcast communication that can be "caught" by multiple processes simultaneously
C. An error message
D. A decision point

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [BPMN_ADVANCED_004]
What is an "Error Event" (lightning bolt icon) typically used for?

A. To show where the code is broken
B. To handle a business or technical error that stops the normal flow
C. To restart the process
D. To delete data

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [BPMN_ADVANCED_005]
What is a "Compensation Event"?

A. A reward for employees
B. An event used to "undo" or reverse the effects of previously completed tasks (e.g., a refund)
C. A salary increase
D. A type of gateway

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [BPMN_ADVANCED_006]
What is a "Call Activity"?

A. A phone call to a client
B. A reference to a globally defined process or task that is reused in the current model
C. A meeting reminder
D. A button to start the process

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [BPMN_ADVANCED_007]
What does a "Multi-Instance" activity (three vertical/horizontal lines) indicate?

A. The activity is performed by three people
B. The activity is performed multiple times (sequentially or in parallel)
C. The activity has three sub-processes
D. The activity is repeated until the end of the day

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [BPMN_ADVANCED_008]
What is a "Complex Gateway"?

A. A gateway with many arrows
B. A gateway used for complex synchronization or decisions that cannot be handled by other gateways
C. A gateway that is hard to draw
D. A gateway that requires a password

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [BPMN_ADVANCED_009]
What is an "Escalation Event"?

A. Moving a task to a higher floor
B. An event used to signal that a situation requires attention from a higher authority or different path
C. A task that becomes more expensive
D. A way to increase the process speed

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [BPMN_ADVANCED_010]
What is a "Conditional Event" (page with lines icon)?

A. An event that only happens in winter
B. An event that is triggered when a specific business condition becomes true
C. A decision point
D. A task that is optional

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [BPMN_ADVANCED_011]
What is a "Terminate End Event" (solid black circle inside)?

A. The normal end of a process
B. An end event that immediately stops all active paths in the entire process level
C. An error that stops the computer
D. A task that is deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [BPMN_ADVANCED_012]
What is a "Transaction Sub-process"?

A. A sub-process that costs money
B. A specialized sub-process where all activities must succeed, or the whole thing is rolled back (ACID properties)
C. A sub-process for the finance department
D. A list of all project transactions

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [BPMN_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Choreography" in BPMN?

A. A dance routine for the project team
B. A diagram type focusing on the exchange of messages between participants rather than internal tasks
C. A way to animate the diagram
D. A set of rules for lanes

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [BPMN_ADVANCED_014]
In a "Call Activity," the process being called is referred to as:

A. The Parent Process
B. The Global Process (or Callable Element)
C. The Sub-task
D. The External Pool

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [BPMN_ADVANCED_015]
What is a "Message Intermediate Throw Event"?

A. An event that receives an email
B. An event that sends a message to an external participant while the process is running
C. A deleted message
D. A notification for the manager

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [BPMN_ADVANCED_016]
What does a "Compensation Boundary Event" trigger?

A. A payment to the analyst
B. A "Compensation Activity" to reverse the work done by the task it is attached to
C. A restart of the process
D. A decision diamond

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [BPMN_ADVANCED_017]
What is an "Expanded Sub-process"?

A. A sub-process that is very long
B. A sub-process where the internal details/flow are visible within the parent process diagram
C. A sub-process with more than 10 tasks
D. A sub-process that has been deleted

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [BPMN_ADVANCED_018]
What is the "Cancel End Event" used for?

A. To cancel a project
B. To trigger the cancellation of a Transaction sub-process
C. To delete a diagram
D. To stop an email from being sent

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [BPMN_ADVANCED_019]
What is a "Link Event"?

A. A link to a website
B. A pair of events used as an "off-page connector" to simplify long sequence flows
C. A relationship between two actors
D. A connection to a database

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [BPMN_ADVANCED_020]
What is "Level 3" modeling in BPMN?

A. The highest level summary
B. A highly detailed, executable level model used for technical implementation and automation
C. A model with only three shapes
D. A process that takes three days to complete

Correct Answer: B

## Skill 50: Wireframing

### Beginner — 20 Questions

Q1 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_001]
What is a wireframe in the context of UI/UX design?

A. A high-resolution final image of a website
B. A two-dimensional skeletal outline of a webpage or app interface
C. The actual source code used to build a site
D. A video showing how to use a software product

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_002]
What is the primary purpose of creating wireframes?

A. To choose the final color palette for the brand
B. To define the information architecture and layout of a screen
C. To test the speed of the server
D. To write the database queries

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_003]
Which level of fidelity is characterized by simple boxes, lines, and "Lorem Ipsum" text?

A. High-fidelity
B. Mid-fidelity
C. Low-fidelity
D. Production-ready

Correct Answer: C

Q4 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_004]
What does "Lorem Ipsum" represent in a wireframe?

A. A secret code for developers
B. Placeholder text used to show where content will eventually be placed
C. The name of the lead designer
D. A specific legal disclaimer

Correct Answer: B

Q5 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_005]
True or False: Wireframes should usually include final images, colors, and logos.

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: B — False

Q6 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_006]

A. Microsoft Excel
B. Figma
C. VLC Media Player
D. Norton Antivirus

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_007]
What is a "Placeholder" in a wireframe?

B. A simple box (often with an 'X') representing where an image or icon will go
C. The person holding the phone
D. A link to a competitor's website

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_008]

B. During the early design and requirement analysis phase
C. Only after the product is launched

Q9 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_009]

A. They look exactly like the final product

C. They include all final text
D. They automatically generate HTML code

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_010]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q11 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_011]

A. Footer
B. Navigation Bar (Header)
C. Sidebar
D. Advertisement

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_012]

A. A large block of text
B. A prominent button (e.g., "Sign Up" or "Buy Now")
C. A background image
D. A hidden menu

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_013]
What is the difference between a wireframe and a prototype?

A. There is no difference
B. A wireframe is a static sketch; a prototype is often interactive and simulates functionality
C. A prototype is always hand-drawn; a wireframe is always digital
D. Wireframes are for mobile; Prototypes are for desktop

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_014]

A. A deleted feature
B. An image or graphic placeholder
C. A close button
D. An error message

Q15 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_015]

A. Only the CEO
B. Stakeholders, developers, and designers
C. Only the end-customers
D. The marketing agency's printer

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_016]

A. True
B. False

Correct Answer: A — True

Q17 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_017]
What is "Information Architecture"?

A. The speed of the computer's CPU
B. The structural design of shared information environments (how content is organized)
C. The physical layout of the server room

A. Visual aesthetics and beauty

Q19 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_019]

A. A piece of food

Q20 [WIREFRAMING_BEGINNER_020]

A. The speed of the screen refresh

### Intermediate — 20 Questions

Q1 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_001]
What is "Annotations" in the context of wireframing?

A. Deleting parts of the design
B. Written notes on the side of a wireframe explaining the logic or behavior of specific elements
C. Changing the color of the lines
D. The names of the designers

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_002]

A. To make the wireframe look like a math problem
B. To ensure alignment, consistency, and proper spacing across different screens
C. To hide data from stakeholders
D. To increase the file size

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_003]
What is "Responsive Wireframing"?

A. Making wireframes that answer questions
B. Designing wireframe layouts for different screen sizes (mobile, tablet, desktop)
C. Creating wireframes that load very fast
D. Wireframing only for touchscreens

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_004]

A. Actual content and more precise spacing/typography
B. Detailed interaction logic and clickable links
C. Basic color schemes and branding elements
D. All of the above

Q5 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_005]
What is the "Fold" in web design?

A. The crease in a physical piece of paper
B. The part of the webpage visible without scrolling
C. A menu that opens and closes
D. An error in the layout

Q6 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_006]

D. To calculate the project's ROI

Q7 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_007]

Q8 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_008]

B. A shopping cart icon in the header
C. A "Burger" menu for mobile navigation
D. All of the above

Correct Answer: D

Q9 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_009]
What is "Visual Hierarchy" in a wireframe?

A. The list of managers in the design department
B. Arranging elements to show their order of importance (e.g., using larger boxes for main CTAs)
C. Sorting screens alphabetically
D. Putting all elements in a straight line

Correct Answer: B

Q10 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_010]
What is a "Modal" or "Pop-up" in a wireframe?

A. A new webpage
B. An element that appears on top of the current screen, requiring user interaction before returning to the main flow
C. A background color
D. The last page of an app

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_011]
Why should wireframes be "Content-agnostic" at the very beginning?

A. To avoid distracting stakeholders with specific wording or images instead of focusing on layout and flow
B. Because content doesn't matter in software
C. To make the wireframe look more technical
D. Because images are too expensive to use

Correct Answer: A

Q12 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_012]
What is "User Flow" documentation?

A. The number of users on a site per hour
B. A diagram showing the path a user takes through the wireframed screens to achieve a goal
C. The speed of the user's mouse movement
D. A list of all user names

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_013]
How do you represent a "Dropdown Menu" in a static wireframe?

A. You can't; it must be an interactive prototype
B. By drawing an arrow icon next to a field and sometimes showing the open state in a separate sketch
C. By writing "Dropdown" in large letters
D. By coloring the box yellow

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_014]
What is the purpose of "Usability Testing" with wireframes?

A. To see if users find the layout intuitive before spending time on high-fidelity design or code
B. To check the server performance
C. To find spelling errors in the Lorem Ipsum
D. To test the security of the login

Correct Answer: A

Q15 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_015]
What is "Navigation Depth"?

A. The number of pixels from the top of the page
B. The number of clicks it takes for a user to reach a specific piece of information
C. The brightness of the navigation bar
D. The length of the menu list

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_016]
Which of the following describes "Consistency" in wireframing?

A. Using different buttons on every page
B. Ensuring that similar elements (like "Submit" buttons) look and behave the same way throughout the application
C. Changing the font on every screen
D. Using a random mix of colors

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_017]
A "State" in a wireframe (e.g., Error State, Loading State) shows:

A. The location of the user
B. How a screen looks during a specific condition or event
C. The physical state of the hardware
D. The emotional state of the designer

Correct Answer: B

Q18 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_018]
What is a "Wireframe Deck"?

A. A physical deck of cards
B. A collection of wireframes organized into a presentation to show a complete user journey
C. A set of programming scripts
D. A folder of deleted images

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_019]
What is "White Space" used for in a layout?

A. To save ink when printing
B. To prevent the interface from looking cluttered and to improve readability
C. To indicate where data is missing
D. To show the end of the page

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [WIREFRAMING_INTERMEDIATE_020]
How does wireframing support "Developer Handoff"?

A. By providing a clear blueprint of the layout and functional logic before coding starts
B. By writing the CSS for the developer
C. By giving the developer a break from work
D. By hiding the business requirements

Correct Answer: A

### Advanced — 20 Questions

Q1 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_001]
What is a "Design System" and how does it affect wireframing?

A. A set of rules for the office; it doesn't affect wireframes
B. A collection of reusable components and standards that ensures wireframes align with a company's universal UI language
C. A computer's operating system
D. A method for organizing files in folders

Correct Answer: B

Q2 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_002]
What is the "Z-Pattern" vs "F-Pattern" in layout analysis?

A. Types of error codes
B. Patterns describing how users typically scan a screen visually
C. Methods for sorting database tables
D. Keyboard shortcuts for zooming

Correct Answer: B

Q3 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_003]
How do "Technical Constraints" impact advanced wireframing?

A. They don't; designers can build anything
B. They require the BA/Designer to understand what is feasible (e.g., API limitations) before proposing a layout
C. They only affect the project budget
D. They are only relevant during the testing phase

Correct Answer: B

Q4 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_004]
What is "Click-through Rate" (CTR) optimization in a wireframe?

A. Making buttons larger and placing them in high-visibility areas based on user behavior data
B. Deleting all links
C. Counting how many times a user clicks a mouse
D. Changing the font of all links to blue

Correct Answer: A

Q5 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_005]
What is "Micro-interaction" modeling?

A. Modeling interactions between microorganisms
B. Defining small, functional animations or feedback loops (e.g., a button changing color when hovered) in annotations
C. Writing code for a small app
D. Analyzing the user's heartbeat

Correct Answer: B

Q6 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_006]
In complex systems, what is "Dashboard Density" management?

A. Putting as many charts as possible on one screen
B. Balancing the amount of information shown on a screen to avoid cognitive overload while maintaining utility
C. The number of pixels in a bar chart
D. The total number of dashboards in a project

Correct Answer: B

Q7 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_007]
What is "Edge Case" wireframing?

A. Drawing lines on the edge of the paper
B. Creating wireframes for unusual or extreme scenarios (e.g., a user with 1,000 items in a cart)
C. Wireframing for a new browser called "Edge"
D. Deleting the borders of the UI

Correct Answer: B

Q8 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_008]
What is "Accessibility-first" wireframing?

A. Making wireframes that are easy to find
B. Considering standards like WCAG (e.g., color contrast, screen reader labels) during the skeletal design stage
C. Giving everyone access to the Figma file
D. Using only the color black

Correct Answer: B

Q9 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_009]
What is "Cognitive Load" and how do wireframes help minimize it?

A. The amount of mental effort needed to use a UI; wireframes help by simplifying and organizing information logically
B. The weight of the user's brain
C. The speed of the server
D. The number of users on a site at once

Correct Answer: A

Q10 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_010]
How does "Iterative Wireframing" work?

A. Creating one perfect wireframe and never changing it
B. A cycle of creating, testing, getting feedback, and refining the wireframes multiple times
C. Creating wireframes for every second of a video
D. Deleting wireframes in a loop

Correct Answer: B

Q11 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_011]
What is "A/B Testing" of wireframes?

A. Sorting wireframes alphabetically
B. Creating two different versions of a wireframe to see which one performs better with users
C. Testing wireframes on two different monitors
D. Having two different designers draw the same screen

Correct Answer: B

Q12 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_012]
What is "Information Density"?

A. The file size of the text
B. The amount of content and control provided per unit of screen space
C. The weight of a printed wireframe
D. The number of people reading the screen

Correct Answer: B

Q13 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_013]
What is "Affordance" in UI components?

A. The price of the component
B. A visual clue that suggests how an object should be used (e.g., a button that looks raised "affords" clicking)
C. The speed of the transition
D. A type of software license

Correct Answer: B

Q14 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_014]
What is "Skeleton Loading" in a wireframe?

A. A wireframe of a ghost
B. Showing a blank version of the UI layout that is filled in as data loads to improve perceived performance
C. A layout with no text
D. An error in the rendering engine

Correct Answer: B

Q15 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_015]
How should "Error Validation" be wireframed for a complex form?

A. By ignoring errors until the testing phase
B. By creating specific "Error State" versions of the form showing where and how error messages appear
C. By writing a paragraph in the user manual
D. By coloring the whole screen red

Correct Answer: B

Q16 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_016]
What is "Mobile-first" design strategy?

A. Only building apps for phones
B. Designing the wireframe for the smallest screen first and then scaling up to larger ones
C. Buying a new phone for the team
D. Putting the phone number on every screen

Correct Answer: B

Q17 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_017]
What is the "Three-Click Rule" in wireframing?

A. A user should be able to find any information with no more than three mouse clicks
B. A button must be clicked three times to work
C. The analyst has only three chances to draw a screen
D. The project must have exactly three screens

Correct Answer: A

Q18 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_018]
What is "Dark Mode" wireframing?

A. Wireframing at night
B. Designing an alternative low-light version of the UI
C. Turning off the computer monitor
D. Hiding the design from the client

Correct Answer: B

Q19 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_019]
What is "Heuristic Evaluation" of a wireframe?

A. A guess about the design
B. Reviewing a wireframe against established usability principles (e.g., Nielsen’s Heuristics)
C. A type of virus scan
D. Checking for spelling errors in Greek

Correct Answer: B

Q20 [WIREFRAMING_ADVANCED_020]
What is the role of "Redlines" in an advanced handoff?

A. Red lines drawn for decoration
B. Detailed measurements (pixels, padding, hex codes) provided to developers alongside the wireframe
C. Mistakes found in the design
D. The boundaries of the company property

Correct Answer: B
